# Changelog

All notable changes to the **MP Mix Archive Manager** project will be documented in this file.

The format is based on [Keep a Changelog](https://keepachangelog.com/en/1.0.0/),
and this project adheres to [Semantic Versioning](https://semver.org/spec/v2.0.0.html).

---

## [Unreleased]

### Added
- **`W` opens an MP Black Glass Window — up to eight, at exactly the size of the window that opened one** (`scripts/black_glass_window.py`, `MP_Audio_Player.py`, `MP_Audio_Player_Liquid_Glass.py`, `MP_Audio_Player_Liquid_Glass_OpenGL.py`, `Mix_Archive_Manager.sh`, `MP_Liquid_Mixer/MP_Liquid_Mixer.py`, `scripts/mp_liquid_visualizer_vulkan.py`, `scripts/mp_liquid_visualizer_metal.py`, `scripts/liquid_player_visualizer.py`, `scripts/visualizer_player_bond.py`, `tests/test_black_glass_window.py`):
  - **One key, four windows, one implementation.** `W` in **MP Liquid Player**, **MP Gui**, **MP Liquid Mixer** and **MP Liquid Visualizer** calls `scripts/black_glass_window.py`. Each press opens a pane in its own process at **exactly the rectangle of the window that opened it**. The glass hosts put their own pid in `MP_HOST_PID` for the child they draw, so the pane takes *that* window's published `liquid_player_window.json` / `liquid_gui_window.json` record and not another open player's. Mini, Micro and Vertical all work because the hosts republish geometry when `M` resizes them; the mixer passes its live GTK allocation; the visualizer passes its own GLFW size and position.
  - **It is a real window.** Borderless, rounded, drawn with Cairo: drag the body to move it and the bottom-right grip to resize it. **`Esc`** and **`Q`** close it.
  - **Eight at most.** Claim files under `~/.config/mix-manager/black_glass/slots` are the count, taken by the pane itself before it draws anything, so a launch that skipped the caller's early check still cannot become a ninth. A dead claim is pruned, and the ninth press reports the limit instead of opening a window.
  - **`R` is Rainbow Mode.** The whole pane switches from Black Glass to a hue sweep that keeps climbing the scale — red, orange, yellow, green, cyan, blue, violet, back to red — at 24°/s, one full turn every 15 s. Pressing `R` again returns it to Black Glass. The sheen, rim and slider all follow the pane's colour.
  - **The transparency slider only exists while the pointer is over the pane.** It runs from **10 % to 90 %** (90 % transparency is 0.10 window opacity), is tinted the same colour as the pane, and **fades in and out** over 220 ms instead of appearing and vanishing. With the pointer elsewhere it is not drawn at all.
  - **Inside a pane:** `Z` attaches an MP Liquid Visualizer behind that pane and takes it away again, `G` opens MP Gui, `L` opens MP Liquid Player, `M` opens the console MP Mix Manager in a dedicated Konsole window, and `W` opens another pane at the same size.
  - **The attached visualizer is a locked companion.** It is started with `MP_VISUALIZER_COMPANION=1` plus `MP_VISUALIZER_MATCH_BLACK_GLASS=1` and its own state file, so it follows the pane's rectangle, never picks up the player's `visible` flag, and closes itself when the pane disappears. `PlayerBondMixin` gained the third host kind next to the player and MP Gui (`_bond_targets_black_glass`, `BLACK_GLASS_TITLE`).
  - **The playlist export keeps its key.** `W` in the terminal player still exports the playlist; inside the glass window lowercase `w` opens a pane and **`Shift+W`** keeps the export. MP Gui only interprets `W` inside an `MP_GUI_SESSION`, so on a plain console it stays a type-to-select letter.
  - **The uninstaller owns the records.** `black_glass`, `black_glass_window.*.json`, `black_glass_visualizer.*.json` and `black_glass_visualizer_state.*.json` are shared state, removed with the last app that can open a pane.
- **The MP Tray can rescan the archive, rescan the music folders, and mute the player** (`scripts/mp_system_tray.py`, `scripts/macos_menu_bar.swift`, `scripts/windows_tray.ps1`, `Mix_Archive_Manager.sh`, `scripts/show_help.sh`, `README.md`, `tests/test_mp_system_tray.py`):
  - **Rescan Mix Archive Folders** is the console's **Storage Management > 4** on the tray. It calls a new headless `Mix_Archive_Manager.sh --rescan-archive`, which reuses that menu's `clear_archive_dir_cache`, `invalidate_archive_stats_cache`, `get_all_mix_archive_dirs --refresh` and `show_stats --force` rather than reimplementing the scan, then prints one `RESCAN_*` key per line so the tray can report without parsing the themed console text. The notice names the scanned folder count plus the FLAC, converted-WAV, pending-WAV and missing-tracklist totals.
  - **Rescan Music Folders** is **Option 2 > Option 3 > A** on the tray. It runs `music_folders_manager.py --generate ALL`, the same `generate_advance_playlists(ALL)` call the menu's playlist-generation entry uses, so the tray and the console rebuild one playlist set. The notice names the refreshed folders, the total tracks in ALL, and any folder it could not reach.
  - **The notice comes from the window manager.** The scan finishes with `notify-send` on Linux/BSD and Notification Center on macOS; the PowerShell tray uses a notification-area balloon. The macOS menu-bar agent asks the tray module for the entry's live title through a new `--action-label`, so both front ends read the same state.
  - **Both scans run off the icon's thread** — a Qt worker with a main-thread watcher on Linux, a child process polled by a UI timer on Windows — because a large archive or a slow SMB share takes minutes and an inline scan froze the menu the way the old inline Close All did. The entry says **Rescanning…** and cannot be clicked twice while its scan is in flight.
  - **Mute MP Player Audio** toggles the player through its own IPC socket (`mute`), the same command the player's `u` key, a headset button and the phone dashboard use. It reads **Unmute MP Player Audio** while the player is muted and flips back after a click; the title is re-read every time the menu opens so a mute from another window is reflected. With no player running both front ends say so instead of failing silently.
  - **The archive stats parser was fixed on the way in.** The first version stripped ANSI with a bracket expression that also ate the count lines, so every count reported as `1`. The scan now strips colour with an `LC_ALL=C` extended expression before matching, verified against the live archive as **484 FLACs**, **52 converted WAVs**, **7 pending WAVs** and **5 missing tracklists**.
- **`X` opens MP Liquid Mixer from MP Gui, MP Liquid Player and the console manager — and never opens a second one** (`scripts/liquid_mixer_launch.py`, `MP_Audio_Player.py`, `Mix_Archive_Manager.sh`, `MP_Liquid_Mixer/MP_Liquid_Mixer.py`, `MP_Liquid_Mixer/mixer_core.py`, `tests/test_liquid_mixer_launch.py`, `tests/test_player_queue.py`):
  - **One key, one implementation.** All three surfaces call `scripts/liquid_mixer_launch.py`, which spawns `scripts/launch_liquid_mixer.sh` (the same launcher the desktop shortcut uses) and strips a parent window's session flags first: `MP_GUI_SESSION`, `MP_LIQUID_GLASS_MANAGER`, `MP_LIQUID_GLASS_MIXER`, `MP_PLAYER_NAMESPACE` and `GDK_BACKEND` belong to the window `X` was pressed in, not to a fresh mixer host.
  - **The lock is the single source of truth.** The mixer already holds `~/.config/mix-manager/liquid_mixer.lock` under `flock` for its whole life, because both of its decks live in fixed IPC namespaces and a second window would retire the first pair of engines. The helper takes the same non-blocking lock to ask "is a mixer alive?", so pressing `X` again can only **raise** the window that owns it — the probe opens read-only and never disturbs the owner's pid record.
  - **The press race is closed too.** Between `Popen` and the mixer taking its own lock there is no lock yet, so a claim file records the spawned pid. A second press inside that window sees `starting`, a claim whose process is gone (or older than 60 s) is ignored, and the mixer retires its own claim once the window is mapped. Closing the window drops the flock, so the next `X` opens a mixer again.
  - **The player's `X` changed meaning, deliberately.** `X` was the MP Audio Player's "stop and exit" key. It is now the mixer launcher on every surface, and **`Esc`, `q` and `Ctrl+C` remain the exit keys** (pinned in `tests/test_player_queue.py`). A text console — TTY Audio Mode, or no `$DISPLAY` — has nowhere to draw the mixer, so there `X` keeps its old stop-and-exit behaviour rather than becoming a dead key. A mixer deck pressing `X` reports that the mixer is already open instead of asking to raise its own window.
  - **Search keeps the letter.** In MP Liquid Player `/` still types an `x`. In the console menu `x` is handled in the `CHAR:*` branch beside `b`, `p` and `f`, only while the typed buffer is empty, so typing `exit` still reaches the text selection instead of launching a window.
  - **Visible confirmation.** The player shows `✦ Opened MP Liquid Mixer` / `MP Liquid Mixer is already open` in its notification line, the menu prints the same in its one-shot path (and stays silent in the key path, which repaints itself), and every window's key hints now list `[X] Liquid Mixer` / `[X] Mixer`.
  - **Verified against the real host.** A live mixer was started through the helper: the second `launch` returned `already-open` and left exactly one `MP_Liquid_Mixer.py` process, the claim disappeared with the window mapped, and `status` read `free` again after the window closed.

- **MP Liquid Mixer: 4 GiB WAV/FLAC recording, themed chrome, live clock and launcher keys** (`MP_Liquid_Mixer/mixer_recording.py`, `MP_Liquid_Mixer/MP_Liquid_Mixer.py`, `MP_Liquid_Mixer/mixer_core.py`, `MP_Liquid_Mixer/README.md`, `tests/test_liquid_mixer_recording.py`, `tests/test_liquid_mixer.py`):
  - **The 4 GiB ceiling is real, not the old 2 GiB wall.** The mixer now captures raw PCM from `parec --raw` and writes the canonical 44-byte RIFF header itself. `ChunkSize` and `data` are packed as **unsigned 32-bit**, and each part is capped at **4,294,967,248 bytes of audio** (4,294,967,295-byte file minus the 44-byte header, rounded down to a whole 16-bit stereo frame). The limit is fixed and not user-configurable.
  - **Recording continues in a new part.** At the ceiling the current file is finalised and a new one starts with `_PART_2`, `_PART_3`, ... appended before the extension. The original file keeps its name and is part 1.
  - **FLAC is supported at the same ceiling.** The new **WAV/FLAC** button toggles the container; a live recording restarts in the selected format. FLAC is encoded with `flac` from the same raw PCM (falling back to the suite's required `ffmpeg`) and rolls 16 MiB below 4 GiB so encoder buffering cannot cross the line. The button label and the record footer show the active format.
  - **The header is refreshed while recording** (once a second), so a crash or pulled cable leaves a playable file up to the last refresh instead of a zero-length header.
  - **Themed chrome.** A **Trance Connection Enabled** status lamp sits in the top bar; a **24-Bit** badge sits next to the **WAV/FLAC** button; and **DREAMWORLDS PRODUCTIONS / MPLANETARIAN** is a themed credit button in the mixer panel. All use the active theme's accent/secondary colours, so switching theme repaints them with the rest of the window.
  - **A live system clock** shows the date and `HH:MM:SS` in the mixer panel. It is read from the system when the mixer is constructed and refreshed from `datetime.now()` once a second by the existing UI tick; it does not depend on playback.
  - **Launch keys.** With the mixer chrome focused, `M` opens MP Mix Manager in a new Konsole (`Mix_Archive_Manager.sh`), `Z` opens the MP Liquid Visualizer, `W` opens a dedicated MP Black Glass pane at the mixer's size (its own `W`, `R`, `Z`, `G`, `L` and `M` keys are documented in `scripts/black_glass_window.py`), `L` opens MP Liquid Player, and `G` opens MP Gui. Each runs detached through the same launchers the shortcuts and tray use; a focused deck keeps its own player keys.
  - **EXPORT MIXES to the primary MIX_ARCHIVE.** The new button moves every finished `.wav` to the archive root and every `.flac` to `<MIX_ARCHIVE>/FLAC_CONVERTED_OUTPUTS`, creating that folder when needed. It scans both the archive's `MP_AUDIOWAV_REC_OUTPUT` and the install-tree fallback, so older recordings are included, and never overwrites an existing file. The primary folder comes from `MIX_ARCHIVE_DIR` (environment, then `config.env`). With no setting the button scans and moves **nothing** and the MP Tray posts a setup reminder; a configured path that is not currently a directory is reported instead of guessed. Success posts an MP Tray notification naming the archive path and the WAV/FLAC counts. A live recording must be stopped first, so the open part cannot be moved while it is being written.
  - **Traktor MIDI-mode buttons map automatically — and only Traktor.** `MP_Liquid_Mixer/mixer_midi.py` watches Traktor ports and maps the S4 MIDI note/CC layout to the mixer's existing actions: Play A/B, Sync A/B, Shift, Browse push/turn, Load, Record, channel volume and deck pitch. A generic controller (MPK mini and the like) is never opened or mapped. It prefers the ALSA sequencer (`aseqdump`), which can be shared with the standalone Traktor service, and falls back to rawmidi. Mapping follows hot-plug, a user override file (`liquid_mixer_midi.json`) can change individual notes/CCs, and the native evdev path still works; duplicate button presses seen by both paths within 80 ms are coalesced. The top-bar status now reports `Traktor MIDI <name>` when mapped.
- **MP Liquid Mixer desktop shortcut and the Exit_MP_Video.mp4 launch/exit bracket** (`MP_Liquid_Mixer.desktop`, `scripts/launch_liquid_mixer.sh`, `MP_Liquid_Mixer/MP_Liquid_Mixer.py`, `scripts/install_desktop_shortcuts.py`, `install.sh`, `scripts/uninstall_apps.py`):
  - **Shortcut style matches the suite.** Linux gets **MP Liquid Mixer** on the Desktop and in the application menu, written by `install_desktop_shortcuts.py` with the same `mp-desktop-exec` resolution, icon, categories and `Terminal=false` as MP Liquid Player. The launcher resolves the install tree, picks the GTK/PyGObject interpreter and the bundled VTE typelib, drops another window's deck identity, then `exec`s `MP_Liquid_Mixer/run.sh`.
  - **The installer creates it.** Every Linux selection except "none" writes the mixer shortcut beside the four selectable apps; macOS, Windows and WSL never do, because the mixer is an X11/XWayland, VTE and ALSA host. The uninstaller keeps it while any app remains and removes it with the last one.
  - **The movie runs on both sides of the window.** The mixer plays `Exit_MP_Video.mp4` through `scripts/display_loader_video.sh --intro-force` **before** GTK is imported or the window is built, and `--exit-force` **after** `window.destroy()` (with pending GTK events drained) in `run()`'s `finally`. The desktop shortcut, the `X` key in another MP window and `./MP_Liquid_Mixer/run.sh` all reach this one implementation, so the clip cannot play twice. A missing movie or player is logged and never blocks the mixer.
  - **One mixer, not two.** Pressing the shortcut while the mixer is open raises the existing window instead of failing, and the launcher claims the single-instance path the other MP windows already use.
- **`M` in the MP Liquid Player now draws the same player in a vertical window** (`MP_Audio_Player.py`, `MP_Audio_Player_Liquid_Glass.py`, `MP_Audio_Player_Liquid_Glass_OpenGL.py`, `MP_Audio_Player_Liquid_Glass_macOS.py`, `MP_Audio_Player_Liquid_Glass_OpenGL_macOS.py`, `scripts/liquid_ui_size.py`, `scripts/exit_video.py`, `tests/test_vertical_player_mode.py`, `tests/test_liquid_glass_player_features.py`, `tests/test_mini_micro_player_features.py`):
  - **The key.** `M` now walks **Normal → Vertical → Mini → Micro**. Vertical is the **same frame**, not a second player: there is one `_draw_frame`, and Vertical calls it with a narrow, tall character grid. A window opened as Mini or Micro steps up to the portrait frame and back (**MINI → MICRO → VERTICAL → MINI**), because a compact window has no landscape frame to return to.
  - **The shape.** `120x40` landscape becomes **`56x52` portrait** — **518x976** on the glass against 1094x760 — so it is recognisably the same window turned ninety degrees, not a cramped strip.
  - **Nothing was added or replaced.** The header box, `▶ PLAYING`, the now-playing title, the format line, the seek bar, the `DJ PITCH` slider, the analyser, the action row, the playlist with its scroll footer and the status and key-hint rows are all the ones the landscape window already draws. `E` still opens the equaliser HUD, `b` the bass dial, `c` the theme cycle, `Z` the visualizer.
  - **Only the narrow width adapts.** The header falls back to shorter title and subtitle forms instead of being sliced mid-word; the action row drops the `VISUALIZATION` chip (its keys still work) rather than running past the border, and a dropped chip gets no click span; the format line drops the device name; the timeline gives its bar the cells the labels leave. The playlist gains the height, showing 13-17 tracks instead of a handful, and the portrait frame pads inside the playlist so it always fills the window it was given.
  - **An earlier attempt is gone.** The first version of this feature was a separate deck renderer with a block-digit clock and its own inline equaliser panel. It has been deleted, along with the EQ-focus key routing it needed; the equaliser is the one `E` already opened.
  - **They used to clip, not resize.** Both macOS hosts never read the size the player published — `_follow_child_ui_size` existed on the Linux hosts only — so pressing `M` there left the glass at the full grid while the child drew something else into its middle. All four hosts now follow the published size, and `liquid_ui_size` accepts `VERTICAL`.
  - **Launch straight in.** `--vertical` joins `--mini` and `--micro`, and all four hosts forward it. The portrait window is a player window, so the intro-video election and the fixed-size KWin rule treat it like Mini and Micro, and the launcher sizes the console to 56x52.
  - **The equaliser would not move.** Pressing a band key painted the new gain for exactly one frame and then showed the old one again. `_get_status()` adopted `eq_preset` and `eq_bands` from the daemon's status file on *every* draw, and that file lags the IPC write by a frame or two, so the stale value overwrote the edit before the next paint. A band set from this window is now remembered until the daemon echoes it (or for three seconds if it never does), like the existing pitch and seek overlays; an external change from another window still lands as soon as the daemon reports it.


- **The Web Companion starts the mix by itself when the page opens, and the Engage button now actually produces sound** (`scripts/mix_web_companion.py`, `tests/test_web_companion_engage_audio.py`, `tests/test_web_companion_playlist.py`, `tests/test_web_companion_autoplay.py`):
  - **Symptom.** Browsing to `http://192.168.1.11:8888/` left the page sitting on **Engage Audio Stream**, and pressing it produced no sound together with a **"The music library stopped responding — skipping is paused"** banner. The page had an automatic start, but it bailed out on iPhone and, more importantly, whenever the desktop MP Audio Player was on air — the normal state of this host — so in practice every visit began with a tap.
  - **Why the tap could not help.** The selected library was **Music** with all four SMB shares down. Their rclone/FUSE mount points still answered `stat()` from the attribute cache, so 55,478 tracks looked like a healthy library and were offered to the page; the read then failed with *Transport endpoint is not connected*. The server had already committed `Content-Length: 10346357` and delivered ~1 KB before the read died, which the browser reports as `ERR_CONTENT_LENGTH_MISMATCH`. The dashboard could not tell that from "this file is corrupt", so it walked the skip queue, reached its burst limit and stopped with the library banner. **No button press could have worked: there was nothing readable to play.**
  - **What changed.** Three independent fixes, because this was three bugs wearing one symptom:
    - **The pool is skipped, not promised.** `_serve_audio_stream()` reads one byte before writing any header, so an unreachable file is a clean `404` the page skips instead of a truncated `200` it misreads. `current_stream_file()` also re-points its cursor at the first *readable* entry, so a partially-offline share serves the tracks that still work instead of the ones that do not.
    - **Reachability is a read, not a stat.** `_music_sample_reachable()` (used to decide whether the library is usable) and the new `_path_readable_now()` open the file. A cached `stat()` on a dead mount can no longer call 55k unreadable tracks healthy.
    - **A hiccup cannot blacklist the library.** `track_failed` now records a mix as undecodable only when the host can actually read it, and `MEDIA_ERR_NETWORK` is treated as a dropped connection. A share outage can no longer permanently skip good files.
  - **The automatic start still works where a browser permits it.** Where it does not, the gate says **"Tap anywhere to start the mix on this device."** and the first touch anywhere — dashboard, tab, transport row, Engage button — starts the stream. **A correction to an earlier draft of this note:** default Chrome refuses *every* autoplay, including muted playback and a silent clip, so an audible automatic start is only possible in a browser that permits media autoplay for the origin. The claim that "Chrome and Firefox on a LAN origin allow it" was wrong; it was measured against a Chrome launched with `--autoplay-policy=no-user-gesture-required`, which is not a normal browser.
  - **A dead library now says so and offers a way out.** When Music is selected and nothing in it can be read, `/api/status` reports `music_offline` and the gate shows a second **Switch to Mix Archive** button that changes library and starts playback in the same tap, rather than leaving the listener on a control that can only repeat the warning.
  - **An unattended start cannot damage the library.** A run no listener began may retry and explain, but never writes a file off.
  - **Crash fix found while testing it:** `lastEngageTapAt` started at `0` and the 700 ms de-dupe compares against `performance.now()`, so any engage tap in the first 700 ms of the page's life was silently swallowed. It now starts at `-Infinity`.
  - **Verified against the real host, not just the HTML.** `tests/test_web_companion_engage_audio.py` drives headless Chrome over DevTools: one test proves a permissive browser starts the mix with no gesture, one proves the clock really advances (a `play()` that resolves on a stalled stream is still silent), one proves the default strict policy offers the tap invitation and that a single mid-page touch starts audio, and one proves a dead mount answers `404` instead of a truncated body. Measured on the affected host: a tap now yields `paused:false` and a clock that advanced 9.0 s → 15.3 s with no banner and no media errors, where before it was `paused:true`, `error.code 4`, and the library banner.

### Fixed
- **The Web Companion stops covering a playing mix on an iPhone, and stops growing memory until iOS kills the page** (`scripts/mix_web_companion.py`, `tests/test_dynamic_rainbow_liquid_glass.py`, `tests/test_web_companion_autoplay.py`, `tests/test_web_companion_engage_audio.py`):
  - **Symptom.** On an iPhone 16 in Safari against all three companions (.11, .13, .138) the dashboard came up on the **Engage Audio Stream** gate and stayed there: a tap started the mix, and the gate snapped straight back over it with the page pinned to the top of the screen. The previous release assumed the theme's compositor load was the whole story. It was not — two separate defects were still live, and both reproduce in this tree.
  - **Cause 1 — the gate never spent its attempt.** An iPhone takes the `needsPlaybackGesture()` branch of `maybeAutoStartStream()`, which returned **without setting `streamAutoStarted`**. That flag is the only thing that stops the branch being taken again, so every 2 s status poll re-entered it and re-showed the gate. `showStreamOverlay()` adds `overlay-active`, which sets `body { position: fixed; height: 100dvh; overflow: hidden }`, so the dashboard was locked at the top of the screen with the engage card over it while the audio kept playing underneath — exactly "fails to play the music and keeps the page open from the start". Reproduced headlessly with an iPhone UA: `overlay:true, bodyPos:"fixed"` on every poll after a successful tap. The branch now spends its one attempt, a leading guard dismisses the gate when the element is already playing, and `renderStreamOverlay()` refuses to cover a playing element at all, so no future caller can reintroduce the state.
  - **Cause 2 — a 4 Hz style write that WebKit never reclaimed.** The theme's hue was stepped from JavaScript every 250 ms by rewriting `--rainbow-hue` on the document element. Twenty-seven custom properties (borders, badges, both button gradients, the wash gradients) derive from it, so each write restyled the whole document. Measured in **WebKitGTK** — the same engine as Safari — at 430×932: the process grew **+0.81 MB/s, about 49 MB a minute and 2.9 GB an hour**, and burned **4.8 % of a core** while nothing else on the page moved. Stopping the timer stopped the growth immediately, and dropping only the timer's target or the derived properties did not, which is what ruled out the page's own layout. iOS ends a WebContent process that grows like that while it is also decoding FLAC, and Safari then silently reloads the page.
  - **What replaced it.** The colour turn is now a **CSS `hue-rotate` animation on the wash layer**, which the compositor runs without touching style: same 24 s cycle, measured at **+0.01 MB/s and 0.4 % of a core** — 90× less memory growth and 13× less CPU than the timer it replaces. The chrome keeps the theme's violet palette and stays steady, which also reads more calmly than a whole page changing hue four times a second. `rainbowTimer` is kept as the single place the palette is applied, but it is no longer an interval, so `stopRainbowDrive()` and the hidden-tab and Reduce-Motion paths still mean what they say.
  - **Verified end to end, not just in the HTML.** A new browser-driven test drives the real page as an iPhone: one touch tap starts the mix, and the gate must then stay away across four further polls with `bodyPos` still `static` and the clock advancing. It fails on the previous tree (`autoStarted:false, overlay:true, bodyPos:"fixed"`) and passes now. A 100-second soak of the fixed page in WebKitGTK held RSS flat at **200 → 201 MB** with audio playing, against **205 → 281 MB** for the version that shipped.
  - **Also fixed:** the same soak exposed that the served page and the tests disagreed about which hue mechanism was live — the tests pinned `setInterval(tickRainbow, 250)` as the *desired* behaviour. They now assert the opposite and name the measurement, so the timer cannot come back.
- **The console tracklist window no longer writes over its own frame, and the header title is centred** (`scripts/view_tracklist_console.sh`, `tests/test_tracklist_viewer_layout.py`):
  - **Symptom.** Pressing `T` opened the borderless tracklist window with the first character of every line sitting under the left rounded corner, long track titles and remix names running past the right edge, and the header title visibly left of centre.
  - **The margin.** Every row is now drawn inside a content box with a three-cell margin at each side (two and then one as the window narrows), and the magenta rules span exactly that box instead of the full terminal width. Nothing is ever written into the margin, so no glyph can land on the glass frame.
  - **The clipping.** Widths are measured in real terminal cells — an emoji counts two, a combining mark none — instead of raw character counts, and every header value, body row, track row and the status line is cut to the box with a trailing `…`. The remix in parentheses can no longer poke out of the right edge, and a long export path in the status row is shortened instead of running under the frame.
  - **The header.** The four header rows share a fixed twelve-cell label field, so `File:`, `Location:`, `Platform:` and `Theme:` line up and their values start in the same column. The title is centred on the content box using those same cell widths, which is what the hard-coded leading spaces used to get wrong once the emoji were involved.
  - **A one-row scroll.** The frame used to draw one row per terminal row and then emit a final newline, which scrolled everything up by one line — that is why the top rule was missing from the screenshot. In a terminal the last row is now written without a trailing newline, so the frame never scrolls. Piped output keeps its newline.
- **Left/Right seek in the Web Companion, and the macOS bundle no longer dies on a Homebrew Python bump** (`scripts/mix_web_companion.py`, `scripts/mix_web_companion.sh`, `tests/test_web_companion_stream.py`, `tests/test_web_companion_macos_service.py`):
  - **Symptom.** On a laptop, a keyboard case or a TV browser, pressing **`Left` / `Right`** on the dashboard did nothing to the mix. The transport row was pointer-only: the only `keydown` listeners on the page belonged to the visualizer tile, the assistant prompt and the autoplay engage retry, and none of them touched playback.
  - **What changed.** A document-level handler turns a bare **`Right`** into **+10 s** and a bare **`Left`** into **−10 s**, with **`Shift`** for ±30 s. It calls `skipBy()` rather than setting `currentTime` itself, so the keys inherit everything the buttons already rely on: the seekable-range probe when `duration` is `Infinity`, the clamped target at both ends of the mix, the parked offset that stacks before metadata arrives, and the forward to a desk player that owns the sound.
  - **The desk player now gets the right distance.** `seek_forward` / `seek_backward` are hard-coded to 30 s in the server, so a 10 s arrow sent that way would have moved the room 30 s while the browser's silent mirror moved 10 — the two would disagree on every press. The key handler passes `{ relativeSeconds: true }`, which sends `seek` with the real distance; the two transport **buttons keep their own fixed 30 s actions**.
  - **It never steals a keystroke that means something else.** Arrows inside the playlist search, the assistant prompt or any `input`/`textarea`/`select`/contenteditable are left alone, as are `Ctrl`/`Cmd`/`Alt`-arrows (word jumps, line jumps, Back). The handler is inert on the assistant tab, while the engage gate is up, and when nothing is queued, so a laptop can still scroll the page.
  - **Holding the key scrubs.** OS key-repeat is throttled to 150 ms, so a held arrow does not fire sixty seeks a second at a service that is also decoding FLAC.
  - **Verified in a real browser, on the live three hosts.** Headless Chrome against `.11`: an engaged page moved **+12.3 s / −7.7 s** (10 s plus elapsed playback), and a strict-autoplay browser that had to tap Engage first moved **+12.4 s / −7.6 s**. A JS harness on the desk-owned path confirms it sends `seek` with `seconds: 10`, `-10` and `30`, not 30 s every time.
  - **Second defect, found while deploying: `.138` was serving nothing at all.** Its bundle launcher had baked in `/opt/homebrew/Cellar/python@3.15/`**`3.15.1`**`/.../Python`; Homebrew had since moved to **3.15.2**, so `dlopen` failed and the job reported as loaded while the port was dead. The launcher now keeps the baked path as its first choice and falls back to a Cellar glob, then to the existing `exec` fallback, so a patch-level bump can no longer take the companion down. Compiled natively and proven: baked path missing → Cellar search → child still runs, exit code 0.
  - **Deployed to all three.** `.11` (systemd) and `.13` (systemd, `/home/mplanetarian/MP_MANAGER_TESTER`) restarted on the new file and now serve the handler; `.138` (macOS, launchd) was rebuilt with `mix_web_companion.sh install` and is serving it too. All three report the same checksum. `.138`'s Mac is sitting at a login screen — its console user is `root` — so launchd's `gui/501` domain refused a bootstrap; the agent loads at next login and the wrapper's fallback is what is serving until then.
  - **Pin tests.** `KeyboardSeekTest` (9 cases) covers the binding, the step, the text-field and modifier guards, the assistant-tab/gate/no-file guards, the repeat throttle and the button tooltips; the macOS suite gains a case that fails if the launcher goes back to an exact-only libpython path.

- **The Web Companion stops reloading itself on an iPhone in Safari** (`scripts/mix_web_companion.py`, `tests/test_dynamic_rainbow_liquid_glass.py`, `tests/test_web_companion_stream.py`):
  - **Symptom.** Both companions (192.168.1.13 and 192.168.1.138) would play, then the page would snap back to the top and restart the mix. It repeated indefinitely on an iPhone 16 Pro Max in Safari, on both hosts, and neither host showed anything wrong in its log.
  - **Why it was not the page.** The served JavaScript contains no reload path at all — `reloadForFreshInstance()` is defined and never called — and headless Chrome driving the real page as an iPhone UA over three minutes recorded **exactly one document load** while audio advanced 0 → 180 s with a flat 9.54 MB heap. A page cannot reload itself here, so the reload was iOS Safari reloading a **WebContent process that had been killed**. Both hosts run the same theme, `dynamic_rainbow_liquid_glass`, which was the common factor.
  - **Cause: a 14.4 MP compositor layer animated forever.** The theme's wash used `inset: -50%` on a `position: fixed` layer with `animation: dynamicRainbowHue` rotating it 360° — on a 430×932 viewport at DPR 3 that is **14.4 megapixels, about 58 MB of RGBA**, promoted to its own layer (`will-change: transform`) and re-composited every frame. Worse, `.card` and `.overlay-card` carried `backdrop-filter: blur(18px)` directly above it, so the compositor read back and re-blurred a backdrop that never stopped changing. Under that load iOS ends the WebContent process and silently reloads the page, which is exactly the behaviour reported. The hue is stepped in JavaScript anyway, so the oversized box and the rotation bought nothing but cost.
  - **What changed.** The wash is `inset: 0` — exactly the viewport, **75 % fewer pixels, 43 MB freed** — and animates **opacity only** (`dynamicRainbowBreath`), never a transform, and carries no `will-change`. The theme's cards keep their translucent fill but drop `backdrop-filter`, so nothing re-blurs the moving backdrop. The hue timer also drops from **10 Hz to 4 Hz**: each tick rewrites `--rainbow-hue`, which 27 theme custom properties derive from, so every tick was a full document style recalculation; at 4 Hz the 24 s cycle still steps 1.7° at a time.
  - **Also fixed: the always-on spectrum processor.** `ensureAudioGraph()` called `attachSpectrumPull()` on every path, so an element source — every iPhone — got a second 2048-frame `ScriptProcessorNode` waking the audio thread ~21 times a second forever on top of the audible connection that already pulls the graph. It is now built only for the `captureStream` path, which is the only one that needs it, and the node that iOS most often tears the audio session down over is gone.
  - **Verified.** The served page on both hosts now reports `inset: 0`, no rotation, no `will-change`, no card blur, a 250 ms hue timer and a capture-only pull. A 2-minute soak with real FLAC playing in the fixed build held heap flat at 9.54 MB with **zero DOM growth** (previously 395 → 451 nodes). `tests.test_dynamic_rainbow_liquid_glass` grows to 19 tests and `tests.test_web_companion_stream` to 44, both pinning the new invariants so the layer cannot grow back; the companion suite passes **407** locally and **401 on 192.168.1.13** (4 skipped).
  - **Deployed.** Both companions updated and restarted (systemd on .13, launchd on .138), with the previous script kept beside it as `mix_web_companion.py.pre-iosreload.<timestamp>`. The new companion also imports `scripts/playlist_formats.py`, which both hosts were missing, so it was deployed with it — without it every playlist read returned nothing.
- **MP Gui's Vertical menu fills the whole window instead of wasting the bottom of the glass** (`Mix_Archive_Manager.sh`, `tests/test_gui_vertical_menu_fill.py`):
  - **Symptom.** After pressing **M** in MP Gui the window is portrait and much taller than the menu, but the main menu ended at its `Selection:` line with a strip of empty glass below the footer. Measured on a 109x74 portrait window the last painted row was 60, so about a seventh of the window was never used.
  - **Why.** The single-column frame is only as tall as its options. That is right for the landscape window, whose height is sized to the menu, but Vertical is sized to a portrait aspect, and `max_visible` did not scroll the list because every option already fitted. Nothing could use the remaining rows.
  - **What changed.** When every option already fits and M has published **VERTICAL**, the spare rows are shared out between neighbouring options, so the list runs the full height of the glass and the rule and the Selection line end on the bottom rows. Quotient and remainder give gaps that differ by at most one row, so the spacing stays even. A list too long to fit is untouched and keeps its existing `▲`/`▼` scroll markers.
  - **Scoped to Vertical.** A new `_mp_gui_vertical_menu()` reads `~/.config/mix-manager/gui_window_shape.$$`, the same file M writes and the glass host reads. Horizontal, a plain console and the multi-screen column layout are untouched: their frame still ends where the options end.
  - **Row accounting.** The spare-row total is derived from the finished header buffer rather than from the header's counters, which can be one row out when a banner line is painted but not counted. A narrow portrait window wraps the Selection line, so every wrapped row is subtracted too; without that the top of the frame scrolled away.
  - **Navigation untouched.** Options keep their row numbers in `_menu_item_row`, so the arrow-key fast repaint, mouse hit testing, wheel scrolling and the search filter all address the new rows. The status line is composed once per frame and reused.
  - **Measured on the real manager.** `tests/test_gui_vertical_menu_fill.py` paints a controlled menu on a pty: it asserts the last used row equals the window height at 109x74, 60x90, 76x100, 80x120 and 100x60, that Horizontal and a run with no shape still end short of the window, that a scrolling list keeps its markers, and that every option is still painted.

- **Steam Controller support actually works now, and the other pads' inputs are read correctly** (`scripts/gamepad_bridge.py`, `tests/test_gamepad_controller.py`, `docs/GAMEPAD_CONTROLLERS.md`):
  - **Symptom.** An Xbox pad drives the menus, but a Steam Controller plugged in over USB never appeared in **Studio Hardware → Game Controllers → Show Connected Controllers**, and the live button test showed nothing. The Nintendo layout was also mapped as if the D-pad arrived on hat axes, which no Nintendo pad does.
  - **Why the Steam pad was invisible.** `hid_steam` starts a Steam Controller in *lizard mode*, where the pad reports itself as a keyboard and a mouse rather than a gamepad. Until its gamepad node is opened there is no pad to find, so the bridge's scan saw only `Valve Software Wired Controller` keyboard and mouse nodes and skipped them. The kernel disables lizard mode as soon as the gamepad node is opened, so the node has to be reached by name once it exists — `--doctor` previously reported the pad as a missing driver, which sent the user looking for a module that was already loaded and bound.
  - **Two constants were wrong for a real Steam pad.** The bridge listened for triggers on `ABS_HAT1X`/`ABS_HAT1Y` (`0x12`/`0x13`); `hid_steam` reports them on `ABS_HAT2X`/`ABS_HAT2Y` (`0x14`/`0x15`), so no trigger ever produced a previous/next-track key. It also treated the D-pad as `EV_ABS` codes in the `0x220` range; on Steam those codes are `BTN_DPAD_*` **key** events. Both are fixed, and the bridge still accepts a Windows-style `-1/0/1` hat.
  - **Steam's left pad is analog, and the old hat code assumed it was not.** A Steam pad reports `ABS_HAT0X`/`ABS_HAT0Y` across `-32767..32767`, so the sign test fired on the smallest resting drift and the menu scrolled on its own. The hat now reads its real range from the driver and uses the same press/release hysteresis as a stick, so a light touch does nothing and a real push moves one row.
  - **D-pad presses now repeat and cannot cancel each other.** The Steam D-pad and left pad arrive as key events, which the bridge previously ignored entirely for movement. They now run through the same held-direction repeat as a stick, kernel autorepeat is not replayed, and holding two directions reports the newest one instead of releasing the first.
  - **The doctor tells the truth.** It now distinguishes a Steam pad in lizard mode from a missing driver, checks `xpad`, `hid_playstation`, `hid_nintendo` and `hid_steam`, and warns when the running kernel has `CONFIG_BT_HIDP` off, which is why a wireless Switch Pro Controller cannot connect at all (`br-connection-create-socket`).
  - **Verified on the attached hardware and through uinput.** A virtual Steam pad built to `hid_steam`'s real capabilities (analog hat, `BTN_DPAD_*`, `ABS_HAT2*`) sends `ENTER`, a repeating `DOWN` and `CHAR:n` through the bridge; the Xbox test still passes. 39 tests run, 0 failed.

- **A theme switch no longer leaves the old palette in the header banner** (`Mix_Archive_Manager.sh`, `tests/test_theme_cache_invalidation.py`):
  - **Symptom.** Choosing a theme with **C**, from **Settings → Themes**, or in another window (MP Gui / the Liquid Player) repainted the menu rows, the box borders and the selection bar in the new palette, but the block above them — the statistics, cloud-backup, weather, planets, audio-interface, alarm and CPU/RAM/disk lines — stayed in the colours of the theme that had just been left. On a light theme such as **Sensorium** the stale dark ink was barely readable; the correct colours only appeared after a background worker happened to run, which could take a minute or more. A Mac test bed left sitting on Sensorium was still showing Sensorium-coloured statistics when it came back up in **Dreamworlds Ultra**.
  - **Why.** Three caches store *finished* lines with their ANSI sequences already expanded: `header_cache.env` (`save_header_cache()`), `perf_display.cache` with `perf_display.stamp`, and the `.header_refresh_stamp` gate that throttles the worker. `save_theme()` rewrote the theme file and repainted the palette but never touched any of them, so the next frame reloaded the old bytes verbatim. The 30-second cache lock and 60-second spawn gate then kept the stale block on screen well past the switch.
  - **What changed.** A new `_invalidate_theme_caches()` deletes those four files, blanks the in-memory `_CACHED_*` copies so the very next frame is drawn in the new palette, and zeroes the refresh throttles so the header repopulates immediately instead of waiting for the gate. It is called from `save_theme()` (the C key and the themes menu) and from `sync_theme_from_file()` (a change made in MP Gui or the Liquid Player).
  - **Second line of defence.** `load_header_cache()` now discards a cache whose mtime is older than the theme file. That covers a writer which only touches `theme` — an older tree on another machine, a restored backup, or a hand edit — so a stale palette cannot survive even when the invalidation call is bypassed.
  - **Also fixed alongside.** The same function that ordered the btop palette, `_sync_btop_theme()`, used GNU `sed -i` with no backup suffix. BSD sed reads that suffix as the argument to `-i` and fails with `invalid command code f`, so **on macOS btop never followed the manager theme**: the `.theme` file was copied into `~/.config/btop/themes` but `btop.conf` kept `color_theme = "Default"`. It now rewrites through a temp file and `mv`, which is portable to both seds and leaves no backup litter. The SMTP/outreach config writer had the identical bug, so it got the same treatment.

- **The MP Visualizer behind MP Gui no longer outlives the menu** (`scripts/visualizer_player_bond.py`, `scripts/liquid_visualizer_session.py`, `MP_Audio_Player_Liquid_Glass.py`, `MP_Audio_Player_Liquid_Glass_OpenGL.py`, `MP_Audio_Player_Liquid_Glass_macOS.py`, `MP_Audio_Player_Liquid_Glass_OpenGL_macOS.py`, `tests/test_visualizer_player_bond.py`, `tests/test_mp_gui.py`):
  - **Symptom.** Pressing **Escape** at the MP Gui menu (or clicking its close button, or choosing **q**) closed the menu but left the full-size MP Liquid Visualizer window sitting on the desktop. With the exit movie enabled the visualizer stayed up for the whole clip and then vanished; with the host killed outright — the tray's **Close MP Mix Manager (Closes everything)**, a `kill`, a crash, a logout — it never went away at all and had to be closed by hand.
  - **Cause 1 — the wrong order.** Each host destroyed its own glass, then played `Exit_MP_Video.mp4`, and only afterwards ran the companion cleanup in `main()`'s `finally`. For the length of the movie the background window was the only thing on screen.
  - **Cause 2 — the cleanup lived in the wrong process.** `stop_companion_visualizer()` is called *by the host*, so anything that ended the host without letting it return from `run()` — `SIGKILL`, the tray's hard close, a segfault — skipped it. The visualizer is a separate process that `start_new_session` detaches, so nothing else reaped it.
  - **What changed.** The companion is now retired in the same breath as the window it belongs behind: `_close_then_exit_video()` (and the OpenGL/macOS hosts' teardown) stops it immediately after `self.close()` and **before** the movie starts, so the desktop is empty for the clip exactly as intended. A new `stop_companion_for_mode()` keeps that decision in one place.
  - **The companion also watches its own host.** `PlayerBondMixin` probes once a second for the host it was started for (`liquid_gui_host_running` / `liquid_player_host_running`) and closes itself when that host has been gone for 2 s — 15 s if it has never seen a host yet, which covers the launch window where `MP_Gui.sh` spawns the visualizer before `exec`'ing the host. This is what fixes the killed-host case on every backend, since all three renderers mix in the same bond code.
  - **Nothing else is touched.** Only a locked companion (`MP_VISUALIZER_COMPANION=1`) is affected: a window opened with **Z** or from the desktop shortcut (`MP_VISUALIZER_DETACHED=1`) is the user's own and stays up. On Windows and macOS there is no `/proc`, so the host cannot be observed and the window is never closed by the watchdog — the orderly path still handles those hosts.
  - **Multi-window.** The stop is deliberately not forced: up to four MP Gui windows share one companion, so closing one of several leaves the background of the rest alone. Measured live: closing the first of two MP Gui windows keeps the visualizer, closing the last removes it within about a second.
  - **Also fixed alongside.** The manager path no longer force-kills the shared companion, and an `--embed-check` probe (a startup health check that opens and immediately closes a window) can no longer blank a companion the user is watching.

- **MP Reporter no longer emails OpenCode progress briefings when no session is running** (`scripts/mp_opencode_progress_reporter.py`, `tests/test_mp_opencode_progress_reporter.py`, `config.env.example`):
  - **Symptom.** With no OpenCode session active the ten-minute timer still built a text report, synthesised a spoken briefing that said "0 agent sessions running", and mailed both to every recipient. The log shows the empty sends at 08:51, 09:01, 09:11, 09:21 and 09:31.
  - **Why.** The gate was opt-in: it only skipped an idle window when `OPENCODE_PROGRESS_SEND_WHEN_IDLE` was explicitly `"false"`, and the shipped default — still present in `~/MP_REPORTER/email_config.env` — was `"true"`.
  - **What changed.** A scheduled dispatch now probes the running sessions first and returns before writing any file, synthesising any audio or opening SMTP when the list is empty. `OPENCODE_PROGRESS_SEND_WHEN_IDLE` is gone from the defaults and documented as ignored, so a stale `"true"` line cannot restore the empty mail. The same check is repeated after the report is built, so a forced dispatch cannot mail an empty report either; `--dry-run` still previews the empty rendering.
  - **Race removed.** The gate and the report now share one `collect_running_sessions()` result (`build_report_data(..., collected=...)`), so the two passes can no longer disagree and send a "0 running" email past a gate that saw a session.

- **Retro Linux Memories text is readable again: the CLI pane is dark gray instead of a pale slate** (`Mix_Archive_Manager.sh`, `MP_Audio_Player.py`, `scripts/mp_theme_palette.py`, `tests/test_cli_theme_background.py`, `tests/test_menu_text_theme_ink.py`, `tests/test_retro_linux_memories_theme.py`, `tests/test_sensorium_theme.py`):
  - **Symptom.** Picking theme option **19** left the manager, the audio player and the tracklist console with **white text on a pale blue-gray slate**. The menu rows, the `Selection:` line and the playlist were washed out, and the theme's own dark ink had to be invented to compensate.
  - **Why.** The pane was `#B0C4D6` (176, 196, 214), a light tint chosen when the theme only needed to differ from the other picks. That is 1.5:1 against white, far below the 4.5:1 WCAG AA floor. Three tables had grown special cases to cope: a hand-picked `#644500` console ink for the player, a playlist-row exemption, and a pinned 1.5:1 pair in the ink test.
  - **What changed.** The console pane is now **dark gray `#2D2D2D` (45, 45, 45)**, the same charcoal field the theme's Liquid Glass pane already paints, so the manager and the glass finally agree. White text measures **13.6:1** on it. The `#644500` console ink, the playlist-row exemption and the pinned low-contrast test pair are all gone, because nothing needs them any more: `theme_console_body_ansi()` now simply returns the theme's body ink, and `theme_has_light_console()` correctly reports `False` for this theme.
  - **Unchanged on purpose.** The Liquid Glass, Vulkan and Metal panes, the VeraCrypt blue selection (`#15539e`), the amber menu number, the btop palette and the web companion are untouched — only the terminal/console pane moved.
- **The manager opens instantly instead of waiting on the startup logo and the screen-span retry** (`scripts/display_loader_video.sh`, `Mix_Archive_Manager.sh`, `tests/test_loader_video.py`):
  - **Why it felt broken.** `MP_loader.mp4` is 10.01 s long and `display_startup_loader_video` waited for it to finish *before* the first menu was drawn, so every launch sat on the logo for about ten seconds. A cold start measured 13.5 s from entry to the first frame of the menu.
  - **The logo is now once per session.** A marker in `~/.config/mix-manager/loader_video_session` records a key built from the kernel boot id, the login session id and the session type. The first launch after a login plays the whole clip; reopening the manager afterwards returns in about 10 ms and goes straight to the menu. Logging out, rebooting or switching Wayland/X11 changes the key, so the logo comes back. `force=1` callers (the installer and the loader tests) are unaffected.
  - **The screen-span loop no longer burns four rounds.** It used to call `mp_screen_span_console`, `sleep 0.5`, re-probe and repeat up to four times whether or not the window had already stretched — about six seconds when the window manager never confirms, and about two seconds when it does. It now spans, then polls the real window size every 150 ms and stops at the first confirmation, with at most one retry.
  - **The KWin settle delay is measured, not fixed.** `mp_screen_span_console` waited a flat `sleep 0.5` for KWin to run the script. The window actually resizes in about 90 ms, so the wait is now 0.1 s and the caller's poll owns the confirmation. A decorated window is a few pixels narrower than the desktop, so the span call deliberately does not wait for an exact pixel match.
  - **Measured result.** The span block fell from ~6.0 s (unconfirmed) and ~2.0 s (confirmed) to ~1.1 s and ~0.2 s respectively. A second launch of the same session now reaches the menu in about 2.3 s, and the first still shows the full logo.
  - **Tests isolate `$HOME`.** `tests/test_loader_video.py` now gives each test an empty config directory. Without it, a test that runs after a real launch would find the session marker and silently skip the logo path it was checking.

### Fixed
- **A boot-death ledger that tells a power cut apart from a kernel panic** (`scripts/mp_boot_death_ledger.sh`, `tests/test_boot_death_ledger.py`, `REPORTS/POWER_CRASH_INVESTIGATION_20261115/`):
  - **Why.** The workstation has had 51 unclean boots out of 123 since 31 Oct. Two unrelated causes were hiding behind the same symptom, because in both cases the journal simply stops: a recoverable kernel oops was being turned into a full reboot by `oops=panic`, and separately the machine was losing power outright. Nothing in `last -x` can tell those apart.
  - **The signal.** The kernel reserves 8 MB of DRAM for crash capture (`reserve_mem=8M:4096:ramoops`). DRAM survives a **warm** reboot, so a panic dump is still readable and the next boot logs `ramoops: using 0x800000@…`. A **total power loss** loses DRAM and destroys the ECC header, so the next boot logs `ramoops: uncorrectable error in header` (10× here). The verdict for a boot is therefore written by the boot that *follows* it: `header_err > 0` means power went away, `0` means a warm reset that can only be a panic, a watchdog or an orderly reboot.
  - **Validated against a known death.** The 13 Nov boot died in `kvm_create_pit` with a confirmed `Kernel panic`; the boot after it opened with `header_err=0`, and the archived dump is stamped inside that boot's death window. A surviving dump is only attributed to a death whose window it falls inside, so a stale dump cannot relabel an older power cut as a panic.
  - **What it stores.** An append-only TSV keyed by boot ID at `${MP_BOOT_LEDGER:-$HOME/.local/state/mp-mix-manager/boot-deaths.tsv}`, so the history outlives journald retention (only ~232 MB here). Re-running is idempotent; `--install` adds a `--user` oneshot unit, `--revert`-style `--reset` clears it, and `--json` feeds the reports.
  - **Two silent-wrong-answer bugs found while building it**, both pinned by tests: `journalctl | grep -q` returns 141 under `set -o pipefail` (grep exits early, the writer takes SIGPIPE) so a **successful** match reads as "no match" and every clean reboot was misreported as unclean — fixed with a herestring; and `VERDICTS=""` followed by `VERDICTS+=(x)` leaves a phantom empty element at index 0, shifting every verdict by a row — fixed with `VERDICTS=()`.

### Fixed
- **`oops=panic` no longer turns a survivable kernel fault into a reboot** (`scripts/mp_fix_oops_panic.sh`, `tests/test_fix_oops_panic.py`):
  - **Symptom.** Any kernel oops rebooted the machine in about a second, which erased the log that would have identified it and made a driver bug look exactly like a power cut.
  - **What it actually cost.** On 13 Nov, starting a QEMU VM hit `BUG: unable to handle page fault for address: 00000000100023c0` at `kvm_create_pit+0x68/0x350 [kvm]`. That is a recoverable fault in the legacy i8254 PIT creation path; it should have killed the VM and left the desktop running. Instead it panicked and rebooted after 23 h of uptime.
  - **What changes.** Only `oops=panic` is removed from `/boot/loader/entries/ostree-99-custom-7.3.4-trimmed.conf`. The flags that catch a genuinely wedged machine stay: `nmi_watchdog=panic`, `softlockup_panic=1`, `hung_task_panic=1` and `panic=30`. Those cases *should* restart; `oops=panic` does not.
  - **Safe by construction.** Dry-run is the default and writes nothing. `--apply` backs the entry up to `*.mp-original` once and rewrites only the `options` line; a test pins that the result equals the original minus exactly that one token, with no double spaces and every other line byte-identical. `--revert` restores the backup verbatim.
- **A Bluetooth headset's play button started a video instead of pausing the music** (`scripts/mp_mpris.py`, `MP_Audio_Player.py`, `MP_Audio_Player_Liquid_Glass.py`, `MP_Audio_Player_Liquid_Glass_OpenGL.py`, `tests/test_mpris_headset_keys.py`):
  - **Symptom.** With the Skullcandy Crusher ANC 2 (and the same for any other Bluetooth headphones) the physical play/pause button did not pause MP Liquid Player. A video started instead, and the mix kept playing.
  - **Why.** The button arrives as a media key. Plasma gives that key to whichever app registered as a media player. MP Liquid Player never registered, so the press went to a video player or the browser.
  - **What changed.** The audio daemon now publishes itself as **MP Liquid Player** on the desktop media bus. Play, Pause, Play/Pause, Next, Previous and Stop from the headset run on the mix. The Crusher sends Play for that button even while the mix is already playing, so Play pauses in that case. A video address is refused, so the button cannot open one. A press that arrives twice (the key, then the Bluetooth bridge) changes playback once. Reopen MP Liquid Player so the running daemon picks this up.

### Removed
- **The console size lock (the "Oversized or Undersized Window Detected" screen) is gone** (`Mix_Archive_Manager.sh`, `tests/test_console_zoom_guard.py`):
  - **What changed.** The whole guard is deleted: the black full-screen warning, the 90 x 26 to 157 x 44 limit check, the `MP_CONSOLE_ZOOM_*` settings, `MP_CONSOLE_ZOOM_GUARD`, `MP_CONSOLE_ZOOM_CLAMP`, the Konsole black colour scheme it pushed, and the ydotool key injection that tried to zoom the window back. `mp_console_zoom_hold`, `mp_console_zoom_after_menu` and `mp_console_zoom_classify` no longer exist, so nothing pauses the menu or blocks a resize.
  - **Why.** It missed resize and zoom events often enough to be unreliable, and every miss either left the menu frozen behind the black screen or let the wrong size through, which is worse than no guard at all. The user-facing decision is to drop it rather than keep patching it.
  - **What still works.** Plain resize handling is untouched: `SIGWINCH` sets `_TERM_RESIZED`, `read_nav_key` returns `RESIZE`, and the menu rescales the box width and header to the new `_TERM_COLS` / `_TERM_LINES`. `tests/test_manager_resize.py` covers that path and still passes.
  - **Leftovers cleaned up.** The exit path no longer calls `_mp_zoom_guard_clamp_release`, `press_enter` no longer polls the guard, and the guard's Konsole scheme file is not written any more.
- **The `Extreme` bass mode is gone from the MP Bass Boost dial** (`MP_Audio_Player.py`, `tests/test_bass_boost.py`, `tests/test_player_queue.py`, `README.md`):
  - **What changed.** MP Audio Player and MP Liquid Player (which hosts the same player) now offer **`Normal` → `Medium` → `High`** and stop there. Only the 100 % position — about **+25 dB at 45 Hz** — was removed; the two remaining steps keep the Crusher curve (their current levels are set by the -3 dB trim noted under **Changed** below).
  - **Why.** At a full +25 dB a loud mix sits permanently in the filter's soft limiter, so the setting added very little bass and a great deal of squashed, pumping top end. The two remaining steps keep the measured Crusher curve without that trade-off.
  - **What happens to a saved `Extreme`.** `~/.config/mix-manager/bass_boost` is read through the same alias table, and `extreme`, `max`, `full`, `100`, `3` and `crusher` now resolve to **`High`** rather than failing, so an existing install opens in the loudest mode that still exists instead of silently dropping to Normal. A stale daemon status file that still says `Extreme` is ignored by the window and the chip falls back to `Normal`.
  - **Every surface follows.** `--bass-mode` rejects anything outside the three modes, `--list-bass-modes` prints three rows ending at **66.7 %**, and the dial's chip is now cyan, green and amber with no red. The `b` / `B` key and the clickable button step three positions and wrap back to `Normal`.

### Changed
- **Closing MP Gui or MP Liquid Player now closes the window at once, then plays the exit movie** (`MP_Audio_Player_Liquid_Glass_OpenGL.py`, `MP_Audio_Player_Liquid_Glass.py`, `MP_Audio_Player_Liquid_Glass_macOS.py`, `MP_Audio_Player_Liquid_Glass_OpenGL_macOS.py`, `scripts/exit_video.py`, `README.md`, `tests/test_exit_video.py`):
  - **What changed.** The order is reversed. The host destroys its own glass window first and only then launches `Exit_MP_Video.mp4`, which plays once on its own and closes itself when it ends, is skipped, or reaches `EXIT_VIDEO_MAX_SECONDS`. Previously the movie ran on top of the still-open window, so the player stayed on screen underneath it for the whole clip.
  - **Why.** Closing a window should make it disappear. Leaving it visible behind the movie read as a frozen app, and the player kept drawing frames that the movie covered anyway.
  - **What is unchanged.** Only the last window of either kind still plays it (MP Gui and MP Liquid Player share one counter, so closing one of two open windows stays silent), it is still elected exactly once when two close together, the tracklist viewer and the embed check stay silent, and `SHOW_EXIT_VIDEO` still turns it off.
  - **Nothing pumps a dead window.** The per-host `_pump_exit_frame` and the `video.wait(pump)` call are gone, since the window no longer exists by then; `wait()` blocks on its own. The three GTK/Vulkan hosts share one `_close_then_exit_video` helper, and the macOS OpenGL host tears down inline in its `finally` before calling `_play_exit_video_after_close`.
- **High on the MP Bass Boost dial is a quarter less powerful** (`MP_Audio_Player.py`, `tests/test_bass_boost.py`, `README.md`):
  - **What changed.** `High` keeps its place on the dial (⅔ of the Crusher curve, after the shared -3 dB trim) and then gives up another quarter of its acoustic power. The peak moves from **+13.7 dB** to **+12.4 dB at 45 Hz**. `Medium` stays at **+5.3 dB** and `Normal` stays a bypass.
  - **How it is implemented.** `BASS_BOOST_HIGH_EXTRA_TRIM_DB` (1.2494 dB, which is `10·log10(4/3)`) is applied only when `bass_mode_peak_db("High")` runs, so the FIR, the engine status, the CLI and `--list-bass-modes` all show the quieter High. Measured on the designed FIR: 45 Hz +12.4 dB, 50 Hz +12.2 dB, 30 Hz +7.3 dB, 100 Hz +4.6 dB, 250 Hz +0.1 dB, 1 kHz 0.0 dB. Against the previous High the power ratio is 0.75.
- **The MP Liquid Player playlist footer sits one row lower** (`MP_Audio_Player.py`, `tests/test_liquid_glass_player_features.py`):
  - **What changed.** The `↑/↓: Scroll …` rule at the bottom of the playlist box — the footer line that starts with `Scroll` — now ends up one row further down in MP Liquid Player. The playlist body claims the row that was held back for it, so a 24-row glass window shows one more mix or track and the rule sits directly above the single blank row before the `Vol` status line, the same distance from the bottom as in the CLI player.
  - **Why.** MP Liquid used to reserve one row more than the CLI frame needed, which parked its footer a row higher and left one fewer track visible than the window could show. Navigator placement should not change between the two hosts.
  - **Heights that are still tight.** A tall glass window keeps its extra breathing row above `Vol`; on a short console that second blank row is still the first thing the existing overflow trim drops (never a control line), which is unchanged. `tests/test_liquid_glass_player_features.py` renders a 40-mix playlist in both hosts and pins the footer's distance from the bottom, plus the blank row and `┘` rule directly above `Vol`.
- **The now-playing title is pure black or pure white, chosen by the pane behind it** (`MP_Audio_Player.py`, `tests/test_now_playing_title_ink.py`):
  - **What changed.** The title line at the top of the player — `▶ PLAYING … ♫ <mix name>` — no longer uses a theme hue. It is painted in the highest-contrast ink its surface allows: **black** on the pale liquid-glass pane, **white** on the two charcoal-pane themes (Retro Linux Memories, Into Darkness). The `♫` glyph now shares that ink instead of staying neon pink beside it.
  - **Why.** The full frame used `COLOR_NEON_PINK` and Mini/Micro used the theme's body ink, and neither has a guaranteed contrast. Pink washed out on the pale glass, and on The Whitest White the body ink was nearly invisible while on the charcoal themes it went the wrong way. The title is the one line read from across the room, so it gets a predictable pair instead of a hue.
  - **The surface decides, because the two disagree.** `now_playing_ink(theme, liquid)` asks per surface. The glass pane is pale on eleven themes whose *console* background is near-black — so a single global rule would have painted black text on a black console on those eleven. `now_playing_uses_white(..., liquid=False)` resolves against `theme_background()` luminance instead. Console contrast is now **8.1:1** at worst, and glass follows the shader's own pane test, which `tests/test_theme_notice_is_black.py` already pins to `theme_uses_dark_glass`.
  - **All three frames.** Full, Mini and Micro all resolve through the one helper, so the title cannot be white in one size and black in another. `tests/test_now_playing_title_ink.py` renders 45 frames per surface (15 themes × 3 sizes) and asserts the colour actually painted by replaying SGR resets, not merely finding an escape somewhere in the line.
  - **Both post-processors keep it.** The visualizer brightness guard would otherwise lift black to grey, and the Liquid bold-stripper rewrites every escape; both are covered so neither can quietly break the ink later.
- **The MP Bass Boost dial is now half as loud** (`MP_Audio_Player.py`, `tests/test_bass_boost.py`, `README.md`):
  - **What changed.** The boost curve is unchanged, but the whole dial is trimmed by **-3.01 dB** before it is designed into the FIR, so each live position gives up exactly half its acoustic power — the half-way point on a decibel scale. `Medium` moves from **+8.3 dB** to **about +5.3 dB at 45 Hz**, and `High` from **+16.7 dB** to **about +13.7 dB at 45 Hz**. `Normal` is untouched because its gain is already zero, so the dial still opens on a true bypass and keeps its two distinct steps.
  - **How it is implemented.** `BASS_BOOST_TRIM_DB` is applied once in the new `bass_mode_peak_db()`, which the filter, the engine status (`bass_peak_db`), the CLI toast and `--list-bass-modes` all read, so no surface can report the untrimmed number. The curve still ignores the trim's effect on tone: 50 Hz stays 0.24 dB under the peak, 30 Hz stays 5.7 dB down, and the boost is still gone by 500 Hz.
  - **Verified.** `--list-bass-modes` now prints `+5.3 dB` and `+13.7 dB`, and the designed FIR measures 45 Hz **+13.7 dB**, 50 Hz +13.4, 30 Hz +8.0, 100 Hz +5.0, 250 Hz +0.1, 1 kHz 0.0 in High — the same curve, 3 dB lower. The trim measures as **half power** (0.4997 for Medium, 0.5004 for High) on the dial's own numbers. `tests.test_bass_boost` passes 36 of 37; the one failure is the pre-existing row-offset assertion documented in `PI_REVIEW_PACKET.md`, which fails on a clean checkout too.

### Fixed
- **Square Visualizer corners showed through the MP Liquid Player's rounded window** (`scripts/mp_liquid_visualizer_metal.py`, `scripts/visualizer_window_shape.py`, `tests/test_visualizer_window_shape.py`):
  - **Symptom:** the window behind the MP Liquid Player had square corners, so the player's curved 36 px silhouette showed the visualizer's own corners poking out, while a window opened with **Z** looked correct.
  - **Cause, macOS:** the standalone visualizer runs on **Metal**, and that backend was the only one that never clipped its window. `radius_px = 36` was declared but never used, and both the `NSWindow` and the `CAMetalLayer` were set **opaque** (`setOpaque: 1`), so the window was a full rectangle painting solid black where the player had cut its corners away. The Vulkan and OpenGL backends already applied `RoundedWindowShaper`; Metal had no shaper at all, which was added after the corner-shaping commit and so never picked it up. The Metal renderer now owns a shaper, applies it on open and on every poll, and creates its window non-opaque so the cut-aways are genuinely transparent. Fullscreen and screensaver windows stay rectangular, exactly as the other two backends do.
  - **Cause, Linux:** the mask was built from the size the caller *believed* the window had. A bonded companion is force-resized immediately after the player publishes a new size, and the window manager can land on a different size than requested, which left a mask for one rectangle on a window of another. The shaper now asks X (`XGetGeometry`) for the window's real size and keys its cache on that, so a stale mask can no longer be treated as already applied. `XGetGeometry` on a just-destroyed window raises `BadWindow`, which kills the process through Xlib's default handler, so the shaper installs the same silent error handler the glass renderers use.
  - **Verified:** the live X11 masks for the player and its companion both measure the same extent as their windows (`1574x1054`) over `44` scanline rectangles with the first row starting at `x=36` — identical silhouettes. Applying the shaper with a deliberately wrong `800x600` request now still produces the correct `1574x1054` mask, and a dead window id returns "unknown" instead of aborting the process. `tests/test_visualizer_window_shape.py` grows to 18 tests, including the Metal shaper, the non-opaque layer, the real-geometry override and the dead-window probe. The 301-test visualizer suite passes.
- **The tree could not be imported on Ubuntu 20.04, the oldest platform it claims to support** (`scripts/mp_logging.py`, `scripts/mp_activity_tracker.py`, `scripts/mp_tracklist_analyzer.py`, `scripts/get_mp_audio_player_file.py`, `scripts/wan2gp_*.py`, `scripts/send_promo_email.py`, `send_promo_email.py`, `tests/test_python38_compatibility.py`, `MP_Auto_Tester_Flash.sh`):
  - **Symptom, found by running the suite on a real Ubuntu 20.04.6 LTS host:** 107 test modules errored at import and the console manager, the player and the tracklist viewer could not start. Every one traced back to the language level, not to logic.
  - **Two syntax levels were in use that 3.8 does not have.** PEP 585 builtin generics (`_RECENT: dict[tuple, list] = {}`) and PEP 604 unions (`def f(x: Path | None)`) are evaluated at import time on 3.8 and raise `TypeError: 'type' object is not subscriptable`; PEP 701 backslashes inside an f-string expression (`f"{f'NOTE:\n{msg}' if msg else ''}"`) raise `SyntaxError`. `scripts/mp_dependencies.py` accepts a 3.8 interpreter (`_works(..., minimum=(3, 8))`), and Ubuntu 20.04 ships **3.8.10** as its system Python, so this was a supported platform that could not run the product at all.
  - **Fixed by** adding `from __future__ import annotations` to the thirteen modules that annotate with newer generics — which defers every annotation to a string with no runtime change — and by hoisting the four backslash f-strings in `send_promo_email.py` onto their own lines. Verified against a real CPython 3.8.20 and on the Ubuntu host itself: every `.py` in the tree now parses and the four previously-crashing modules import.
  - **`tests/test_python38_compatibility.py` stops it coming back.** It parses every shipped file with `feature_version=(3, 8)` and separately fails any module that annotates with `dict[...]`/`list[...]`/`X | None` without the future import. It needs no 3.8 interpreter, so it runs in the normal suite.
  - **Ubuntu 20.04 is now a first-class test target** (`MP_UBUNTU_HOST`, alias `ubuntu`, address configurable like the other beds). It is the only target that catches this class of bug, so `tests/test_mp_auto_tester.py` pins it.
  - **Measured on the real host:** before the fix, 1,859 tests ran with 107 import errors; after, **2,574 run, 10 failed, 5 errored, 116 skipped**. The 15 remaining are all hardware or packaging limits of that machine — no Vulkan device (`llvmpipe` software GL only), no ollama, no Piper voice models, and a GDM theme script that only `install.sh` creates. The console manager boots to its menu and the Liquid Glass host imports and builds its player command there.
- **MP Reporter missed OpenCode sessions that had been resumed after finishing a task** (`scripts/mp_opencode_progress_reporter.py`, `tests/test_mp_opencode_progress_reporter.py`):
  - **Symptom:** with six OpenCode sessions genuinely running, the reporter's database fallback disagreed with what OpenCode itself reported. It looked like a hard limit of four sessions; there is no such limit.
  - **Cause:** the reporter treated `idle_outcome` as proof a session was finished. OpenCode writes that value when a turn goes idle and **leaves it in place when the session starts another turn**, so a session given more work after completing a task still carried `outcome = succeeded` while it was busy again. The `(idle_outcome IS NULL OR idle_outcome = '')` clause then filtered it out. Here, 4 of the 6 running sessions were hidden this way.
  - **Fixed by** dropping that clause and keeping only `time_busy IS NOT NULL AND (time_idle IS NULL OR time_busy > time_idle)`, which is exactly the rule OpenCode's own `/api/session/active` endpoint applies. Verified against the live service: the reporter's database fallback and the API now return the same six session ids.
  - **The audio briefing also stopped at six sessions** in `build_speech_script`, while the text report listed up to `OPENCODE_PROGRESS_MAX_SESSIONS` (12). It now names the same sessions as the email, and the remainder is still summarised by count.
- **MP Liquid Player refused to open after its intro movie, saying "already open 4 times" when nothing was on screen** (`scripts/mp_instance_limit.py`, `tests/test_instance_limit.py`):
  - **Symptom:** the first launch of a session played `Exit_MP_Video.mp4`, and when the movie ended no player window appeared. The launch log had `MP Liquid Player is already open 4 times. Close a window first (4 is the maximum).` while the desktop was empty, so there was nothing to close.
  - **Cause:** `role_from_cmdline()` matched the host file name against **every** token of every process's command line. An editor with `MP_Audio_Player_Liquid_Glass.py` open, a `tail -f` of a log named after it, or any resident shell whose arguments merely mentioned the file were each counted as an open player window. Four such processes filled the four slots, and the real host — which claims the last slot *after* the movie — was refused. Reproduced here: four `bash -c '... # MP_Audio_Player_Liquid_Glass.py'` shells gave `count player = 5`, `claim` returned 3, and the host exited with the limit message while no window existed.
  - **Only a real run counts now.** `_host_script_index()` requires the host to be the program a Python interpreter actually runs (the first non-flag argument, or the shebang path itself), so `vim`, `less`, `tail`, `grep`, `pgrep` and `bash -c` lines are ignored. `python3 -c '...'` is a program, not a script path, so its argument text is ignored too.
  - **Launcher spellings still count.** `_skip_wrappers()` steps over `env`, `nice`, `nohup`, `setsid`, `stdbuf`, `timeout`, `command` and `exec`, including their value flags (`env -u LINES`, `nice -n 5`) and `env`'s `KEY=VALUE` assignments, and versioned interpreters (`python3.14`) are accepted, so the cap still sees every window a launcher opens. Verified across 30 command-line shapes.
  - **Why the warning was invisible.** The host printed the message to stderr, and a shortcut launch discards it, so the only evidence was the silent no-window launch. Every Liquid Glass host now reports a refusal through the shared `notify_limit()`, so the limit also appears as a desktop notification (`notify-send` on Linux, `osascript` on macOS) instead of failing silently.
- **The OpenGL transparency bar was never drawn, and the window printed a traceback on every frame** (`MP_Audio_Player_Liquid_Glass_OpenGL.py`, `tests/test_liquid_glass_transparency_slider.py`):
  - **Symptom:** on the OpenGL backend the bottom transparency bar was missing, and the launch log filled with `Slider draw error: 'CustomGlassRenderer' object has no attribute '_slider_strip'` — one line per frame for the life of the window (39 identical lines in a 10-second run here).
  - **Cause:** moving the bar from a fixed 22 px offset to `self._slider_strip()` pointed the renderer at a method that only exists on `GlassHost`. The call raised before any GL work, so the bar had never been drawn since that change.
  - **The renderer answers for itself now.** `CustomGlassRenderer` keeps `_slider_strip_px` (starting at the classic `SLIDER_STRIP`) and `_slider_strip()` returns it, falling back to the classic strip if the value is unusable. `_place_terminal()` publishes the strip the host actually reserved, so MP Gui and the tracklist viewer keep 44 px while the player keeps its one-text-row-shorter strip, and the bar stays centred in the space that was really freed. Verified: a 10-second OpenGL run now reports 0 slider errors and 0 `AttributeError`s, with the glass window mapped.
- **TTY Audio Mode showed `CLI Mode` and stayed half-off in an SSH session** (`Mix_Archive_Manager.sh`, `scripts/show_help.sh`, `README.md`, `tests/test_header_cli_mode.py`, `tests/test_tty_desktop_switch.py`):
  - **Symptom:** with **MP Mix Manager TTY Audio Mode** enabled on a remote Fedora host over SSH (`Mix_Archive_Manager.sh` on `192.0.2.13`), the header still read **CLI Mode** instead of **TTY CLI Mode**. The mode line under the title that names the player also looked like a normal desktop session.
  - **The header named the mode from the device path only.** `render_main_header` chose `TTY CLI Mode` for `/dev/ttyN`, `/dev/console`, or `MP_TTY_KIOSK=1`, and otherwise `CLI Mode` whenever stdin or stdout was a terminal. An SSH login owns a **pts**, never a virtual console, so a session that was genuinely in TTY Audio Mode fell through to the desktop label. The mode is now checked first: `mp_tty_audio_mode_active` (the env flag *or* `~/.config/mix-manager/tty_audio_mode`) forces **TTY CLI Mode** on any terminal, and the device path is only the fallback for sessions that are not in the mode. A real console and the TTY kiosk keep their existing label, and MP Gui still shows none.
  - **Option 37 tried to switch the machine, not the login.** From SSH, `_mp_tty_switch_target` saw no `DISPLAY`/`WAYLAND_DISPLAY` and either reported `here` or, with X11 forwarding, ran the desktop switch against the seated user's Plasma session.
