It now detects an SSH login (`SSH_CONNECTION`, `SSH_CLIENT`, or an `SSH_TTY` device) and a new `session` target, which turns the mode on for that login only: the terminal player, the closed desktop windows and the console restore all apply, but the flag file is **not** written, so the seated Plasma session and its login screen are left alone. `MP_TTY_SESSION_ONLY=1` forces the same target without an SSH login.
  - **The mode is now real over SSH, not just a label.** Verified on a pty session: `switch_target=session`, `active=yes`, `MP_MIX_MANAGER_TTY_MODE=1`, the flag file left absent, `mp_tty_skip_desktop` refusing MP Gui, and `play_audio_file` resolving `mp_audio_player` instead of Liquid Glass. `bash -n` passes, and `tests.test_header_cli_mode` (11 tests) plus `tests.test_tty_desktop_switch` (16 tests) pass.
- **The Metal visualizer's fullscreen picture is no longer blocky** (`scripts/mp_liquid_visualizer_metal.py`, `tests/test_mp_metal_visualizer.py`):
  - **Symptom:** on macOS, pressing **Z** (or `--fullscreen`) filled the display with a visibly blocky picture while the windowed mode looked fine. The renderer capped its raymarch at a **768-pixel long edge** regardless of window size, so fullscreen marched 768x432 and stretched that over a 1920x1080 panel -- a 2.5x upscale through a *bilinear* sampler. It held 60 fps precisely because it was shading a sixth of the pixels the display shows.
  - **Fullscreen now marches from a real 1920x1080 target.** A new `fullscreen_march_size()` fits the display's own aspect ratio into a 1080p box (exactly 1920x1080 on 16:9, 1728x1080 on 16:10, 1920x804 on ultrawide) and never upscales a smaller panel. The windowed cap is untouched, so the 1094x734 window behaves exactly as before.
  - **The remaining scale is bicubic, not bilinear.** The upscale pass is now a Catmull-Rom (16-tap) reconstruction. Measured on the M2, a full 1920x1080 raymarch at the default 64 steps costs about **144 ms/frame (7 fps)** -- sharp but not playable -- so fullscreen has its own adaptive controller: it starts at 1920x1080, targets **30 fps** (a slowly moving scene does not need 60), and stops at a floor of 0.65 (1248x702, still 1.6x the old 768 cap). A faster Mac settles much higher; this M2 settles around 1600x900.
  - **`MP_METAL_FULLSCREEN_LONG_EDGE`** overrides the fullscreen ceiling and **`MP_METAL_FULLSCREEN_ADAPTIVE=0`** pins it at exactly 1920x1080. Both are documented alongside the existing `MP_METAL_LONG_EDGE`.
  - Verified on the M2: `--self-test` still passes, the windowed march is still 653x438 at 60 fps, and a fullscreen capture is a real 1920x1080 frame.

### Added
- **The MP Liquid Player's timeline and DJ pitch track are clickable** (`MP_Audio_Player.py`, `tests/test_timeline_and_pitch_clicks.py`):  - **What changed.** Clicking anywhere on the progress track seeks the playing mix to that point and keeps it playing — it is "play from here", not a pause. Clicking anywhere on the `🎚 DJ PITCH` track moves the pitch knob to that percent, from **-20 %** at the far left through the **0 %** detent to **+20 %** at the far right. Pressing and dragging either one scrubs it, and the drag keeps working after the pointer leaves that row.
  - **Why it was needed.** Both bars were paint-only. The timeline redrew every frame with the position, but nothing hit-tested it, so a click on the bar a user reads as a seek control did nothing. The pitch bar had the same problem in the other direction: the only way to move it was `+`/`-`, one percent at a time.
  - **Exact geometry, not a guess.** `render_horizontal_pitch_bar` was split into `pitch_bar_parts`, which returns the prefix, track and suffix separately, so the player knows the precise column where the -20 % … +20 % track begins after the narrow-window shrink. The timeline span is measured from the painted position label the same way. Both spans are inclusive 1-based column pairs, matching SGR mouse columns.
  - **The rows survive a trimmed frame.** A short console drops the middle band in `_fit_frame`, which used to move every row after it while the recorded click rows kept their old numbers. `_fit_frame_plan` and `_fit_frame_row` are now the single source of truth for that mapping, and `_present` remaps every recorded target — playlist rows, the Favorites / Visualizer / Bass chips, the export label, the timeline and the pitch track — through it. A target whose row is dropped is cleared rather than left pointing at whatever now occupies that line.
  - **The click paints on the next frame.** A timeline or pitch click goes to the player daemon over IPC, and the first status that comes back can still be the pre-click one. `_apply_pending_status` overlays the clicked value until the daemon echoes it back (or for two seconds), so the knob and the clock never snap back for a moment. `AudioEngine.seek(force=True)` and `set_pitch` also force a state write so a paused player repaints immediately.
  - **Mini keeps working.** Mini draws its own compact progress bar, so it carries its own seek target; Micro draws no bar and exposes none. 28 tests cover the geometry, both ends of each track, drag and hover, the trim remap, and the untouched click paths.
- **A Mac can mount its Linux archives with one command** (`scripts/mount_mix_archives.sh`, `config.env.example`):
  - **The problem it solves.** A Mac has no local `MIX_ARCHIVE`, so it either saw nothing or was pointed at volumes that only existed while a Finder window happened to be open. `bash scripts/mount_mix_archives.sh --install` mounts each configured share under `~/MIX_ARCHIVE_MOUNTS` and installs a LaunchAgent (`com.mplanetarian.mp-mix-archives`) that remounts a dropped share every five minutes, so the library is intact at login and after a network blip.
  - **Three modes, all bounded.** `--status` prints each share, its host and its live mix count; `--mount` (the default) mounts what is missing; `--uninstall` removes the agent and leaves the mounts alone. Every filesystem probe and the `mount_smbfs` call itself run under `timeout`, because a powered-off workstation leaves `mount_smbfs` blocked in the kernel and a wedged mountpoint hangs any process that touches it — one dead share is skipped rather than stalling the login.
  - **The password is not in the script.** It comes from `MIX_ARCHIVE_SMB_PASSWORD` when you set one, otherwise from the login keychain (`security find-internet-password`). Shares and the SMB login are configured in `config.env` (`MIX_ARCHIVE_SMB_SHARES`, `MIX_ARCHIVE_SMB_USER`), with this suite's own three archive hosts as the default.
  - **Verified on the Mac mini:** all three shares mount and every folder reads — `192.0.2.11` WD BLACK (277 mixes), `192.0.2.13` `512GB_BKP_004` (139) and `1TB_BKP_005` (559). `python3 MP_Audio_Player.py --list-mixes` reports **975 mixes across exactly those three roots**, and the login agent remounted a share dropped during the test.
- **The installer can set up all four MP apps or only the ones you want** (`install.sh`, `scripts/mp_app_selection.py`, `scripts/install_desktop_shortcuts.py`, `scripts/mp_system_tray.py`, `scripts/mp_suite_control.py`, `scripts/macos_menu_bar.swift`, `scripts/windows_tray.ps1`, `scripts/windows_user_integration.ps1`, `scripts/install_windows_user.sh`, `scripts/reinstall_mp_manager.sh`, `tests/test_app_selection.py`):
  - **Step 9 is a new prompt.** It offers **all four**, a single app (**MP Mix Manager**, **MP Gui**, **MP Liquid Player** or **MP Liquid Visualizer**), or a multi-pick of any combination. `--apps LIST` answers it without a prompt (`./install.sh --apps gui`, `--apps gui,liquid`, `--apps all`), a non-interactive run takes all four, and a bad value is treated as all four rather than an empty install.
  - **The choice is stored once** in `~/.config/mix-manager/installed_apps` as one line of component ids (`manager gui liquid visualizer`). `scripts/mp_app_selection.py` owns the parser, the spellings (`gui`, `MP Gui`, `mp-gui`, `player`, …) and the writer, so the installer, the shortcuts and every menu agree.
  - **The Desktop and the application menu carry only the selected apps.** `install_desktop_shortcuts.py` grew `--components`, `selected_names()` and `_remove_deselected()`: writing a narrower selection also deletes the shortcuts of the apps it dropped, so switching to "only MP Gui" does not strand three dead icons. `desktop_shortcuts_present()` and `ensure_installed()` only require the selected shortcuts, so the first-run reward stops re-adding the rest.
  - **The shared tray menu is filtered.** Qt, the macOS menu bar and the PowerShell tray only offer **Launch** entries for the installed apps; **Start / Stop MP Audio Player (Web)** and **Close MP Mix Manager (Closes everything)** always stay, because they are not tied to one app. A direct `--action` for an app that was not installed now refuses instead of spawning it.
  - **The Web Companion launch pad follows too.** `app_definitions()` and the `/api/status` payload only list the installed apps, and `launch_app()` returns `not_installed` for the rest, so the phone cannot open a window the desktop never installed.
  - **A reinstall keeps the selection** (it is staged with `backups` and `exports`), and the installer's closing banner lists exactly what was set up plus the command that changes it later.
  - **Windows and macOS are wired the same way**: `-Components` reaches `windows_user_integration.ps1`, which maps ids to shortcut names and deletes the unselected links, and the macOS menu-bar agent asks `mp_app_selection.py --enabled <id>` before it adds an item.

### Changed
- **The tray's web entry is now named after the player it starts** (`scripts/mp_system_tray.py`, `scripts/mp_web_service.py`, `scripts/mix_web_companion.py`, `scripts/macos_menu_bar.swift`, `scripts/show_help.sh`, `README.md`, `tests/test_web_audio_tray.py`, `tests/test_mp_system_tray.py`):
  - The shared tray menu entry reads **Start / Stop MP Audio Player (Web)** instead of *Start / Stop MP Web Audio*, and the state line follows it: **Stop MP Audio Player (Web)** while the companion is serving, **Start MP Audio Player (Web)** while it is not. The action id (`web-audio`) and the module API are unchanged, so nothing else had to move.
  - The same name is used wherever the entry is spoken about: the macOS menu-bar agent, the tray's unsupported-platform message, `show_help.sh`, and the README's tray section.
  - The restart notice a phone sees is now **MP Audio Player (Web) has been restarted** (and **MP Audio Player (Web) is restarting** when the connection drops before the notice arrives), matching the page's own **MP Audio Player (Web)** title.
- **The MP Visualizer stops re-raymarching frames nobody can see** (`scripts/mp_liquid_visualizer_vulkan.py`, `scripts/liquid_player_visualizer.py`, `scripts/mp_liquid_visualizer_metal.py`, `tests/test_visualizer_power.py`):
  - **A paused or stopped player is drawn at 20 FPS, not the display rate.** The scene only creeps forward while audio is paused (`march_advance`), so the extra frames were identical pixels at full GPU cost. All three backends share the same pacing rule; a playing window is untouched and still runs at the display's refresh rate. Measured on an RTX 3080 at 1080p: 710 → 241 frames per 12 s while paused, with the card's power over background falling from about 170 W to about 77 W. `MP_VISUALIZER_IDLE_FPS` overrides the idle rate; `0` disables the reduction, and the value is clamped to 5–60.
  - **A minimized or hidden window draws nothing at all.** Each backend's `draw()` returns before encoding when the window is iconified or hidden, and the loop polls for it coming back four times a second. Waking it resumes the full rate immediately. The screensaver, F10/F11 spans and the `--self-test` capture are unaffected (the capture path always draws). Measured: 0 frames while hidden, against about 700 for the same 12 s window before.
  - **A 4K fullscreen surface is raymarched at 1080p and scaled up on every backend.** The Vulkan path only capped the swapchain in one branch when the panel itself reported 4K, so a compositor that answered the fullscreen request with a 2160p surface marched 3840×2160; the OpenGL path had no cap at all. Both now share `fullscreen_render_cap`, and the Vulkan swapchain is created at the size the march draws, so a 4K surface can no longer recreate the swapchain every frame. A windowed 4K surface is still drawn natively.
  - **`MP_VISUALIZER_GL_LONG_EDGE` and `MP_VISUALIZER_GL_MARCH_STEPS` now apply on desktop Linux too.** Both were silently macOS/Pi-only in the OpenGL layer, so the documented overrides did nothing on a desktop. Defaults are unchanged: desktop Linux still marches at full resolution and 84 steps.

### Fixed
- **Left/Right in the MP Liquid Player no longer switch MP Visualizer presets** (`MP_Audio_Player.py`, `tests/test_visualizer_arrow_key_presets.py`):
  - **Symptom:** while the MP Visualizer was on behind the player — the state you are in when a visualizer video is playing — the arrow keys stepped through the preset library instead of seeking. Left/Right are the player's ±10 s seek in every other state, so the same key did two unrelated things depending on the theme you happened to be watching.
  - **The player now always seeks.** `_process_key` sends `seek` for `\x1b[C`/`\x1b[D` and the application-cursor forms `\x1bOC`/`\x1bOD` unconditionally; the `_step_mp_visualizer_preset()` helper that consumed the keys is gone.
  - **Preset stepping stays where it belongs.** Left/Right, `+`/`-` and `1`–`9` still step the whole 13-preset library **inside the MP Visualizer window**, on Vulkan, OpenGL and Metal. The player still selects a theme with its own `0`–`9` keys and toggles it with `Shift+V`.
  - The player header subtitle and the Visualization button hint no longer advertise `[←/→] preset`, and the README rows for the player's key map now say the arrows seek in every state. `tests/test_visualizer_arrow_key_presets.py` pins the new contract: arrows seek with the visualizer on *and* off, and a player arrow never writes `preset_id` to the shared state.
- **Two scripts were committed as symlinks into a private directory, so a clone was broken** (`scripts/mp_daily_email_reporter.py`, `scripts/mp_email_manager.sh`, `tests/test_no_escaping_symlinks.py`):
  - Both files were git-tracked as **mode 120000 (symlinks)** pointing at `/var/home/mplanetarian/MP_REPORTER/...`. A fresh clone therefore received **dangling links**: importing the reporter raised `FileNotFoundError` anywhere but this workstation, and the Windows portability fixes made to it could never be committed, because the blob git stored was only the link text.
  - Both are now **real files** in the tree (mode 100755). `mp_email_manager.sh` prefers the reporter that ships beside it and keeps `~/MP_REPORTER` as a fallback.
  - **A shared `~/MP_REPORTER` installation keeps working exactly as before.** With the file as a symlink, `Path(__file__).resolve()` followed the link, so `BASE_DIR` meant `~/MP_REPORTER` — which is where the SMTP config, the **"one email per day" state** and every generated report live. Making the file real would have silently moved all of that into the app tree and **reset the daily limit and forgotten 20 sent reports**. New `_resolve_base_dir()` prefers `$MP_REPORTER_DIR`, then an existing `~/MP_REPORTER` that holds `email_config.env` or `data/email_state.json`, and otherwise the tree the file lives in.
  - Verified on this workstation: `BASE_DIR` still resolves to `~/MP_REPORTER`, `--dry-run` still builds the unified PDF (15 pages), the Piper voice briefing and the 12 tester screenshots, and `status` still reports **20 emails sent** and the 1-per-day limit. Verified on a clean `git checkout-index` clone: real files, **no dangling links anywhere**, imports with `BASE_DIR` pointing at the clone, 62 tests OK.
  - `tests/test_no_escaping_symlinks.py` now fails the build if any tracked symlink points outside the checkout, or if either file becomes a symlink again.
- **A `reportlab`-less machine could not import the reporter at all** (`scripts/mp_daily_email_reporter.py`): the earlier optional-dependency fix guarded the `import` but not `class NumberedCanvas(canvas.Canvas)`, which is evaluated at module level — with reportlab absent `canvas` is `None`, so the class statement raised `AttributeError: 'NoneType' object has no attribute 'Canvas'`. The class is now declared only when reportlab is available (`NumberedCanvas = None` otherwise), and both paths are tested.
- **The last two MP Visualizer presets render in 3D instead of as a flat 2D pass** (`shaders/mp_liquid_visualizer.frag`, `shaders/presets/p*.frag.spv`, `shaders/presets/manifest.json`, `shaders/mp_liquid_visualizer.frag.spv`, `scripts/liquid_player_visualizer.py`, `tests/test_sof_dynamic_waves.py`, `tests/test_visualizer_preset_override.py`):
  - **Symptom:** presets 12 and 13, *Stream of Frequency — Dynamic Waves* and *Dynamic Waves P2*, were the only presets in the library with no 3D geometry. Both were a full-frame colour pass on a paper-white page: `main()` returned early with `sofHorizontalRiver(screenUv, t)` / `sofVerticalRiver(screenUv, t)`, and `mapScene` had no branch for them at all, so nothing was ever raymarched.
  - **They are raymarched scenes now.** `mapScene` dispatches ids 11 and 12 to `sofHorizontalRiver(p)` and `sofVerticalRiver(p)`, which return `vec2(distance, material)` over world space like every other preset, and the early return from `main()` is gone. Both run the shared march, the volumetric glow accumulation and their own material set, against their own deep-void background in `renderCosmicBackground`.
  - **The horizontal river is a vault of twelve glass ribbons.** The stack converges with perspective toward the far end (`taper`), each ribbon weaves toward and away from the camera in Z, and two thin guide rails frame it. The ribbon bodies stay dark so the accent rim and the specular key carry their depth.
  - **The vertical river is a cylinder of glyph columns.** Each column is a spine on the cylinder wall plus blocks repeated along its own fall speed, framed on the cylinder's tangent and radial axes so the rain reads as a ring rather than a flat curtain. Three current rings climb it on the beat.
  - **The PID burst is geometry, not an overlay.** `sofPidBursts` now returns a distance field, so the travelling front's motes are occluded by the river and lit by the same key as everything else, instead of being alpha-composited over a page.
  - **The pair keeps its rainbow, keyed off world space.** `sofSpectrum` is the Stream of Frequency hue ramp; `sofSurfaceHue` samples it down the ribbon stack or around the glyph cylinder, so each ribbon keeps one stable hue and the river no longer reads as a single flat colour. The per-fragment cover sampling is gone, which is what made the first cut look like camouflage painted onto a tube.
  - **Two camera bugs surfaced with the change.** `isChromaticStorm` was `u.preset.x > 9.5`, which also matches 11 and 12, so the storm's per-sequence camera overwrote the river framing and pushed both rivers to a near-axial view; it is now `> 9.5 && < 10.5`. The rivers also have their own marker (a longer far plane, `tMax = 18.0`, because they run wider than a vessel).
  - **Presets 12 and 13 were unreachable on the OpenGL backend.** Only the Vulkan renderer set `_preset_pinned`, so a `--preset 12` window on OpenGL took the player's saved preset on the first bond poll — the same class of bug that `test_visualizer_preset_override` pins for Vulkan. `LiquidVisualizerOpenGLRenderer.__init__` now sets the pin, with the same `random` exemption.
  - **Shipped SPIR-V rebuilt.** All thirteen `shaders/presets/p*.frag.spv` modules and the unfolded `shaders/mp_liquid_visualizer.frag.spv` were rebuilt from the new source, and `shaders/presets/manifest.json` now carries the new digest (`a5c87822724083f8`, 64 steps). Without that the visualizer would have kept loading the previous module — the stale-manifest failure mode the warning in `vulkan_preset_spirv` exists to catch.
  - **Verified on both backends.** `--preset 12` and `--preset 13` render the two rivers as distinct 3D scenes on Vulkan and on OpenGL, and preset 10 (*Resonant Fluid Dynamics*) is byte-for-byte unmoved.

### Added
- **The first MP Liquid Player window plays `Exit_MP_Video.mp4` as an intro** (`scripts/exit_video.py`, `scripts/display_loader_video.sh`, `scripts/mp_instance_limit.py`, the four Liquid Glass hosts, `config.env.example`, `tests/test_player_intro_video.py`):
  - **The movie is on screen before the window is.** `PlayerIntro.play()` is called after the four-window slot has been claimed and **before** the host builds its glass, so the first launch of a session shows the 10 s movie first and then the player, instead of a bare desktop while the window initialises. It uses the same borderless always-on-top player as the startup logo and the exit movie (`display_loader_video.sh --intro`), so `ffplay` / `mpv` / VLC / the Swift helper are the same on every platform, and Esc, Space, Enter and `q` still skip it where a terminal is attached.
  - **Only the first of the four windows is introduced.** MP Liquid Player allows four windows; the second, third and fourth open immediately, so only the first launch ever waits. The election is `mp_instance_limit.instance_count("player", exclude_pid=<this process>)`, which means a launcher's own pre-exec claim is not mistaken for a sibling window, and a shortcut pressed while the intro is still playing is already counted — so it opens straight into its window and never queues a second movie.
  - **Once per session, not once per install.** The decision is live-window state only; no marker is written that would make this a first-run event. Close every window, launch MP Liquid again, and the movie plays again.
  - **The movie runs before the companion visualizer too.** `play_player_intro()` is called before `ensure_random_visualizer_started()`, so the backdrop window cannot appear over or beside the movie.
  - **`instance_count()` can now exclude a process from both halves of the count.** It previously dropped the excluded pid only from the live-host set, leaving that pid's pending launch claim in the total, which made any host that inherited its launcher's claim look like a second window.
  - **MP Gui, the tracklist viewer, `--embed-check`, `--self-test`, VR Mode, benchmark and soak runs stay silent**, so startup timing measurements are not offset by a movie, a movie never covers the VR canvas, and a copied host without `scripts/exit_video.py` keeps working through a no-op stub.
  - **Two new settings**, `SHOW_PLAYER_INTRO_VIDEO` (default `true`) and `PLAYER_INTRO_VIDEO_MAX_SECONDS` (default `90`), read from `config.env` **without sourcing it** — an environment variable of the same name wins. Both are documented in `config.env.example`. `MP_NO_PLAYER_INTRO=1` skips it for one launch, which is what the performance harness now passes.
  - **A stuck player cannot hang the launch:** the intro is bounded by the **movie's own length plus a short grace**, not by the configured ceiling. `ffplay -autoexit` sometimes never exits after a finished movie (a stalled PipeWire/Bluetooth sink), and sometimes drops out after a second; waiting for `PLAYER_INTRO_VIDEO_MAX_SECONDS` then held the window back for **90 s after a 40 s movie**, which looked exactly like "the player never loads". Measured before and after on this machine: a hung player delayed the window by 90 s+, now about 50 s, and a healthy movie opens the player at ~43 s. The same bound now applies to the exit movie. The `G` shortcut is muted during the intro, since the user is waiting for a player that has not opened yet.
- **The console menu can span two or three displays, one section per screen** (`Mix_Archive_Manager.sh`, `config.env.example`, `tests/test_multiscreen_layout.py`):
  - **Triple screen** puts `SECTION 1` on the left display, `SECTION 2` on the middle display and `SECTION 3` on the right display. **Dual screen** keeps `SECTION 1` on the left and merges every later section on the right. One display is unchanged: the classic single column, byte for byte.
  - **Any resolution works as long as the displays agree.** Three 1920 panels, two 2560 panels or three 4K panels all lay out; the split is computed from each panel's pixel width and the console's own column count, so font size and HiDPI scaling do not skew it. A group of matching displays wins over a wider odd one out, so three 1920 monitors beside a 4K TV still use the three monitors and leave the TV alone.
  - **The layout only engages when the window really spans the displays.** The console's text-area size is read from `TIOCGWINSZ` (which cannot steal a keystroke, unlike a CSI 14t query, kept only as a fallback) and the layout requires the window to cover at least 90 % of the desktop width. Squeezing two panels into one 1920 display is never allowed.
  - **The console is stretched across the displays on launch.** `mp_screen_span_console` wins on Wayland through KWin scripting and falls back to `wmctrl` / `xdotool` on X11. It uses the displays' own height, not Konsole's small windowed height, because a 17-row console cannot hold three menu columns. Plasma 6 answers `loadScript` with `0`, which is a valid script slot, so that reply is no longer mistaken for a failure.
  - **Navigation, the mouse, the breathing highlight and paging all follow the panels.** Arrow keys step through the flattened option order and repaint only the row that changed; a click is resolved to the row *and* the panel under the cursor; an up-arrow from the first option wraps to the last option of the right-hand panel.
  - **Short terminals degrade instead of breaking.** If the full banner will not fit beside three columns, the banner is trimmed to `MP_SCREEN_HEADER_LINES` rows (default 6); if the panels still do not fit, the menu falls back to the classic single column. Submenus with two section groups on a three-screen desktop spread their two panels evenly instead of hugging the left edge.
  - **Option 31 → Multi-Screen Console Layout** picks Automatic, Single, Dual or Triple, toggles console spanning, and reports the detected display widths and the active column split. Selections are saved to `config.env` as `MP_SCREEN_LAYOUT` / `MP_SCREEN_ALIGN_TERMINAL`.
  - **Verified on the real rig:** a Konsole stretched to 5760 × 1200 across three 1920 × 1200 panels renders the shipped main menu as 302 columns, with `SECTION 1` starting at column 8, `SECTION 2` at 110 and `SECTION 3` at 210, every painted row 302 columns wide, and the frame stable across every repaint.

### Fixed
- **Three defects that stopped the multi-screen layout from ever appearing** (`Mix_Archive_Manager.sh`, `tests/test_multiscreen_layout.py`):
  - **The startup span could never run.** `_MS_N` served as both "panels the displays allow" and "panels in use". The span check set `_MS_N=1` when the window was too narrow, and the startup code only asked for a span `if _MS_N >= 2`, so the console was never stretched and the layout stayed single column forever. The two meanings are now separate: `_MS_WANT` is what the displays support, `_MS_N` is what is actually drawn. Startup spans on `_MS_WANT`, retries four times while the window manager maps the new window, and only then re-evaluates.
  - **`local` at the script's top level silently aborted the whole block.** The span loop used `local _span_try`, which is only valid inside a function. Bash printed "can only be used in a function" and returned 1, so nothing after it ran. The loop now uses a plain global.
  - **`ensure_manager_windowed` un-maximised the spanned window.** It sends `CSI 10;0t` / `CSI 9;0t` (the xterm restore-window escape) on every menu entry for the macOS tab-title workaround. On Linux those escapes restore the pre-maximised geometry, so the console snapped back to Konsole's default 90 × 20 two frames after the layout engaged: the first frames were 301 columns, then everything collapsed to 99. The escape is now skipped whenever a spanning layout is active, and `mp_screen_span_console` owns the geometry.
  - **The frame could be taller than the terminal, scrolling its own header away.** The frame is `header + tallest panel + 3` rows; the budget reserved 5 and the banner trim used `head -n` on a string holding literal `\n` escapes, which is one shell line, so the trim returned a single empty row and the real banner was never shortened. The header is now expanded before trimming, the cap shrinks a row at a time until the panels fit, and a hard guard in the renderer drops blank separator rows (never option rows or the status line) if a frame would still overflow. A 29-row console now shows three full columns with the header still at the top.
  - **Window matching is exact.** The KWin script now matches Konsole by the manager's own parent PID instead of guessing from a caption the application may not have set yet, with the caption as a fallback only.


### Added
- **Game controllers drive the Manager menus** (`scripts/gamepad_bridge.py`, `Mix_Archive_Manager.sh`, `config.env.example`)
  - Xbox, PlayStation (DualShock 4 and DualSense), Nintendo Switch Pro and Steam controllers work on Linux. `scripts/gamepad_bridge.py` reads each pad through evdev and sends the key names the keyboard already produces (`UP`, `DOWN`, `LEFT`, `RIGHT`, `ENTER`, `ESC`, `SPACE`, `PAGE_UP`, `PAGE_DOWN`, `CHAR:[`, `CHAR:n`), so every menu takes a pad without new code.
  - **Controls:** the D-pad or left stick moves and repeats while held. A / Cross / Nintendo A selects. B / Circle / Nintendo B goes back. X / Square / Nintendo Y plays or pauses. LB and RB page up and down. LT and RT (ZL and ZR on a Switch pad) step to the previous and next track.
  - **Hot-plug:** pads can be switched on, plugged in or removed while the Manager runs. The helper rescans every two seconds, and it exits with the Manager.
  - **Studio, Hardware & Volume → Game Controllers** lists the connected pads and runs a live button test that shows the key each input sends.
  - **A lit pad that never appears usually has no kernel driver.** A Switch Pro Controller and a Steam Controller only reach Linux once the kernel binds a driver to them. Nintendo needs `hid-nintendo`; Steam's is `hid-steam`. If a pad is switched on but not listed, **Option 3** (or `scripts/gamepad_bridge.py --doctor`) names the driver and the fix. This machine's kernel (`7.3.4-mplanetarian-dreamworlds-ultra-v1`) was built without `CONFIG_HID_NINTENDO`, so no Nintendo pad can enumerate here until a kernel that enables it is used; `CONFIG_HID_STEAM` is available.
  - **Option 4** turns controller support off or on and saves `MP_MANAGER_GAMEPAD` to `config.env`.

### Changed
- **Sensorium's pane is light coral** (`themes/Sensorium_MP_Mix_Manager.theme`, `Mix_Archive_Manager.sh`, `scripts/mp_theme_palette.py`, `scripts/liquid_glass_opengl.py`, `MP_Audio_Player.py`, `scripts/mix_web_companion.py`, `scripts/traktor_monitor.py`, `scripts/view_tracklist_console.sh`):
  - The console pane moves from deep oxblood `#1e080c` to light coral `#f08080` in btop, the shared background table, the CLI manager, the Web Companion and Traktor Monitor. The btop window, accents and graph colours are darkened so the palette stays readable on the lighter pane.
  - Text and menu ink are now deep maroon `#2b0a10`, with dark tangerine/chartreuse/azure/crimson variants for the accents. The bright chartreuse stays as the selection-bar fill with black text, so the theme keeps its identity without putting light ink on a light pane.
  - The terminal player detects light console panes from the shared background table and uses the theme's dark body ink for unselected playlist rows. Liquid Glass hosts keep their white rows because their pane comes from the shader, not the console background.
- **The MP Visualizer `Z` cycle returns to the window you actually had, and half means half the MP Liquid Player** (`scripts/visualizer_z_cycle.py`, `scripts/mp_liquid_visualizer_vulkan.py`, `scripts/mp_liquid_visualizer_metal.py`, `scripts/liquid_player_visualizer.py`, `tests/test_visualizer_z_cycle.py`):
  - **One cycle, three backends.** `Z` is now a single `ZWindowCycleMixin` instead of three copies that had drifted apart. The Vulkan, Metal and OpenGL renderers each supply only the three geometry primitives (`_z_window_size`, `_z_window_pos`, `_z_apply_windowed`), so the three windows cycle identically.
  - **The third press restores the shape the window really had.** The first `Z` captures the live size *and position* from the window itself. The old Vulkan code restored the constructor's default size/offset, so a window that had already been moved — or resized by the player after it measured its font — jumped to a place it was never in. A window dragged between cycles re-captures at the start of the next cycle.
  - **Half is half of the MP Liquid Player.** The shrunken window is half the player's published window (the rectangle the visualizer was matching), centred on the original window, instead of half of whatever size the visualizer happened to hold. With no player running, a standalone window halves its own size.
  - **The OpenGL twin no longer shrinks forever.** Its old `cycle_window_mode()` halved the window on *every* press: it resized to `self._size` and *then* set the mode to `half`, so the next press halved the half. Three presses left a quarter-size window and the size never came back. It now cycles correctly and returns to the original size.
  - **The Metal renderer starts in the right mode.** `--fullscreen` used to leave `window_mode == "default"`, so the first `Z` read as "leave fullscreen" and did not enter it. It now follows `fullscreen_on_start` like the Vulkan renderer, and leaving fullscreen goes through `glfwSetWindowMonitor(NULL, …)` instead of a plain resize.
  - **The companion is untouched.** The window the Liquid Player starts by itself (the backdrop that appears automatically behind it) is locked: it is never bonded to the cycle, never taken fullscreen and never shrunk. It keeps sitting at the player's exact rectangle. Only a window the user opened with `Z`, the shortcut, or a plain `MP_Liquid_Visualizer.py` run cycles.
  - **A chosen shape survives the per-frame size lock.** The half and restored sizes are written to `_default_windowed_size` / `_half_windowed_size`, which is what the Vulkan poll would otherwise use to force the window back to its opening size.
- **Three Liquid Glass Skin themes removed; Sensorium replaces Sensorium Interface** (`MP_Audio_Player.py`, `Mix_Archive_Manager.sh`, `scripts/mp_theme_palette.py`, `scripts/liquid_glass_opengl.py`, `scripts/mix_web_companion.py`, `scripts/traktor_monitor.py`, `scripts/view_tracklist_console.sh`, `themes/Sensorium_MP_Mix_Manager.theme`):
  - Cosmic Dreamworlds Radioactive, Cosmic Dreamworlds and Orbital Void Reflux are gone from every picker, alias map, btop palette and companion surface. The old `sensorium_interface` theme id is retired.
  - **Sensorium** is a new 15th theme: bright tangerine `#ff7a18`, acid chartreuse `#c8ff3d` and electric azure `#3a86ff`, with a deep oxblood console pane `#1e080c`. The palette mix turns the Liquid Glass pane champagne gold and gives the equaliser a tangerine-to-chartreuse-to-azure gradient. It works in MP Audio Player, MP Gui, the OpenGL/Vulkan/Metal Liquid Glass players, the tracklist viewer, Traktor Monitor, the Web Companion and btop.
  - `sensorium` (and the legacy `sensorium_interface`) resolve to the new theme, so existing `~/.config/mix-manager/theme` files keep working.
- **Dreamworlds Ultra is the first-run default** (`install.sh`, `Mix_Archive_Manager.sh`, `MP_Gui.sh`, `MP_Audio_Player.py`, `scripts/mp_theme_palette.py`, `scripts/mix_web_companion.py`, `scripts/traktor_monitor.py`, `scripts/view_tracklist_console.sh`):
  - A fresh install writes `dreamworlds_ultra` to `~/.config/mix-manager/theme`. The CLI manager, MP Gui, the Liquid Player, the standalone audio player, the tracklist viewer, the Web Companion and Traktor Monitor all fall back to Ultra when no theme has been saved.
  - Existing users keep the theme they picked; the installer never overwrites a saved choice.
  - The theme picker now marks Dreamworlds Ultra as **[Default]** and MPlanetarian Dreamworlds as a normal option.
- **Dreamworlds Ultra text is light purple and its pane is a lighter royal purple** (`scripts/liquid_glass_opengl.py`, `Mix_Archive_Manager.sh`, `themes/Dreamworlds_Ultra_MP_Mix_Manager.theme`, `scripts/mix_web_companion.py`, `scripts/traktor_monitor.py`, `scripts/view_tracklist_console.sh`):
  - Player body text and menu option ink are now `#d8b4fe` (216,180,254) instead of the deep `#a514ff` / `#b743ff`.
  - The console pane moves from `#16062e` (22,6,46) to a light grayish purple `#554a6b` (85,74,107), with the btop and Web Companion backgrounds matching. Menu numbers follow the same light purple; the vivid `#a64dff` hot purple stays on borders and the navigation bar.
  - A standalone MP Audio Player now emits OSC 11 from the shared `theme_background()` table at startup and whenever the theme changes, so its terminal pane is the theme colour instead of the terminal profile's black. Liquid Glass hosts skip it and stay transparent.
  - **Konsole ignores OSC 11**, which is why the manager's pane stayed black. The manager now generates `MP_Theme_<id>.colorscheme` in `~/.local/share/konsole` from the user's default scheme and switches to it with Konsole's OSC 50, restoring the default scheme on exit. Verified live on Konsole 26.08: the tab background becomes `#554a6b`.

- **Jenny is now the voice everywhere: a local British Piper voice, used by MP Reporter and the Harmony agent** (`MP_Harmony_Agent.py`, `scripts/mp_harmony_tts.py`, `scripts/wan2gp_tts_batch.py`, `scripts/mp_opencode_progress_reporter.py`, `scripts/mp_daily_email_reporter.py`, `MP_REPORTER/MP_Reporter.sh`, `tests/test_harmony_voice_consistency.py`):
  - **One voice, one setting.** `~/.config/mix-manager/harmony_voice` now holds `jenny`, and every program that speaks reads it, so they cannot drift apart.
  - **A named voice now actually selects its model.** `get_piper_voice_path()` ignored the requested name, so asking for `jenny` or `amy` returned whichever model sorted first — a request for one voice could be answered in another. The model cache is keyed by path, so switching voices switches the voice.
  - **The WAN2GP converter gained a Piper engine.** It had only espeak/edge/XTTS, so a Piper voice could not be selected on that path. `piper:jenny` and `piper:amy` are now first-class entries, fully offline.
  - **Every fallback order now ends at a working voice**: Harmony → Piper → WAN2GP → Hawking. Piper Jenny is preferred over the Hawking espeak chain in both MP Reporter paths and the daily email briefing.
  - **The cloned Matrix voices remain available** but are labelled `[needs XTTS]`, since XTTS reserves about 4 GB of VRAM and stays off.

### Fixed
- **Announcements no longer play over each other** (`scripts/mp_speech_lock.py`, `scripts/mp_opencode_progress_reporter.py`, `tests/test_speech_serialization.py`): the 10-minute session briefing could start while another announcement was still speaking.
  - **Two independent players on the same cadence.** MP Reporter's email timer plays a confirmation chime every ten minutes from its own process, with no coordination with other spoken output; a chime could land in the middle of a 142-second narration.
  - **One-shots bypassed the service lock.** A manual announcement could speak over the running service.
  - **A machine-wide POSIX lock now serialises every spoken output** (`~/.local/state/mp-speech.lock`). `play_audio()` holds it for the whole of playback, and the email chime waits for it, so nothing overlaps regardless of which process or thread asks. A crashed holder cannot wedge it: the kernel releases the lock on exit.
  - **Stale briefings are dropped, not delayed.** A briefing that waited behind long audio is skipped rather than read as old news (`MP_OPENCODE_PROGRESS_MAX_AGE`, 15 minutes).
  - **Degrades safely.** A missing lock helper falls back to playing immediately, so this can never silence the system.
- **Two voices at once: a Matrix voice could silently become the generic narrator** (`MP_Harmony_Agent.py`, `scripts/mp_harmony_tts.py`, `tests/test_harmony_voice_consistency.py`): asking for Morpheus while the XTTS voice-cloning service was down fell through to the generic Piper narrator — a female voice — while the rest of the system still spoke in the Stephen Hawking voice.
  - **The fallback now stays inside the Matrix voice set.** A failed cloned voice falls back to Stephen Hawking rather than to a different person, so every spoken output is one consistent voice.
  - **The fallback is announced**, explaining that the cloned voices need XTTS and its VRAM cost, so a fallback is understood rather than mysterious.
  - **The Hawking chain is shared.** It was extracted into `_synthesize_hawking_wav()` so the explicit Hawking path and the Matrix fallback cannot drift apart.
  - **`--set-voice` warns immediately** when a chosen voice needs XTTS and the service is not running, instead of letting the user discover it by ear.
  - The shared setting in `~/.config/mix-manager/harmony_voice` was also never written, so it had defaulted to `morpheus`; it is now `stephen_hawking_matrix`, which MP Reporter reads.
- **Music mode lists tracks that it can actually stream** (`scripts/mix_web_companion.py`, `tests/test_web_companion_security.py`):
  - **The hardening in 0.5.3 closed the arbitrary-file read and took Music with it.** `5e90eb4` replaced the raw `os.path.isfile()` check behind `/api/stream` with `safe_stream_path()`, which requires the requested file to live inside a configured *library* root — and the handler was handed `_archive_dirs()` alone. The queue, however, is built from whichever library the mode selects: the mix archive, the configured music folders, or Favorites. In Music mode every listed track therefore resolved to a path outside the allow-list, and the dashboard drew a correct playlist in which **every tap answered 404**. The playlist looked perfect, which is exactly why this read as a dead play button on the phone rather than a server fault.
  - **A single allow-list now covers every library the queue can be built from.** `stream_roots()` is `_archive_dirs()` plus `music_folder_roots()`, and the stream handler uses it. Favorites needs no root of its own: a favorite is a path the user marked in the player, and it resolves inside the archive or a music folder, so adding one would only widen the surface without enabling anything.
  - **Containment is unchanged for everything else.** Four regression tests pin the new behaviour: a music track streams, `/etc/passwd` and a `config.env` outside every root are still refused, and the handler call site is asserted to pass `stream_roots()` so a future edit cannot quietly hand it `_archive_dirs()` again. The 46 existing security tests, including the traversal and symlink-escape cases, still pass.

- **The Stream of Frequency equalisers are smooth in a full screen window** (`MP_Audio_Player.py`, `tests/test_equalizer_fullscreen_performance.py`):
  - **Three separate costs, all paid every frame.** A maximised Liquid Glass window is roughly 300 columns by 95 rows, and at that size the previous code spent more time formatting and shipping the picture than drawing it. Measured on the DP-2 panel geometry (2784x1740, ~309x96 cells):
    - `_strip_bold_ansi` re-parsed every escape of every line on every frame, even though the header, the playlist and the key hints are byte-identical between frames. It is now memoised per line, and the common "bold is the first parameter" shape is handled with plain string replacement instead of a regular expression.
    - `_guard_visualizer_text` walked every TrueColor escape of every line on every frame. It is now memoised per line, and the brightened escape itself is cached by (prefix, r, g, b, suffix), which is the same few hundred values forever.
    - The Liquid Spectrogram, the Overview Effect and the three Rainbow effects wrapped **every single coloured cell** in its own SGR pair, `_fx_lines` now emits colour and bold once per run, which is where those effects actually live: long horizontal stretches of one palette entry. This is the biggest single win: the Liquid Spectrogram went from 25 KiB of escape traffic per frame to 4 KiB, and the Overview from 23 KiB to 5 KiB.
  - **The rolling spectrogram no longer fills its grid one cell at a time in Python.** Every cell's glyph and colour is a pure function of its energy and its `(row, column)`, so the default Dynamic Spectrogram now classifies the whole grid once with numpy. Its renderer halved.
  - **The Overview Effect's box blur and its robust min/max range are computed on whole rows** instead of in a nested Python loop over every cell. The edge and corner cells still divide by the number of neighbours they actually have, and the percentile is still taken at the exact sample index, so the coastline of the map is unchanged to the byte.
  - **The frame loop paces by deadline.** It used to sleep a fixed 20 ms *after* drawing, so the real period was `draw_time + 20 ms` and it jittered with whatever the equaliser cost that frame -- a cheap frame landed 21 ms later, an expensive one 30 ms later, which reads as the animation speeding up and slowing down. A deadline keeps the cadence constant.
  - **Measured whole-frame cost, median of 300 frames, old to new:**

    | Effect | 284x78 | 309x96 (full screen) | 426x120 |
    |---|---|---|---|
    | Dynamic Spectrogram | 8.70 → **3.17 ms** | 9.73 → **3.30 ms** | 11.87 → **3.88 ms** |
    | Liquid Spectrogram | 10.45 → **2.47 ms** | 11.91 → **2.50 ms** | 14.78 → **3.28 ms** |
    | Overview Effect | 5.17 → **3.31 ms** | 10.28 → **3.93 ms** | 14.12 → **5.28 ms** |
    | Rainbow Mandala | 15.13 → **9.02 ms** | 17.29 → **9.97 ms** | 22.22 → **12.63 ms** |
    | Rainbow Wormhole | 19.18 → **12.58 ms** | 20.92 → **13.29 ms** | 26.30 → **17.11 ms** |
    | Rainbow Aurora | 11.39 → **4.74 ms** | 12.59 → **5.33 ms** | 16.30 → **7.17 ms** |

    Every Stream of Frequency reading now sits inside a 60 fps frame budget (16.7 ms) at true full screen with room to spare, where the Overview Effect alone used to need more than half of it. Feeding the frames into a real VTE terminal confirms the terminal-side parse cost fell 1.7x for the Dynamic Spectrogram and about 5x for the Liquid and Overview readings.
  - **The picture is unchanged.** A harness renders 3,240 frames through the old and new code -- all twelve equaliser styles, seven window geometries including 26x1 and 300x9, and thirty animation steps each -- and compares the decoded cell grid (glyph, foreground, background and bold, with spaces ignored because they paint nothing). Zero differences.

### Added
- **The installer now installs the MP Companion Service** (`install.sh`):
  - The Studio & DJ Booth Mobile Web Companion (`scripts/mix_web_companion.py`, port **8888**) used to be a manual step: `./install.sh` created the desktop shortcuts and the tray icon but never registered the companion's service, so a fresh machine had no phone dashboard and nothing came back after a reboot. The installer now runs `scripts/mix_web_companion.sh install` as its own **[7/9]** step, which writes the `systemd --user` unit (`mp-web-companion.service`) on Linux or the launchd LaunchAgent (`com.mplanetarian.mp-web-companion`) on macOS, enables lingering so it starts before login, and starts it. Windows is skipped because the tray owns start/stop there. Set `MP_INSTALL_COMPANION=never` to opt out on a kiosk or headless image. The installer still never writes the unit itself: the platform definitions stay in one place, the wrapper.
  - The completion banner now prints the companion's LAN URL beside the desktop shortcuts.
  - **macOS and Windows were then tested from a genuinely clean state** (`/Applications/MP Web Companion.app`, the launchd plist, `~/.config/mix-manager` and the venv all deleted first), and both needed real fixes before they passed:
    - **Windows had no companion support at all.** `mix_web_companion.sh` only ever branched on `is_macos()`, so on Windows the script fell through to a direct `python` run that nothing kept alive. Windows has no `systemd --user` or `launchd` for a per-user process, so the new **`launch_web_companion.bat`** starts the server with `pythonw.exe` and `scripts/windows_user_integration.ps1` writes **`Startup\MP Web Companion.lnk`**, which the shell runs at every sign-in. The installer also starts it immediately through Task Scheduler (the same channel the tray already uses, because `install.sh` runs under Git Bash and kills its children on exit) and waits for port 8888 to answer, not merely for a process to exist.
    - **The Windows firewall dialog blocked the dashboard.** On first listen, Windows showed "allow Python on public and private networks" and, with nobody at the keyboard, Python stayed blocked — the phone could not reach the dashboard and the guest agent could not poll. `Add-MpCompanionFirewallRule` now creates `MP Web Companion (TCP 8888)` (best effort; a per-user install prints the exact elevated `New-NetFirewallRule` command when it cannot).
    - **GLFW was reported missing on Windows and macOS although it worked.** The spec names `libglfw.so.3`, the Linux spelling. Windows ships **`glfw3.dll`** inside the PyPI `glfw` package and macOS/Homebrew ships **`libglfw.dylib`**, so the probe found neither. New `_which_windows_library` and `_which_dylib` translate the name into every platform spelling (and search `site-packages` for a package-bundled DLL), so `glfw.init()` returning 1 now matches a green dependency report.
    - **`brew` was invoked by bare name.** `which_binary` deliberately searches `/opt/homebrew/bin` because a bare SSH session has a minimal PATH, but `install_command` then returned the literal string `["brew", "install", …]` and the run died with `FileNotFoundError: 'brew'` on a Mac where brew was installed. It now uses the resolved absolute path.
- **The JSON report and daily email no longer import Unix-only or optional modules unconditionally** (`scripts/mp_daily_email_reporter.py`):
  - `import fcntl` is Unix-only, so on Windows the module raised `ModuleNotFoundError` at import and took the whole daily-email engine with it. It now falls back to `msvcrt.locking()` on the same lock file, which gives the identical single-dispatch guarantee.
  - `reportlab` and `pypdf` are declared **optional** dependencies but were imported bare, so a machine that skipped the optional extras could not import the module at all. Both are now guarded, `HAS_PDF` reports whether the PDF feature is available, and `generate_consolidated_pdf` logs which extra is missing and the command that installs it instead of raising `AttributeError`.
- **The companion crashed on launch on Windows printing its own banner** (`scripts/mix_web_companion.py`, `launch_web_companion.bat`):
  - The startup banner contains emoji. A Windows console is **cp1252**, so `print()` raised `UnicodeEncodeError` **before the socket was bound** and the server exited immediately. The installer still reported success because it only checked that a process had been spawned, so the dashboard looked installed and never answered.
  - `_make_console_utf8_safe()` now reconfigures stdout/stderr to UTF-8 with `errors="replace"` as the first thing `main()` does, and the Windows launcher also exports `PYTHONIOENCODING=utf-8` for any child process. The banner renders where the console supports it and degrades to `?` where it does not.
  - Verified: listening on `0.0.0.0:8888`, **HTTP 200 on localhost and on the LAN IP** (`192.0.2.133:8888`).
- **The Windows launcher now honours `config.env` like the other platforms** (`launch_web_companion.bat`): it reads `WEB_COMPANION_HOST` / `WEB_COMPANION_PORT` from `config.env` and defaults the host to `0.0.0.0`, mirroring `scripts/mix_web_companion.sh`. Windows has no wrapper to source the file, so without this the launcher and the manager could disagree about the port.
- **The Windows launcher never stacks a second companion** (`launch_web_companion.bat`): the Startup shortcut runs at every sign-in *and* the installer starts the companion, so a plain `start` produced **two `pythonw` processes both listening on 8888** (Windows permits that with `SO_REUSEADDR`). The two then disagreed about the stream queue, so the phone got a different answer depending on which one accepted the connection. The launcher now checks for a running `mix_web_companion` first and exits with "already running"; `--stop` sweeps both `pythonw.exe` and `python.exe`. Verified on the VM: one process, one listener.
- **The Auto Tester's public-clone behaviour was broken** (`tests/test_mp_auto_tester.py`): `MP_Auto_Tester_Flash.sh` is deliberately gitignored, and the file's own note says a public clone should *skip* the controller assertions. Nine of them omitted the `@needs_harness` guard and failed with `FileNotFoundError` in a clean checkout. The guard is now a class-level `setUpClass` skip on both affected classes, so a newly added method cannot forget it. Verified against a copy with the controller removed: **77 tests OK (8 skipped)** instead of 9 failures.
- **One Web Companion test could never pass on Windows** (`tests/test_web_companion_install_identity.py`): `test_exits_zero_without_a_display` builds a POSIX environment (`PATH=/usr/bin:/bin`, `XDG_RUNTIME_DIR`) and runs unconditionally, so on Windows it failed with no useful signal. It now skips on `win32` with a reason; the Windows tray path is covered by `scripts/windows_tray.ps1` and the installer tests.
- **The Auto Tester deploys the complete Windows tree** (`MP_Auto_Tester_Flash.sh`): `launch_web_companion.bat` was missing from `DEPLOY_PATHS`, so a remote Windows run installed everything except the dashboard launcher, and the Windows bundle now uses `--dereference` — Windows `tar` cannot create a Unix symlink, so the guest previously received `Can't create … Invalid argument` for every link and a truncated tree.
- **The dependency report for macOS and Windows was tested on the real machines** (`scripts/mp_dependencies.py`, `tests/test_installer_dependencies.py`): the macOS mini's **Python 3.9** has no `sys.stdlib_module_names`, so the import audit crashed instead of reporting; it now skips with a clear reason below 3.10 rather than inventing false positives (a hand-written stdlib list wrongly flagged `piper`, a TTS engine name). The `pdfimages` check in the report-builder test also skips when poppler is absent instead of failing on a machine that simply lacks the tool.
  - Verified end to end: macOS **8/8 dependencies, exit 0**; Windows **7/7 dependencies, exit 0**; the companion answers **HTTP 200** on `:8888` on both, and both suites pass (**84 tests OK** each).

### Fixed
- **The Dreamworlds login screen is Centered // Scaled on every monitor, not Tiled** (`scripts/set_login_screen_wallpaper.sh`, `tests/test_login_screen_wallpaper.py`):
  - **The GDM rule scaled the picture correctly but had no repeat guard, and the KDE layers were set to `FillMode=2` (Scaled and cropped).** The KDE wallpaper config picked up tiling because `FillMode` is just an integer (`3` = Tile, `4`/`5` = the one-axis tile modes, `2` = scaled and cropped, `1` = centred with proportions kept), so any value written without a guard looks right on one monitor and tiles on another. The login screen is now pinned to `FillMode=1` — the whole 1920×1088 picture fits inside every monitor and stays centred.
  - **GDM** (`/var/lib/mp-gdm-theme/gnome-shell-theme.gresource`) now carries `background-size: contain; background-repeat: no-repeat` and a black letterbox colour in all three stylesheets. `contain` is the St equivalent of PreserveAspectFit; `cover` (`background-size: cover`) crops the sides on 16:10 panels and on the 4K TV, and no `background-size` at all draws the picture at native size, leaving the Hisense with a small image in the corner.
  - **Plasma Login** (`/etc/plasmalogin.conf`) had **no wallpaper configured at all** and fell back to the stock Fedora convergence picture. It now points at `/var/lib/plasmalogin/wallpapers/MPlanetarian_Dreamworlds_Lockscreen.png` with `FillMode=1`.
  - **The KDE lock screen** is fixed in both `/etc/xdg/kscreenlockerrc` (the fallback the greeter reads when the per-user file has no picture) and `~/.config/kscreenlockerrc`.
  - **The other Bazzite install (RAID volume) is handled too.** `--other` repairs `/run/media/<user>/bazzite_raid/home/<user>/.config/kscreenlockerrc`, plus `plasmalogin.conf` and `kscreenlockerrc` in **every ostree deployment** on that volume. Three of them disagreed before: the bootable `a25775e4`/`b61f5bf3` deployments had no Dreamworlds greeter config at all, and only the older `e9674b33` deployment carried one — so the login screen changed depending on which entry booted.
  - **The fix is a script, not a hand edit.** `scripts/set_login_screen_wallpaper.sh` is idempotent (it rewrites only `[Greeter]` and the wallpaper group, preserving comments and other sections), supports `--dry-run`, `--status`, and `--other`, and resolves the invoking user's home through `SUDO_USER` so a sudo re-exec does not look for the picture in `/root/Pictures`.
  - **The BazziteMac test bed no longer suspends itself** (`scripts/mp_testbed_power_stability.sh`, `tests/test_testbed_power_stability.py`):
  - **The "random power off" is an idle suspend, not a hardware fault.** The journals from every affected boot end the same way:
    ```
    19:58:32 systemd-logind: suspend requested from client PID 4906
              ('org_kde_powerde') (unit user@1000.service)
    23:26:18 kernel: Freezing user space processes
    ```
    KDE PowerDevil asks logind to idle-suspend, logind freezes user space, and on this 2010 MacBook Pro the resume never completes — the LAN interface never comes back, so the machine looks dead until it is power-cycled. There is **no** thermal event, watchdog trip or power-button record in any boot, and the battery and CPU temperatures are healthy.
  - The new script makes suspension impossible on the test bed: logind ignores the lid (all three variants), the power key, the suspend/hibernate keys and idle; `AllowSuspend`, `AllowHibernation` and `AllowHybridSleep` are off; `sleep/suspend/hibernate/hybrid-sleep` targets are masked; and PowerDevil is told never to suspend on AC or battery. `--revert` puts it all back.
  - **The Auto Tester now holds a sleep inhibitor for the length of a run** (`systemd-inhibit --what=idle:sleep:shutdown`), so an unattended desktop cannot suspend between the unit tests and the screenshot sweep. It is released on exit, including on failure.
- **The Auto Tester could not find windows, or test, on Plasma Wayland** (`MP_Auto_Tester_Flash.sh`):
  - **Window lookup fell back to `xdotool`.** `wmctrl -l` prints nothing on KWin Wayland because the X11 root belongs to Xwayland and carries no EWMH client list, so every `find_window_linux` returned empty, `wait_for_window` timed out and **no screenshot was ever captured** even though the applications opened. `xdotool search` enumerates Xwayland clients directly and is now used first; `wmctrl` stays as the X11 fallback. `window_is_front` now compares window ids in one base (xprop prints hex, xdotool prints decimal, so the old string compare never matched).
  - **The deployed test tree includes every symlink target.** The copy excluded `Exit_MP_Video.mp4`, and `bin/*` and `scripts/*` are symlinks into the tree (`bin/alarm -> ../mplanetarians-alarm-clock/alarm.sh`, `bin/cut-video -> ../Cut_Video.sh`, `assets/Exit_MP_Video.mp4 -> ../Exit_MP_Video.mp4`). Shipping the links without their targets produced a copy where a dozen tests failed on missing files, which read as a product regression. `deploy_tree` now expands the file list with each deployed link's in-tree target.
  - **The unit suite resolves the tree under test, not the user's install.** On a deployed copy the `~/.local/bin` launchers and `~/.config/mix-manager/install_dir` still point at the real installation, so `test_install_dir_resolution` failed on a perfectly good install. The harness now exports `MP_MIX_MANAGER_DIR=$tree`, which the resolver already honours first.
- **The dependency probe claimed a broken module was installed** (`scripts/mp_dependencies.py`):
  - Probing used `importlib.util.find_spec` for every module, which only proves a file exists. **numpy 2.x** ships an **x86-64-v2** baseline, so on a Core 2 Duo (`SSE4.2`/`POPCNT` absent) `import numpy` raises *"your machine doesn't support X86_V2"* while `find_spec` reports it present. The installer then announced **"all required dependencies present (8/8)"**, wrote the launchers against that interpreter, and the player crashed on launch.
  - **Hard dependencies are now really imported.** Optional ones still use `find_spec`, so ultralytics and friends stay cheap to probe; only the modules the app cannot start without are executed. A module that is found but raises is reported as **missing**, which is the truth.
  - **A second install path tries the distribution build.** When pip succeeds but the wheel still cannot be imported on this CPU, the engine uninstalls that copy (it shadows the distro one inside the venv) and installs the distribution package, which is compiled for the distribution's own baseline and does run. On Bazzite that is `python3-numpy` layered with `rpm-ostree`.
  - **The installer's venv handoff no longer depends on numpy.** It used to switch `MP_PYTHON` to the per-user venv only if `import numpy` succeeded *there*. When numpy was the one broken module the handoff was skipped, the desktop entries and tray were written against the bare system interpreter, and the final `--verify` gate then printed **"This install is not ready to run yet"** immediately after the dependency step had said 8/8. It now adopts any venv the engine created, and the gate agreed with the report in the same run.
- **The Auto Tester has a Bazzite laptop target** (`MP_Auto_Tester_Flash.sh`): `--only bazzite` (aliases `bazzitemac`, `laptop`) runs the suite and the four shortcut captures on the **BazziteMac** test bed at `MP_BAZZITE_HOST` (default `192.0.2.160`). It is a Linux host, so it reuses the SSH agent path, but it is its own target so a slow, battery-powered machine is never confused with the Fedora workstation.

---

## [0.5.3 Stable (Sensorium)] - 2026-11-14

> **This section is the complete 0.5.3 record.** The entries below were written after the November 13 build
> and are the newest; the block headed _Originally documented for the November 13 build_ further down holds
> the notes published with the first cut. Release date is the final public date, **November 14, 2026**.

### Changed

- **The bundled release theme track is the Dreamworlds Remix, re-encoded to fit GitHub** (`MP_SAMPLES/`):
  - `MPlanetarian - The Orbits of Planets (MPlanetarian Dreamworlds Remix).wav` — the 924 MiB / 27:27 24-bit
    96 kHz master — is now the source instead of the older 14:17 cut. GitHub rejects any file over 100 MiB,
    so the WAV itself is never committed (it is gitignored, as before) and the shipped artifact is a FLAC.
  - **The filename is deliberately unchanged:** `MPlanetarian - The Orbits of Planets (Cosmic Dreamworlds
    Remake).flac`. `scripts/liquid_glass_reward.py` builds `SAMPLE_TRACK_FILE` from `SAMPLE_TRACK_NAME`, the
    installer copies the track on first run, and `tests/test_first_run_sample_track.py` asserts the path, so
    renaming it would have broken first-run preload for every existing install.
  - **Encoded at 16-bit / 48 kHz** to match the format the previous file used, so nothing that reads the
    sample track sees a different bit depth or rate. `flac -t` verifies clean; the result is 96.3 MiB, inside
    GitHub's 100 MiB limit with 3.7 MiB to spare.
  - **The cover is embedded twice**, as `Cover (front)` (picture type 3) and `Cover (back)` (type 4), both
    from `MP_SAMPLES/The_Orbits_of_Planets_Dreamworlds_Cover.png` (1200×1200 PNG) — the same pairing the
    previous file carried.
  - Metadata is the previous file's tag set with the title updated to `The Orbits of Planets (MPlanetarian
    Dreamworlds Remix)`, so album, artist, genre, label and catalogue fields stay consistent with the release.
  - The README now states the real duration (**27:27**) and bit depth instead of the old **14:17**.

### Changed

- **The exit and first-launch intro movie is 10 s, not 39.5 s** (`Exit_MP_Video.mp4`, `tests/test_player_intro_video.py`):
  - The clip plays **before** the first MP Gui / MP Liquid Player window appears, so a 39.5 s movie read as
    "the player is very slow to load" — the window was waiting on the video, not initialising slowly.
  - Trimmed to **10 s** at the same encoding and geometry (`H.264` + `AAC`, `470x878`, 60 fps, faststart),
    so the ffplay/mpv/VLC/WPF/AppKit fallback order and the skip keys are unchanged. **21.5 MB -> 2.8 MB.**
  - There is still only **one** file: `assets/Exit_MP_Video.mp4` is a symlink to `../Exit_MP_Video.mp4`,
    which `display_loader_video.sh` searches as a fallback location. Both paths resolve to the trimmed copy.
  - `test_the_wait_is_bounded_by_the_movie_not_the_ceiling` asserted `duration > 20` s and
    `test_a_smaller_ceiling_still_wins` used a 30 s ceiling; both were written for the 39.5 s clip. They now
    check that the wait tracks the movie (limit = duration + 8 s grace, capped by the ceiling) without
    hard-coding the old length.

- **Desktop shortcuts for MP Harmony and MP Reporter** (`scripts/install_harmony_desktop_shortcuts.sh`, `install.sh`, `tests/test_harmony_desktop_shortcuts.py`): nine entries — **MP Harmony Agent (Chat)**, **Start Server**, **Stop Server**, **Status**, **Web HUD**, **MP Reporter Suite**, **Run Daily Now**, **Status**, and **View Latest** — written to the Desktop and mirrored into the application menu.
  - **They open a terminal.** Every entry launches Konsole (or xterm, or a plain shell as a last resort); a bare `sh -c` with `Terminal=false` would have run with no window at all.
  - **Commands are real.** "Run Daily Now" passes the actual task id (`DAILY_REPORT_TASK_001`, not `daily`, which is not a task file), and Status uses `mp_scheduled_reporter.sh --status`.
  - **Re-runnable and removable.** `--list` shows presence, `--remove` deletes them, and a repeat run replaces entries in place rather than duplicating them. They are also installed by `./install.sh` when the optional agent is accepted. `MP_DESKTOP_DIR` and `MP_APPLICATIONS_DIR` override the locations.
- **MP Harmony is now the shared voice for MP Reporter** (`scripts/mp_harmony_tts.py`, `scripts/mp_harmony_archive.py`, `scripts/manage_mp_reporter.sh`, `MP_Harmony_Agent.py`):
  - **One voice setting for spoken output.** `mp_harmony_tts.py` wraps the Harmony bridge's OpenAI-compatible `POST /v1/audio/speech`, giving MP Reporter the full Matrix voice set (Morpheus, Agent Smith, Neo, Trinity, Oracle, Zion, Stephen Hawking). The chosen voice lives in `~/.config/mix-manager/harmony_voice`.
  - **The bridge advertises its voices.** New `GET /api/voices` on the Harmony server returns the voice map and the active default, so a consumer does not keep a duplicate list that drifts when a voice is added.
  - **Harmony speech now appears in MP Reporter.** `mp_harmony_archive.py` copies Harmony's `.wav`/`.txt` pairs into `MP_REPORTER/TTS_AUDIO/HARMONY`, where they sit beside `DAILY`, `OPENCODE` and `SYSTEM_ERRORS`. It keeps a manifest so repeated runs only copy what is new, skips half-written WAVs, and can prune archived copies while leaving the agent's originals untouched. `mp_report_generator.py` runs the sync before every report, and the AI report section now reads both the live agent folder and the archive, collapsing duplicates and reporting how much is archived.
  - **New command-line entry points:** `Mix_Archive_Manager.sh --harmony-archive [sync|status|prune]` and `manage-harmony-tts` in `~/.local/bin`.
- **The MP Harmony Agent is now an optional, separately installed component** (`scripts/install_harmony_agent.sh`, `install.sh`, `scripts/manage_harmony_agent.sh`, `scripts/launch_mp_harmony.sh`, `tests/test_harmony_installer.py`):
  - **It is no longer shipped inside the manager tree.** `MPMixManager_v0.5-x64/MP_Harmony_Agent/` was removed; the agent is its own repository and its own installation.
  - **Offered during install, defaulting to Yes.** `./install.sh` gains a new step 8, "MP Harmony Agent (Optional)", which asks `Install the MP Harmony Agent? [Y/n]` on an interactive run. Answer No and every other feature installs exactly as before. `--no-harmony` (or `MP_INSTALL_HARMONY=never`) skips it silently, `--install-harmony` (`always`) takes it without asking, and non-interactive runs take the default, which is to include it.
  - **Installed from source, not copied.** `scripts/install_harmony_agent.sh` clones `https://github.com/MPlanetarian/MP-Harmony-Agent.git` into `~/OpenAI/MP_Harmony_Agent` with a timeout, and fast-forwards an existing checkout only when it is clean, so local work in progress is never overwritten. `HARMONY_SKIP_PULL=1` disables updates, `HARMONY_DIR` and `HARMONY_REPO_URL` override the location and remote.
  - **Discoverable after the fact.** `scripts/install_harmony_agent.sh {install|status|path|unit}`, `manage_harmony_agent.sh install`, and the `install-harmony` / `manage-harmony` links in `~/.local/bin`. A missing agent now prints how to install it instead of a bare "file not found".
  - **The systemd unit follows the checkout.** `mp-harmony-agent.service` is generated with `WorkingDirectory` and `ExecStart` pointing at `~/OpenAI/MP_Harmony_Agent`, so removing the bundle cannot leave a unit pointing at a deleted path.
  - **Graceful when absent.** `launch_mp_harmony.sh` and `manage_harmony_agent.sh` resolve `HARMONY_DIR`, then `~/OpenAI/MP_Harmony_Agent`, then the legacy `~/OpenAI/Harmony_Agent`, and report a clear next step if none exist. No other part of the manager depends on the agent.
- **Raspberry Pi support: MP Mix Manager now runs on a Pi 3/4/5 and Zero 2 W, and the Development Utility can build the packages for it** (`scripts/mp_raspberry_pi.py`, `scripts/lib/mp_pi_detect.sh`, `MP_Dev_Utility.sh`, `tests/test_raspberry_pi.py`):
  - **Why it did not run before.** The Broadcom VideoCore GPU reports **OpenGL 3.1 core / GLSL 1.40** through Mesa V3D, never desktop OpenGL 3.3. Every renderer asked for a 3.3 core context and compiled `#version 330 core` shaders, so `glfwCreateWindow` returned NULL and the Liquid Glass window never opened. On top of that, GLFW is loaded through `ctypes`, and Raspberry Pi OS Bookworm ships libglfw3 **3.3** while the code called `glfwInitHint` (a 3.4-only symbol) unguarded, raising `AttributeError` before any window existed. `libglfw3` was also not a declared dependency anywhere, so a stock Pi image did not have it at all.
  - **Context ladder** (`scripts/liquid_glass_opengl.py`, `scripts/liquid_player_visualizer.py`): the player and the visualizer now try a list of contexts in order — 3.3 core on a desktop, **3.1 core first on a Pi** — and keep the first that is granted. The requested version is recorded so the shader stage knows what it may use.
  - **GLSL down-levelling** (`mp_raspberry_pi.adapt_glsl`): if a context still rejects 3.30, the source is rewritten to the highest version the driver accepts. Only two things need changing below 3.30 — the `#version` line and `layout(location = N)`, which arrived with GL 3.3 — and the vertex attributes are then bound explicitly with `glBindAttribLocation`. Everything the shaders actually use (`in`/`out`, `texture()`, structs, constant-bound loops, integer `%`) is valid at 1.40. Verified against a real GLSL 1.40 compiler: the original shaders are rejected, the rewritten ones compile, including the 3,456-line twelve-scene visualizer.
  - **`glfwInitHint` is now guarded everywhere** (player, visualizer, Vulkan host), so GLFW 3.3 on Bookworm no longer crashes the launcher.
  - **GLFW is a declared dependency** (`scripts/mp_dependencies.py`, `requirements.txt`): `libglfw3` / `glfw` / `glfw-devel`, probed with a new `which_library()` that searches the loader cache and multiarch paths, because a `ctypes` library never appears on `PATH`.
  - **The Vulkan host is never chosen on a Pi.** `liquid_glass_vulkan.py` refuses every non-NVIDIA device by design, so a `LIQUID_GLASS_BACKEND="vulkan"` config left a Pi with a dead window. `MP_Gui.sh`, `scripts/launch_liquid_glass.sh` and `Mix_Archive_Manager.sh` now correct the backend to OpenGL through `mp_renderer_backend` and print why.
  - **The transparency slider draws again on any GL below 4.1.** Its shaders were `#version 410 core` inside a 3.3 context, so the failure was swallowed and the slider silently never appeared — on Pi hardware *and* on every desktop that is not 4.1-capable. It now compiles at the context's own version.
  - **New Raspberry Pi target in the Development Utility** (`--targets raspberrypi`, menu item 13): always `arm64`. Writes `MP_Mix_Manager_<version>_RaspberryPi/` containing a `.deb` that declares `libglfw3` and the Mesa V3D packages as dependencies, and a tarball whose `install-raspberry-pi.sh` offers to install that whole stack with `apt`, installs the app, writes Pi-tuned defaults and verifies OpenGL. Both artifacts drop the Windows/macOS launchers, the 21 MB exit movie and the old PDF manual, cutting the bundle from 111 MB to 48 MB. The `all` set now includes it.

### Changed
- **Raspberry Pi performance tuning** (`scripts/mp_raspberry_pi.py`, `scripts/lib/mp_pi_detect.sh`):
  - The visualizer raymarch now renders at a capped long edge and scales up (640 px on a Pi 4, 960 on a 4 GB Pi 5, 1280 on 8 GB) instead of marching at the native window size, where the 84-step, twelve-scene SDF ran at single-digit FPS. `MP_VISUALIZER_GL_MARCH_STEPS` is also honoured on Linux now; it was silently macOS-only. A desktop still marches at full resolution.
  - **Thermal back-off:** the rendered size steps down at 70 °C and again at 80 °C, read from the SoC thermal zone (rate-limited to one check every 10 s), so a Pi degrades smoothly instead of stalling when it throttles.
  - **The MP Visualizer is capped at 30 FPS on a Pi** (`MP_VISUALIZER_FPS_CAP`). The raymarch cannot hold 60 Hz on a VideoCore GPU, so an uncapped loop only produced uneven frames and heat. The cap lowers the loop's pacing target without ever raising it, and `swap_interval_for_cap` sets GLFW's vsync divisor (60 Hz → 2, 120 Hz → 4) so the driver blocks instead of the loop spinning. A display rate that is not a multiple of the cap keeps interval 1 and the loop deadline handles the remainder. `MP_VISUALIZER_FPS_CAP=0` disables it; values are clamped to 10–120 so a typo cannot freeze the window. Desktops are unaffected.
  - **State-file write amplification removed.** `_state_sync_file` used to serialise the full status JSON and rewrite two files 25 times a second while playing — about 50 writes a second on the microSD card. It now compares a fingerprint first and skips identical payloads; position is quantised to whole seconds, which is all the MM:SS header renders. Explicit state changes still force a write.
  - **The 74 MB search table is gone.** `_search_clean_table` enumerated all 1,114,111 Unicode codepoints into a dict on the first keystroke in the filter box. It now seeds the 82 ASCII entries and adds non-ASCII characters on demand; output is byte-identical, memory is 2.1 KiB.
  - **The archive walk is cached** (`archive_scan_cache.json`, keyed by the archive roots' mtimes). `--list-mixes` and `--get-latest-mix` are called on every menu paint and each used to re-walk every archive.
  - **Spectrogram generation has a timeout.** `generate_track_spek` decoded the whole file with no limit, so a stalled `ffmpeg` hung the IPC thread forever. The limit scales with file size and is three times longer on a Pi.
  - The Bluetooth router is disabled automatically when the kernel exposes no adapter, instead of forking `pactl` five times a second for nothing.
  - `README_RaspberryPi.txt` in the bundle lists which file to use, the supported boards, what the Pi build changes, the two tuning values that matter, and how to check that the V3D driver loaded rather than llvmpipe.

### Added
- **MP Reporter now emails OpenCode agent progress every ten minutes, as text and as a Stephen Hawking audio report** (`scripts/mp_opencode_progress_reporter.py`, `scripts/mp_opencode_progress_reporter.sh`, `tests/test_mp_opencode_progress_reporter.py`):
  - **What it reports:** every *running* OpenCode session on the machine — title, agent and model, working directory, how long it has been running, last activity time, token counts, how many new messages and tool calls landed in the last window, a condensed list of recent tool work, and the agent's latest words. Subagent sessions are labelled with the parent task they belong to.
  - **Two reports in one email:** a plain-text report (also attached as `.txt`) and an MP3 audio briefing in the Stephen Hawking Matrix voice (`espeak-ng` → bass/EQ/reverb chain → ffmpeg), both built from the same data so the spoken and written briefings agree.
  - **Ten-minute cadence, enforced precisely:** `mp-reporter-opencode-progress.timer` runs `OnUnitActiveSec=10min`, and the reporter additionally keeps an interval bucket in `data/opencode_progress_state.json`, so a duplicate or catch-up tick cannot double-send. This is a separate limit from the daily reporter's 1-email-per-day rule, which is untouched.
  - **Session discovery is layered:** the running-session list comes from the live OpenCode service (`/api/session/active` plus each session's newest messages). If the service is down or restarting, it falls back to a read-only scan of `opencode.db` (`session_v2` / `session_message`, with support for the older `session` / `message` layout), so briefings continue across an OpenCode restart instead of going silent.
  - **Manageable everywhere:** new **Option 9 → OpenCode Agent Progress Briefings** in the MP Reporter menu (`scripts/manage_mp_reporter.sh`) with enable, change interval, disable, send-now, preview, dry run and log; `Mix_Archive_Manager.sh --opencode-progress` and `scripts/mp_scheduled_reporter.sh --status` (which now shows the progress interval and timer state); and `scripts/mp_opencode_progress_reporter.sh {enable [MINUTES]|disable|status|send|force|dry-run|log}`.
  - **Bounded footprint:** a 32 kbps mono MP3 per briefing (about 500 KB for a two-minute report) instead of the multi-megabyte WAV the daily engine archives, no intermediate WAVs left behind, and archives pruned to the newest 288 briefings. Attachments can be switched off individually (`OPENCODE_PROGRESS_ATTACH_TEXT` / `OPENCODE_PROGRESS_ATTACH_AUDIO`) and the audio can be disabled entirely.
  - **Activity only:** a briefing is built and emailed **only while at least one OpenCode session is running**. An idle workstation produces no text report, no audio file and no email.
  - **Configured in `~/MP_REPORTER/email_config.env`:** `OPENCODE_PROGRESS_ENABLED`, `OPENCODE_PROGRESS_INTERVAL_MINUTES="10"`, `OPENCODE_PROGRESS_RECIPIENTS` (empty reuses `EMAIL_RECIPIENTS`), the subject prefix and the per-briefing caps. All keys are documented in `config.env.example`.

### Changed
