- **The MP Harmony AI Assistant now runs on a model that fits the GPU, and answers in fractions of a second instead of minutes** (`scripts/launch_mp_harmony.sh`, `scripts/manage_harmony_agent.sh`, `scripts/install_harmony_agent.sh`, `tests/test_mp_harmony_menu.py`):
  - **Symptom:** every MP Harmony reply took minutes, even for a one-line question. On an RTX 3080 (10 GB) the assistant was essentially unusable.
  - **Cause:** the configured model was OpenAI **gpt-oss-20b** — 20.9B parameters, about **13.8 GB** of weights. That cannot fit in 10 GB of VRAM, so Ollama quietly fell back to `-ngl 0` and ran it **100% on the CPU** (`ollama ps` reported `100% CPU`). A 3.5 GB `OLLAMA_GPU_OVERHEAD` reservation made the shortfall worse. Measured on this machine: **110.9 s** for a trivial arithmetic question.
  - **The model is now NVIDIA Nemotron 3.5 Bolt E4B** (8B total / ~4B effective, ~7.9 GB, quantized GGUF, OpenMDW License 1.1). It loads **100% on the GPU**, uses about 6 GB of VRAM at a 4096 context, and the same question now takes **~0.2–0.5 s**. It supports tool calling and a 128K context window.
  - **The old 20B weights are kept under a new tag, `gpt-oss-20b-legacy:latest`**, so nothing that relied on them is lost.
  - **The model tag stays `gpt-oss-pinned:latest`**, as requested, so existing configs, menu text, the Web HUD and Open WebUI integrations continue to work untouched.
  - **The agent gained a protocol adapter** (`model_speaks_harmony`, `generate_turn_text`, `_convo_to_chat_messages`). MP Harmony speaks OpenAI Harmony wire tokens, which only gpt-oss understands; Nemotron uses a normal chat template. The adapter detects the model family from Ollama (`gemma4` here vs `gpt-oss`) and drives chat-template models through `/api/chat`, translating to and from Harmony text so the tool loop, streaming, speech archive and OpenAI-compatible API all behave exactly as before. Harmony-native models keep the original raw-prompt path.
  - **The agent is a separate project again, not a bundled copy.** `MP_Harmony_Agent/` was removed from the manager tree in 0.5.3: MP Harmony is installed by its own installer (`scripts/install_harmony_agent.sh`, cloned from `github.com/MPlanetarian/MP-Harmony-Agent` into `~/OpenAI/MP_Harmony_Agent`) and the launcher and manager scripts now resolve it there. The systemd user unit `mp-harmony-agent.service` points at that checkout, and the installer writes it when the optional agent is selected.
  - **`OLLAMA_GPU_OVERHEAD` was reduced from 3.5 GB to 512 MB** in `~/.config/systemd/user/ollama.service` (matching `manage_ollama.sh`), freeing VRAM so models can actually stay on the card. The previous unit file is backed up beside it.
  - **Tool routing for plain questions was tightened** (`_DIRECT_QUESTION_BLOCK`) so host-inspection wording such as "how much disk is free", "RAM", "df", `top` and `uptime` takes the tool path instead of being answered from memory.
  - Requires **Ollama 0.40+**; the container was upgraded from 0.34.0 (previous binary kept as `/usr/local/bin/ollama.bak-0.34.0`) because the Nemotron 3.5 manifests reject older clients.

---

  - **Stream of Frequency (Liquid Spectrogram)** (`stream_of_frequency_liquid_eq`) — the same FFT history read as a still pool instead of a river. Newest audio is the bright meniscus on the surface and older audio sinks into a cool blue-black below, with half-block cells for viscosity, a surface water line and caustic glints drifting slowly rightward.
  - **Stream of Frequency (MPlanterian Overview Effect)** (`stream_of_frequency_overview_eq`) — the same history read as a topographic survey from above: the data is rescaled, blurred and drawn as contour lines at fixed elevations, in a warm land-to-snow palette with symmetric ridges, summit markers and a slow satellite sweep.
  - Both sit directly after the original Dynamic Spectrogram in the `e` cycle, so all three readings are reachable in sequence, and all three draw from one shared history buffer — switching with `e` never restarts or reshuffles what is on screen. The original effect is untouched in look and remains the default.
  - Friendly aliases: `liquid`, `liquid_spectrogram`, `liquid_stream` and `overview`, `overview_effect`, `mplanetarian_overview`, `mplanetarian_overview_effect`, `topographic`. Choices persist to `~/.config/mix-manager/eq_style` and are honoured by `--style`/`--eq`.
  - The Micro-mode meter and the Mini/Normal frames each gain a matching miniature: filled fluid cells for the pool, contour marks for the survey.

### Fixed
- **Close MP Mix Manager left the windows open: the tray froze for the whole exit movie, and the sweep only half of it** (`scripts/mp_system_tray.py`, `scripts/windows_tray.ps1`, `tests/test_mp_system_tray.py`):
  - **Symptom:** right-clicking the tray icon and choosing **Close MP Mix Manager (Closes everything)** played `Exit_MP_Video.mp4` and then nothing happened — MP Liquid Player, MP Gui, the console manager and MP Visualizer all stayed on screen. Clicking it again did nothing either.
  - **Cause 1 — the tray was frozen for the movie.** `dispatch()` is a Qt slot, and it called `shutdown_suite()` inline. That function waits out the whole movie (`Exit_MP_Video.mp4` is 40 seconds) with `time.sleep()`, so the Qt event loop was blocked for 40 seconds. The menu stayed painted, the icon stopped answering the pointer, and the desktop treats a blocked StatusNotifier process as hung. On Plasma that is what made the close look like a no-op, and the teardown never got to run or was killed by the compositor. Verified with a real Qt event loop: **0 timer ticks** during the close before the fix.
  - **Cause 2 — the sweep only half the suite.** After the kill script, the tray signalled `manager_pids()` — the console manager alone — and relied on the script's `pgrep -f` patterns for everything else. A manager started the way the tray launches it (`bin/mix-archive-manager`, which execs the script rather than naming it on the command line) matches **none** of those patterns, so it survived. The visualizer and terminal player were likewise only covered by the script.
  - **The close now runs off the GUI thread.** `dispatch()` starts a worker thread and a GUI-thread `QTimer` watches it, so the icon and menu keep responding for the whole movie and `app.quit()` runs only after the windows are actually gone. `QTimer.singleShot` from the worker was rejected on purpose: a timer created on a thread with no event loop never fires, which would have left the tray running forever — that case is now pinned by a test.
  - **The sweep covers everything the tray can see.** New `suite_pids()` signals every classified role (manager, MP Gui, MP Liquid Player) **plus** the visualizer, terminal player/daemon and MIDI companion, so a Close All no longer depends on the kill script's patterns or on how a window was launched.
  - **A verify pass makes the close trustworthy.** After the first sweep, `shutdown_suite()` re-reads the process table for up to 4 s; if anything MP is still there it re-runs the kill script and SIGKILLs the survivors, then confirms. A clean close costs nothing extra — the loop exits on the first empty read.
  - **The same two fixes are in the Windows tray.** `Invoke-MpTrayAction` now runs the movie and teardown in a background runspace with a winforms timer posting the quit, instead of blocking the message loop; `Stop-MpSuite` loops until nothing matches and gained the `mix-archive-manager`, `launch-manager` and `traktor_s4_midi` patterns. The macOS menu bar already dispatched on `DispatchQueue.global` and is unchanged apart from a test that pins it.
  - Proven end to end in a PID namespace (so the patterns could not touch the live session): five stand-in windows — including a manager behind the wrapper and a visualizer and player that a deliberately useless kill script could not stop — are all gone afterwards, and the real Qt tray stayed responsive (91 UI ticks during the close) with the icon still live.
- **A damaged mix no longer stops the stream: the Web Companion skips to the next one** (`scripts/mix_web_companion.py`, `tests/test_web_companion_unplayable_skip.py`, `tests/test_web_audio_tray.py`):
  - **Symptom:** playing `MPlanetarian - Stream of Frequency 006.wav` on a phone showed *"This mix cannot be played on this device … it is empty or damaged on this computer — pick another mix or re-export it."* The mix after it played fine, but the listener had to reach for the playlist by hand. If every mix in a playlist was damaged, there was no way forward at all.
  - **Cause:** the export was interrupted, leaving a 1.9 GB file that begins with 1.9 GB of nulls — no `RIFF` magic, no frames. It was larger than zero bytes, which was the only WAV check the queue made, so it entered the queue and the phone received a body it could not decode. The dashboard read that media error as "the companion restarted", re-attached to the same dead URL, and after two attempts replaced the player with the dead-end message above.
  - **The queue now rejects a WAV whose head is all nulls.** Every WAV variant (`RIFF`, `RF64`, `BW64`, `RIFX`) carries a magic at offset 0, so an all-zero head cannot be valid audio. This is the same failure class as the 44-byte header stub, one layer down, and it is caught at scan time so the file never reaches a phone.
  - **A frame-probe cache written by an older build is discarded** (`FRAME_PROBE_CACHE_VERSION`). The verdict is keyed on `(mtime, size)`, so the dead file had a cached "has audio" answer that the stricter probe would never recompute; the cache file is now versioned and an older one is re-probed once.
  - **The device's own verdict is now used.** When a phone still cannot decode a file (a container the header check cannot judge), it reports `track_failed`; the host records the exact copy that failed in an in-memory ledger and advances the queue. Every poll, the mini playlist and any later phone skip that file, so the same dead URL is never served twice. The ledger is keyed on `(mtime, size)`, so a re-exported or replaced mix gets a fresh chance automatically, and a rescan clears it.
  - **The dashboard skips instead of stopping.** A `MEDIA_ERR_DECODE` / `MEDIA_ERR_SRC_NOT_SUPPORTED` on the real mix now advances to the next one and keeps playing (`skipResumePending`), the skipped row is greyed out for what it is, and the run is bounded by the queue length. When every entry has failed in one run, one message says so — *"None of the mixes in this playlist can be played on this device…"* — and the page stops, instead of looping on the same dead URL.
  - **A file that errors twice does not cost two slots** in that run, so the last mix in a queue is still tried.
  - The "companion has been restarted" banner is now reserved for an actual restart; a damaged file reports itself by name as it is skipped.

- **"Engage Audio Stream" now starts the mix on iPhone** (`scripts/mix_web_companion.py`, `tests/test_web_companion_autoplay.py`):
  - **The tap looked dead because the page's own security policy refused the iOS unlock clip.** Safari only allows audible `play()` from inside the tap's own task, so the engage handler plays a silent WAV first to mark the element as gesture-approved. That clip was a `data:audio/wav` URL, and the dashboard is served with `Content-Security-Policy: … media-src 'self' blob: …`. The policy blocked the clip, so the element failed with `MEDIA_ERR_SRC_NOT_SUPPORTED` before any sound could start.
    - The clip is now built as a `blob:` URL, which the policy allows and which needs no inline data in the page.
    - The media-error handler ignores an error raised while the element is still holding the unlock clip. It used to read that as "this mix cannot be decoded here" and write off the file the listener was about to play — the tap reported nothing and the mix was silently skipped.
  - **The 2 s status poll erased what the tap had just said.** A tap with nothing queued asks the host to rescan and then explains the result ("The host still has no mixes in the archive…"). The next `/api/status` painted the generic "Waiting for the host to queue a mix…" over it, so the answer the listener had just earned blinked away and the button looked broken again. A tap's explanation is now held for 8 s and cleared as soon as audio really starts.
  - **`WEB_COMPANION_AUTOPLAY=false` was reported to the phone as a failure.** That setting only stops the host arming the queue on its own; the queue is still built and `/api/stream` still serves it, so a tap plays normally. The dashboard nevertheless showed **"⏹ Streaming disabled"** and put "Streaming is disabled on the host (WEB_COMPANION_AUTOPLAY)." under the engage button — a failure banner over a working player. It now reads as ready, and the reason is only shown when the stream genuinely cannot play.
  - Five tests pin the behaviour, including a source guard that fails if any `data:` media source is reintroduced, a check that the served `media-src` directive allows the clip, and a check that a tap explanation survives the poll. Reverting the fix makes four of them fail.
  - `StreamQueueCacheTest` no longer leaks a background queue refresh into the next test, which made `test_scan_runs_once_across_repeated_calls` fail about half the time depending on test order.

- **Menu option text follows the selected theme again** (`Mix_Archive_Manager.sh`, `tests/test_menu_text_theme_ink.py`):
  - **Symptom:** every theme showed the menu option text in white. Changing theme recoloured the header, the pane, the menu numbers and the selected row, but the option labels themselves stayed white — the one thing that used to make each theme feel different.
  - **Cause:** 0.4 painted each unselected row with the theme's menu-number hue (`MENU_NUM`). 0.5 replaced that with a fixed `MENU_WHITE` so The Whitest White could flip the ink to black, and in doing so pinned every other theme to white.
  - `set_theme_colors` now publishes `MENU_INK` / `MENU_INK_RGB` alongside the existing tables: the theme's own hue, per theme, exported so sub-shells see it. The three option-row renderers (the scrolling list, the fast repaint path and the non-TTY `Enter choice:` fallback) use it for the bracketed number, the label and its hint.
  - `MENU_WHITE` is kept as the neutral console ink for the two lines that sit directly on the pane rather than on a themed row: the `Selection:` / `Filter / Search:` status line and the player panel's `EQ:` line. It still flips to black on The Whitest White.
  - **Seven themes needed their hue lifted, or the ink would have been unreadable.** `gruvbox` measured 2.1:1 against its own pane, `orbital_void_reflux` 2.5:1, `sensorium_interface` 2.9:1, `nord` 3.1:1, `dracula` 3.7:1, `dreamworlds_ultra` 3.8:1 and `cyberpunk` 3.9:1 — all below the 4.5:1 WCAG AA floor for body text. Those inks are mixed toward white just far enough to clear 4.5:1 (the hue is kept, only its lightness moves); the other twenty-two themes keep their exact accent. Every one of the 29 themes now clears 4.5:1 on its own pane, and the test measures that in the run rather than trusting the table.
  - `sync_theme_from_file` includes `MENU_INK` in its palette signature, so a theme changed from MP Liquid Player repaints the open menu.

### Changed
- **The Linux maintenance page now measures cleanup instead of asserting it** (`Mix_Archive_Manager.sh`, `tests/test_cleanup_suite_is_measured.py`, `README.md`):
  - **Why:** a BleachBit run reported `5.9 GB recovered / 50257 files / 568 errors`, and reading the 568 errors showed 553 of them were one unavoidable mistake — `system.localizations` trying to delete `/usr/share/locale/*` on an immutable ostree host. That is the wrong tool for a Bazzite machine. The built-in page exists for this, but its own suite was not honest.
  - **The Complete Cleanup Suite hid the thumbnail purge inside the `else` branch** of its `ujust clean-system` check. On Bazzite `ujust` exists, so `rm -rf ~/.cache/thumbnails` never ran. Each cleanup step is now its own function called unconditionally.
  - **It called `ujust clean-system`, which is not the whole job.** That recipe covers podman, Flatpak, ostree and Brew but leaves the DNF cache (`573 MB` here) and the per-user journal (`88 MB` here) untouched. The suite now runs `dnf5 clean all` and vacuums both stores.
  - **`sudo journalctl --vacuum-size` only vacuums the system journal.** The user journal is a separate store, so "Vacuum System Logs" was renamed to "Vacuum Journals to 200MB" and now runs `journalctl --user --vacuum-size` as well.
  - **`swapoff -a` was run after a single y/N prompt with no memory check.** Pulling every swapped page back into RAM when less RAM is free than swap in use is an OOM kill, not a cleanup — and this host runs `4.6 GB` of swap on zram. The step now compares `MemAvailable` against the swap in use and refuses with an explanation.
  - **`fstrim -av` could grind for minutes with no output.** `/dev/sdb` reports `discard_granularity 0`, so trim has nothing to do but still walks the whole 929 GB volume. Each mount is now checked against `/sys/class/block/*/queue/discard_granularity` (falling back to the parent disk for partitions), unsupported devices are skipped by name, every trim is bounded by `timeout`, and subvolumes sharing one block device are trimmed once.
  - **`rm -rf` on a cron path is replaced with measured steps.** Every step is wrapped in `_mp_cleanup_step`, which samples `df` before and after and reports `+1.2 GB reclaimed` or `nothing to reclaim`. `mp_do_cleanup_suite` ends with a per-step summary and a net figure, and says plainly that a step can free nothing while the net moves because other processes write to the same filesystem.
  - **Dropping page cache no longer claims disk space.** Freeing page cache frees RAM, not disk; the kernel reclaims it on demand, and forcing it early only makes the next reads slower. The step still runs (it is a legitimate RAM reset) but contributes nothing to the summary.
  - **Pinned Flatpak runtimes are now reported.** `flatpak uninstall --unused` can never remove a pinned ref, so a pin is permanent. This user installation carries `~3.9 GB` of a pinned `aarch64` Platform/Sdk/GL/nvidia set that no x86_64 host can use; the suite lists pinned runtimes, flags those for a foreign CPU, and prints the `flatpak pin --remove` command rather than silently reporting "nothing unused".
  - **Container volumes are opt-in.** `podman image prune` and `ujust clean-system` both ignore volumes, which is where the space sits (`1.136 GB` here). The suite now shows the reclaimable figure and only prunes after an explicit y/N.
  - **`rpm-ostree cleanup -b -m` instead of a bare `cleanup -m`.** `-b` clears temporary files and `-m` the cached repo metadata; `-r` is deliberately absent because it deletes the rollback deployment, which is the only way back from a bad upgrade.
  - `flatpak uninstall` is always called with `--noninteractive -y`, so a cleanup step can never sit waiting on a `(y/N)` prompt.

### Added
- **MP Bass Boost: a four-mode dial for MP Liquid Player and MP Audio Player (CLI)** (`MP_Audio_Player.py`, `tests/test_bass_boost.py`):
  - **What the headphones actually do.** The dial on the left ear cup of the **Skullcandy Crusher ANC 2** does not change the main driver's EQ. It controls the level of a second "Crusher" driver and its dedicated bass amplifier. SoundGuys' lab measurements of the same headphones show the effect in the signal: a broad boost **centred at ~45 Hz**, **+20 dB at 50 Hz** with Crusher at 80 %, a deliberate **taper below ~30 Hz**, and **nothing left by ~500 Hz**, which is why vocals, hi-hats and cymbals stay intact. The dial cycles presets on a press and steps finely when scrolled, and the app can switch Crusher off completely.
  - **The four modes** are `Normal` (Crusher off), `Medium` (⅓ of the headphone's boost), `High` (⅔) and `Extreme` (**100 %** — about **+25 dB at 45 Hz**). Each step is exactly a third of the full response, which is what the three gallery positions on the headphones represent.
  - **Signal path.** A **minimum-phase FIR** (4096 taps, overlap-save FFT) reproduces the measured curve per sample rate, so there is no group delay to drift against the visualizer the way a linear-phase design would. A soft knee above 0.8 replaces hard clipping, so a loud mix is limited rather than shredded. Measured with the shipped filter: 45 Hz +25.0 dB, 50 Hz +24.6 dB, 30 Hz +14.6 dB, 20 Hz +3.0 dB, 100 Hz +9.2 dB, 250 Hz +0.2 dB, 1 kHz 0.0 dB.
  - **UI.** In the Liquid Player (full, mini and micro) a `BASS BOOST: <MODE>` chip sits beside Favorites and Visualization and is clickable; `b` / `B` steps the dial from any view, including while the Equalizer HUD is open. The mode also appears as a chip in the now-playing footer. Colours are distinct per mode — cyan, green, amber, and **red for Extreme** — and the Extreme chip is `48;2;72;0;0`.
  - **Sharing and persistence.** The engine publishes `bass_mode`, `bass_modes`, `bass_peak_db` and `bass_percent` in its status file, so every attached window and the CLI agree, and the choice is saved to `~/.config/mix-manager/bass_boost` for the next launch. IPC adds `set_bass_mode`, `cycle_bass`, `bass_down` and `get_bass`; the CLI adds `--bass-mode`, `--cycle-bass` and `--list-bass-modes`, and `--status-line` now prints the mode.
  - **`b` is no longer an Exit key.** It was a second Exit shortcut in the very first CLI player. Exit is `Esc`, `q`, `x` or `Ctrl+C`; the footer hints were updated everywhere they said `b/Esc`, and `tests/test_player_queue.py` now pins that `b` cycles the bass mode without closing the window.
  - **The button does not cost the CLI its playlist.** The chip first got a row of its own, which at the default 24-row console took the line out of the playlist: the list was left with **zero visible rows**, so the arrow keys moved a cursor nobody could see (three existing arrow-navigation tests caught it). The bass control now shares the Favorites row — `★ FAVORITES  0 saved   BASS BOOST: NORMAL   [3] open  [H] mark  [b] bass` — and the CLI form is a **text-only label** in the mode's ink, because that row is deliberately never a filled block (`tests/test_player_favorites.py` pins it). The Liquid hosts keep the filled chip beside VISUALIZATION. The clickable span is measured from the drawn prefix, so a click lands on the chip at 55, 70 and 90 columns (it did not at first: a hand-derived offset was three columns out).
  - **Mini and Micro no longer clip their bottom row.** Both had a pre-existing overflow — the equaliser block returns more rows than the height it is asked for, which pushed the `[G] MP Gui` hint off a 14-row Mini window (fails at the pre-bass commit too). Each renderer now reserves the footer rows and keeps only the visualiser rows that fit, and `_fit_frame()` trims any remaining excess **from the middle** so the bottom hint row always survives. 60 frame shapes — CLI and Liquid, 3 libraries, 24/40-row full, 14/16-row Mini, 7/9/12-row Micro — now all fit.
  - Not reproducible in software: the physical head-rattle, which comes from the second driver moving air. Everything the audio signal itself contributes is emulated.
- **The last MP Gui or MP Liquid Player window plays an exit movie before it closes** (`Exit_MP_Video.mp4`, `scripts/exit_video.py`, `scripts/mp_instance_limit.py`, all four Liquid Glass hosts, `tests/test_exit_video.py`):
  - `ScreenRecorderProject8.mkv` from `~/Videos` is converted to **`Exit_MP_Video.mp4`** in the install tree (`H.264` + `AAC`, faststart, `470x878`, 39.5 s). The MP4 copy is what ships: a host that has no Matroska support can still play it, and MP4 is the format the startup logo already uses.
  - It is played by **the same script and the same player flags as `MP_loader.mp4`** (`scripts/display_loader_video.sh --exit`), so the borderless always-on-top window, the `ffplay`/`mpv`/`vlc`/WPF/s macOS AppKit fallback order, and the skip keys are identical. The launch and wait code was extracted into `_launch_borderless_video` / `_wait_for_video_player` and the startup logo now uses the same functions, so the two movies cannot drift apart.
  - The movie runs **while the closing window is still on screen**, and the host keeps drawing frames underneath it (`_pump_exit_frame`), so the glass does not freeze for the movie's whole length. The window is destroyed only after the movie ends, is skipped, or hits `EXIT_VIDEO_MAX_SECONDS` (default 90 s).
  - **Only the last window plays it.** MP Gui and MP Liquid Player share one counter: closing one of two open windows is silent, and the movie waits for the very last window of either kind. The election lives in `mp_instance_limit.is_last_window()`: each closing host writes a `closing/<pid>.closing` marker and counts the remaining windows under one lock, so two windows closed at the same instant elect exactly one — the first still sees the second, only the second sees none. Markers are dropped on `finish()` and stale ones are discarded when their pid is gone or older than five minutes.
  - The tracklist viewer, the embed check, MP Liquid Visualizer, and the console manager never play it.
  - The **tray's Close MP Mix Manager item plays it too** (`scripts/mp_system_tray.py`, `scripts/windows_tray.ps1`). That item kills every MP process at once, so the windows never reach their own shutdown path; the tray now plays the movie first and stops the suite only once it ends. An `instances/suite.closing` marker, set for the length of that close, makes the windows being stopped stay silent, so one close never plays it twice. The same path covers the macOS menu bar and the Web Companion's "close all".
  - `MP_INSTANCE_PROC_TABLE=empty` forces the counter to see no live windows. It is what lets the two subprocess-based instance tests pass while the user has a player open; normal runs never set it.
  - `SHOW_EXIT_VIDEO="true"`, `EXIT_VIDEO_FILE="Exit_MP_Video.mp4"` and `EXIT_VIDEO_MAX_SECONDS="90"` are read from `config.env` **without sourcing it**, since that file is user-editable; an environment variable of the same name wins. Both settings are documented in `config.env.example`.
  - The exit movie does not bind `G` to reopen MP Gui (the loader does); a closing window should not relaunch what the user just closed.

### Changed
- **Navigation highlighting colours restored from 0.4** (`Mix_Archive_Manager.sh`, `tests/test_nav_bar_same_in_both_windows.py`, `tests/test_menu_number_theme_color.py`, `tests/test_whitest_white_theme.py`):
  - The highlighted menu row steps through a four-colour rotation as the user moves down a menu: **Pink** `247;37;133`, **Blue** `0;229;255`, **Orange** `255;140;0`, **Grey** `88;98;118`, then back to Pink. The rotation is keyed on the row's position among the selectable options, so every menu cycles in the same order.
  - `highlight_menu_line()` is the 0.4 implementation copied verbatim. It also restores the tinted `▶ ❯` pointer beside the row, the brighter pointer shade per colour, and the breathing animation dimming both the fill and the pointer together.
  - `cosmic_supernova` keeps its own four hues (Orchid, Aurora Mint, Solar Gold, Nebula Indigo) and `retro_terminal` stays plain white on black, exactly as in 0.4.
  - This replaces the single theme-wide `SEL_BAR_*` fill that 0.5 introduced for MP Gui/MP Liquid Player colour symmetry. Those tables are still published and still drive the player; only the manager's selected row uses the rotation again.

### Fixed
- **MP Auto Tester: the cross-machine copy silently stopped syncing and hijacked Fedora's desktop launcher** (`MP_Auto_Tester_Flash.sh`, `README.md`, `tests/test_mp_auto_tester.py`):
  - The other Bazzite install is a udisks mount whose path moved from `/run/media/system/bazzite_raid/...` to `/run/media/<user>/bazzite_raid/...`. `--sync-other` had the old path hard-coded, so it did nothing and the second machine kept a stale copy. It now probes the known mount points (and still honours `MP_OTHER_BAZZITE`).
  - The `REPORT_SCRIPT` written into the other machine's `DAILY_REPORT_TASK_005_AUTO_TESTER.conf` was **this** host's mount path, which does not exist on that machine. The scheduler there would have failed every morning. It is now rewritten as that machine's own `/home/<user>/...` path, and the 10:10 systemd timer units are installed alongside it (previously only the task file was copied, so nothing ever ran).
  - A test run invoked `install.sh`, which republished `~/.local/bin/mp-desktop-exec` into the throwaway test tree. On Fedora that repointed the **real** desktop launchers at a directory that is deleted after the run, so MP Gui / Liquid Player / Visualizer / Mix Manager stopped opening from the desktop. The harness now snapshots the user's `~/.local/bin` shims before the suite runs and restores them afterwards, and the suite runs with `MP_RUN_SLOW_INSTALLER_TESTS=0`.
  - Fedora's `mp-desktop-exec` link was restored to `~/MP_MANAGER_TESTER/bin/mp-desktop-exec`.
  - The README also lost its Auto Tester section in the 0.5.3 release merge; the feature row and the spotlight were restored.
- **KVM is loaded at boot, and the VM launchers no longer depend on one fixed NIC or mount path** (`/etc/modules-load.d/kvm.conf`, `/home/linuxbrew/.linuxbrew/bin/qemu-system-x86_64`, `~/VMs/WIN11_TESTBED/net-up.sh`, `~/VMs/X299ROGFREEBSD/start_vm.sh`, `MP_Auto_Tester_Flash.sh`):
  - The host has 32 CPUs with VT-x and both `kvm.ko`/`kvm-intel.ko` installed, but nothing loaded them, so `/dev/kvm` was absent and both VM test beds failed with `Could not access KVM kernel module`. A `/etc/modules-load.d/kvm.conf` now loads `kvm` and `kvm_intel` through `systemd-modules-load` on every boot.
  - The `qemu-system-x86_64` shim hard-coded its private library directory to `/run/media/system/bazzite_raid/...`. That mount moved, so every QEMU invocation died with `libxenctrl.so.4.21: cannot open shared object file`. The shim now probes the known mount points, requiring the actual soname so an empty stale mount cannot be selected.
  - The Windows 11 `net-up.sh` hard-coded `UPLINK=eno1`; this host boots onto Wi-Fi (`wlp2s0`) when the wired NIC is down, which aborted the launch with `Device "eno1" does not exist`. It now auto-detects the default-route interface and falls back to the first LAN-addressed NIC, matching what the FreeBSD launcher already did.
  - The FreeBSD launcher routed and NAT'ed `192.0.2.14`, but the current `X299ROGFREEBSD.qcow2` configures itself as `192.0.2.16`, so a running VM looked unreachable. Both addresses are now routed and NAT'ed, the default is `.16`, and the tester resolves the live address before deciding a system is down.
  - FreeBSD's SDDM had no autologin, so the guest sat at the login greeter and every screenshot was the same boot console. `/usr/local/etc/sddm.conf.d/autologin.conf` now logs `mplanetarian` into `plasmax11`.

- **Every MP Visualizer preset except the player's saved one rendered the wrong scene** (`scripts/mp_liquid_visualizer_vulkan.py`, `scripts/visualizer_player_bond.py`, `tests/test_visualizer_preset_override.py`):
  - **Symptom:** presets 11 and 12 (and in fact every preset) drew the same flat, banded "river" and had no 3D geometry. It was reported as "presets 11 and 12 are broken", but the scene was always Dynamic Waves P2 whatever was selected.
  - **Cause:** the Liquid Player publishes its own `preset_id` in `~/.config/mix-manager/liquid_player_visualizer.json`, and the player-bond sync copied it onto the visualizer on its first poll. That is correct for the companion window the player spawns, but it also overrode a preset the user had just chosen, so every window snapped back to P2 (id 12). The shader was never at fault: forcing a different `u.preset.x` produced a byte-identical frame, which is what proved the value never reached the branch.
  - An explicitly requested preset now pins the window. `LiquidVisualizerVulkanRenderer(name)` sets `_preset_pinned`, and `_sync_bonded_preset` leaves a pinned window alone. A companion with no named preset still follows the player, and `--preset random` is deliberately *not* pinned, because three launchers pass that and pinning it would freeze the shuffle on its first pick.
  - **`--self-test` ignored `--preset`** (`scripts/mp_liquid_visualizer_vulkan.py`). It saved the choice and then rendered whatever was already saved, so a `--preset N --self-test` capture was always the previous scene. The argument is now forwarded.
  - **The UBO staging buffer was four floats short** (`scripts/mp_liquid_visualizer_vulkan.py`). It was allocated `F32 * 36` while `VisualizerUBO` is 40 floats, so the entire `stream` vec4 — the PID phase and heat the river presets read — was dropped on the path where the mapped buffer is unavailable. It is now sized from `sizeof(VisualizerUBO)`.
  - Verified by rendering presets 1, 2, 3, 11, 12 and 13 with the player still bonded to P2: six distinct images, each on the expected id. `tests/test_visualizer_preset_override.py` pins the pin rule, the random exemption, the self-test forward and the UBO size.

### Changed
- **The PLAYING badge in MP Liquid Player is now black, not green** (`MP_Audio_Player.py`, `tests/test_theme_notice_is_black.py`):
  - The `▶ PLAYING` badge (full, mini and micro frames) used traffic-green. It is now black on every theme whose glass pane is pale, matching the "Color Theme" notice above.
  - It shares that decision through one helper, `dark_glass_ink()`, so the badge and the notice cannot drift apart. PAUSED stays amber and STOPPED stays red, so the three states remain distinguishable at a glance.
  - The two charcoal-pane themes (**Retro Linux Memories**, **Into Darkness Void Out**) keep the light ink, because black there is 1.7:1 and 1.0:1 — invisible.
  - The visualizer brightness guard now preserves *any* pure-black run rather than only lines that contain the notice ink. The badge shares its line with the pink title and the cyan format, so a line-level check alone would have left it light grey whenever the MP Liquid Visualizer was active.

- **The "Color Theme" notice in MP Liquid Player is now black, not green** (`MP_Audio_Player.py`, `tests/test_theme_notice_is_black.py`):
  - Pressing **C** (or changing the theme from another window) prints `Color Theme: <name>` just below the equaliser. It used the same traffic-green as every other toast, so the theme name came out green; it is now black on every theme whose glass pane is pale.
  - Black rather than the theme's own accent on purpose: that pane is the same light surface across those themes, so black is the one ink that stays readable on all of them. A per-theme accent washed out on the pale palettes and clashed with the rest.
  - Two themes paint the opaque charcoal pane instead — **Retro Linux Memories** (black would be 1.7:1) and **Into Darkness Void Out** (1.0:1, i.e. invisible). Those keep the light ink every other body line already uses there. `tests/test_theme_notice_is_black.py` asserts the exception list matches `theme_uses_dark_glass()` so the two cannot drift apart.
  - `_notify_theme()` replaces the inline `_notify(f"Color Theme: …")` at both theme-change sites and sets a flag, so the three renderers (full, mini, micro) colour the line from one helper instead of re-deriving it from the message text.
  - The visualizer brightness guard, which lifts dark inks to about `#d1d1d1`, now leaves a line that asks for pure black alone. Without that the notice would have rendered light grey instead of black.

- **`MP_Auto_Tester_Flash.sh` is now internal-only and gitignored** (`.gitignore`, `tests/test_mp_auto_tester.py`):
  - The controller is a Dreamworlds Productions internal dev utility. It is not shipped to users and is no longer published to the repository: the file stays in the working tree, but `git add -A` will not pick it up.
  - It carries this studio's private test-bed topology — LAN addresses for the Mac mini and Fedora workstation, the Windows 11 and FreeBSD VM paths, and the VNC/SMB credentials used to drive them — which is internal infrastructure detail rather than end-user functionality.
  - The report builder (`scripts/mp_auto_tester_report.py`), the MP Reporter bridge (`scripts/mp_auto_tester_report.sh`) and the Windows agent (`scripts/mp_win_agent_server.py`) remain **tracked**, because they ship and the daily briefing uses them.
  - `tests/test_mp_auto_tester.py` is tracked, so the assertions that read the controller's source now skip with a clear reason when the file is absent. On this studio machine they still run and all 28 pass; from a public clone the suite reports `28 run, 0 failed, 8 skipped` instead of 10 hard failures.
  - The bridge already guarded its optional local sweep with `[ -x "$ROOT/MP_Auto_Tester_Flash.sh" ]`, so a checkout without the controller still runs its tracked tests and reports cleanly.

### Added
- **The installer opens with the MP Mix Manager brand logo** (`install.sh`):
  - `install.sh` now prints a white block-letter wordmark as its very first output, before any status line, option parsing message or platform probe. The logo is the first thing a user sees when they run the installer, so the branding is unmistakable.
  - The wordmark is a six-row "MP MIX MANAGER" block-letter bitmap built from the full block `█` plus the double-line box-drawing glyphs (`╔`, `╗`, `╚`, `╝`, `═`, `║`), so every letter has double-line stems, solid counters and a uniform letter height. Each row is a single `printf` line, every stroke is bright white (`\033[1;97m`), and the artwork matches `MP_Mix_Manager_ASCII_Logo.txt` byte for byte.
  - `print_brand_logo()` is defined next to the other colour constants and called unconditionally at the top of the script, so it also appears for `--help`, `--reinstall` and `--dry-run`.
  - Tests that read `install.sh` still parse it: `bash -n` passes and the reinstall/windows/VR suites are unaffected.

### Fixed
- **The Web Companion no longer needs an access token — open its URL and it works** (`scripts/mix_web_companion.py`, `scripts/mp_companion_security.py`, `scripts/mix_web_companion.sh`, `tests/test_web_companion_security.py`):
  - 0.5.1 added a per-install bearer token to the companion. It closed a real hole, but it put a secret between a phone and its own stereo: opening `http://192.0.2.11:8888` produced `{"error": "unauthorized", "detail": "This companion requires an access token. Open the URL printed by the service, or set MP_COMPANION_TOKEN."}`, and the only copy of that token was in the systemd journal on the other machine. `0.5.4` briefly added an unlock form and a `token` command; both were the wrong answer to "why do I have to type a password to play my own music?". The token is now gone entirely.
  - **No credential is required for anything.** The dashboard, every `/api/` endpoint, the audio stream and the agent proxy all answer a device that can reach the port. The printed URL is just `http://<host>:8888/`.
  - **The real protections are unchanged.** `/api/stream?file=` still refuses any path outside a library root (`/etc/passwd`, `config.env` and an SSH key are all `404`), and mutating requests still need the `X-MP-Companion` header plus a same-origin `Origin`, so another web site cannot drive playback. Losing the token does not reopen either hole: it was defence in depth on top of containment, not what made containment work.
  - **The LAN allowlist is the access control.** `WEB_COMPANION_ALLOW_CIDR` remains, and the startup banner now says plainly that the port is open to the network and points at that setting. Binding stays loopback unless `WEB_COMPANION_HOST` asks otherwise, so a laptop on café Wi-Fi is still not exposed by default.
  - `--print-token`, `--rotate-token` and `--no-auth` are still accepted but hidden and inert, so an existing unit file, launcher or bookmark that passes one starts instead of dying with "unrecognized arguments". `--print-token` prints a pointer to the URL rather than a secret.
  - `mix_web_companion.sh token` is now a signpost that prints the dashboard URL, and `start`/`status` no longer mention a token. Any leftover `~/.config/mix-manager/companion.token` is deleted on the next start so a stale credential does not sit there looking meaningful.
  - A bookmark that still carries a dead `?token=…` keeps working, and the page strips the stale value from the address bar.
  - The security suite was rewritten for this model: 46 tests now pin that no token is required, that an empty allowlist admits everyone while a configured one rejects outside peers, that containment and CSRF still refuse, and that a reintroduced `COMPANION_TOKEN`/`COMPANION_REQUIRE_AUTH` fails loudly rather than silently passing.


- **The transparency bar no longer floats over other applications** (`scripts/liquid_glass_slider.py`, `tests/test_liquid_glass_slider_stacking.py`):
  - **The bar was an override-redirect window, so nothing could restack it.** It was created as `Gtk.WindowType.POPUP`, which GTK makes override-redirect. The window manager never positions or restacks such a window, so the bar sat at the very top of the X stack for its entire life: as soon as the user switched to any other window, the bar stayed drawn on top of it. Reproduced on KWin/XWayland with an unrelated window focused — the bar was at stack index **23** while that window was at index **4**.
  - **It is now a managed toplevel made transient for the player.** The window manager stacks it with the player instead of above everything, so the bar is over the glass while the player has focus and goes behind whatever the user switches to. Verified live: with another window focused the bar dropped to index **4** against that window's **5**, and activating the player again brought the bar back in front, at index **5** against the player's **3**.
  - **It still takes clicks.** The window keeps its full input region, so the press, release and motion events that drag the thumb are unchanged; the only thing that differed was who is allowed to stack it.
  - **It is also mapped only once positioned.** A managed window is mapped by the window manager at a spot it picks, so `show_all()` up front would flash the bar there before `place()` moved it onto the reserved strip. The widgets are realised immediately but the window is unmapped until `place()` knows the geometry. `place()` now also submits a move or resize only when the geometry actually changed, because a managed move is a window-manager request rather than a bare X call and the host calls it on every frame.
  - Fourteen tests pin the window type, the transient parent, the no-focus hints, the deferred mapping and the geometry throttling, plus a source guard that fails if `Gtk.WindowType.POPUP` is ever put back. Reintroducing the popup fails two of them.

- **The transparency slider now changes the window, in MP Liquid Player and MP Gui** (`scripts/liquid_glass_opengl.py`, `scripts/liquid_glass_vulkan.py`, `MP_Audio_Player_Liquid_Glass.py`, `MP_Audio_Player_Liquid_Glass_OpenGL.py`, `MP_Audio_Player_Liquid_Glass_OpenGL_macOS.py`, `tests/test_liquid_glass_transparency_slider.py`):
  - Dragging the bar from left to right moved the thumb but left the glass at the same transparency. Two independent defects caused it, and either one alone was enough:
    - **The hosts wrote the wrong variable.** `draw()` republishes the window's opacity from `renderer.clear_opacity` on every frame. The Vulkan host updated only its own `clear_opacity`, and the OpenGL host updated only `renderer.slider_val` (the value that paints the thumb). Neither touched the value the renderer actually reads, so a drag was overwritten within one frame. Both hosts now write `renderer.clear_opacity` and clear the "already published" memo so the next frame applies it immediately.
    - **The publish was gated on the animation.** All four copies of the republish block were guarded by `factor != 1.0`, which is the rainbow theme's oscillation. Every static theme therefore never wrote the property at all, so the slider could not have had any effect there regardless of the host. The guard is gone; the comparison is against the value actually published, so a change always lands and an unchanged value still costs nothing (no 60 fps property writes).
  - The four copied blocks are now one shared `apply_clear_opacity(factor)` method on each renderer, so the two hosts cannot drift apart again.
  - Nine tests drive the real host methods and the real renderer method on both backends, including a left-to-right drag over the full range, a static-theme publish, and a source guard that fails if a fifth copy of the `factor != 1.0` guard is ever reintroduced. Reverting the fix makes three of them fail, which is the evidence they pin the bug rather than the implementation.

- **"Engage Audio Stream" now plays on iPhone, and the macOS archive grant sticks** (`scripts/mix_web_companion.py`, `scripts/mix_web_companion.sh`, `tests/test_web_companion_autoplay.py`, `tests/test_web_companion_macos_service.py`):
  - **The phone button did nothing when the host queue was empty.** The listed archive lives on a removable disk, and its mount point changes between boots (`/run/media/<user>/WD BLACK B`, then `/mnt/X299ROG_WD_BLACK`). `MIX_ARCHIVE_DIR` in `config.env` still named the old path, so `_archive_dirs()` resolved only the empty placeholder folders inside the install tree, the queue was empty, and the "no mix queued" branch returned without telling the listener anything. The tap looked dead.
    - `_archive_dirs()` now falls back to the mount roots each platform actually uses (`/run/media/*/*/MIX_ARCHIVE`, `/media/*/*/MIX_ARCHIVE`, `/mnt/*/MIX_ARCHIVE`, `/Volumes/*/MIX_ARCHIVE`) whenever the configured folders contain no audio. A replugged drive is found again with no rewrite of `config.env`.
    - An empty install-tree placeholder no longer counts as "the archive". That was the reason the fallback never ran; `_dir_is_archive_candidate()` requires at least one audio file.
    - `WEB_COMPANION_ARCHIVE_AUTODETECT=0` pins discovery to the configured folders for a machine whose `/mnt` holds unrelated `MIX_ARCHIVE` trees.
    - An Engage tap with no queue now asks the host to rescan and reports what is left, instead of returning silently.
  - **The stream element was not `playsinline`.** iOS can hand a non-`playsinline` element to the system player, so playback left the page and the overlay never cleared.
  - **The iOS unlock ran outside the tap.** A rescan completed asynchronously, so the later `play()` was no longer inside the gesture and Safari rejected it. A silent `play()` now runs in the tap's own task, before the first await.
  - **macOS showed Full Disk Access as granted while refusing the archive.** Three defects stacked:
    - The bundle was signed and *then* had `Contents/Resources/install_dir` written into it, so `codesign --verify` reported "a sealed resource is missing or invalid". TCC compares the bundle's requirement against what is on disk, and a broken seal stops matching the recorded grant. Everything in the bundle is now written first and `seal_bundle()` runs last.
    - Ad-hoc signing without `-r` derives the requirement from the cdhash, so every rebuild produced a new identity while TCC kept the old one. The requirement is now pinned to `designated => identifier "com.mplanetarian.mp-web-companion"`, which survives a rebuild.
    - `compile_bundle_launcher()` could bake `dlopen("")` when `PYTHON_LIB` did not resolve. The launcher then fell back to `execl`, which replaces the process image with the interpreter, so macOS judged the archive read as "Python" and the bundle's grant never applied. An empty or missing libpython now keeps the shell entry point instead of building a launcher that silently loses the identity.
    - `start` re-seals a bundle whose seal does not verify, so an install damaged by the old order repairs itself.
  - **A slow disk was reported as a privacy block.** `_dir_readable()` gave up after 2 s, but macOS answers a *denied* read with `EINTR` when the TCC watchdog expires after about five seconds, so every denial was recorded as a timeout and a spun-down USB disk produced the same "allow Full Disk Access" advice. The probe now waits past the watchdog, classifies `EACCES`/`EINTR` as a refusal and anything else as a stall, and two different messages are sent to the phone.
  - **A rescan forgot the negative probe.** After the user granted access, the cached verdict kept the folder skipped, so the phone still said "blocked" until the service restarted.
  - **Probes stacked.** Each scan started its own probe thread and a denied read parks for the whole watchdog window, so a polling dashboard raised one TCC consent request per poll. Only one probe per folder may now be outstanding.
  - `test_web_companion_autoplay` empties `ARCHIVE_MOUNT_GLOBS` so the suite never walks the developer's real archive. Before this the queue tests read 900+ real mixes and the file took minutes.

- **The Web Companion no longer answers "unauthorized" after you open its printed URL** (`scripts/mix_web_companion.py`, `tests/test_web_companion_security.py`):
  - Opening `http://<host>:8888/?token=…` served the dashboard, but every poll the page then made (`/api/status`, `/api/eq`, `/api/playlist`, …) came back `{"error": "unauthorized"}` and the phone sat on a dead page. The `?token=` visit was meant to leave an `mp_companion_token` cookie behind; that promotion lived *after* `is_authorized()` had already returned `True`, so the early `return True` skipped it and the cookie was never sent. A page load and a `fetch()` are different requests, and only the former carries the query string, so the second request had no credential at all.
  - Promotion now happens on the accepted request, which is the only place it can run. A `Bearer` client is still left alone (it manages its own credential and gets no `Set-Cookie`), and a wrong token still gets a plain 401.
  - `HEAD` now goes through the same allowlist and token gate as every other method. It previously answered `200` to an unauthenticated caller, which exposed the route table and an existence oracle around the token.
  - The per-request header list is reset in `handle_one_request()`. It was a mutable class attribute shared by every keep-alive connection on a threaded server, so one phone's `Set-Cookie` could be emitted on another phone's response.
  - Four tests drive the real handler over a loopback socket: the token URL must set the cookie, the promoted cookie must authorise a later poll, a Bearer request must not be downgraded to a cookie, and `HEAD` must require the token.

- **Up/Down arrow keys browse the playlist again in MP Audio Player** (`MP_Audio_Player.py`, `tests/test_player_arrow_navigation.py`):
  - **Down did nothing.** Commit `46b1ecd` (the Favorites DJ Set exporter) split the shared Down binding into a plain `j` plus a `Shift+J` export branch and dropped `\x1b[B` and `\x1bOB` with it. Up and `k` kept working, so the cursor could only move up, and from a playlist that starts on the first mix it looked completely frozen: every Enter replayed mix 1. Both escape sequences are back, so ↑/↓ navigate and `j`/`k` still scroll.
  - **A held arrow did nothing.** One `read()` returns several sequences while a key repeats (`\x1b[B\x1b[B\x1b[B`), and `_process_key` compared that whole burst against each single-key binding, so it matched nothing and every press in it was discarded. `split_key_burst()` now splits a burst into individual key presses, keeps a partly-received sequence for the next read, and leaves plain text in one piece, so holding ↓ walks the list and browsing does not stutter.
  - `Enter` still plays the highlighted row; the tests drive the real `_draw_frame` and read which playlist row carries the selection bar, so a cursor that moves in memory without painting is a failure.

- **MPlanetarian Dreamworlds [Default] is back to its original palette** (`Mix_Archive_Manager.sh`, `scripts/view_tracklist_console.sh`, `tests/test_menu_number_theme_color.py`):
  - A 0.5 change (`6f01a82`) had recoloured theme `1` from the original Sweet Hot Pink / Electric Cyan / Amber Gold / Lavender & Violet set to a flat Azure Blue, and relabelled the menu row `[Azure Blue on Black]`. The palette is restored to `237;37;78` / `0;229;255` / `255;170;0` / `155;89;182` / `247;37;133` / `0;193;228`, with menu numbers back to `255;20;129`.
  - The tracklist console (`scripts/view_tracklist_console.sh`, also published as `bin/view-tracklist`) carries the same table and is restored with it, so the viewer matches the menu again.
  - `tests/test_menu_number_theme_color.py` expects `255;20;129` for `dreamworlds` again.

- **A fresh install starts on MPlanetarian Dreamworlds [Default]** (`install.sh`, `MP_Gui.sh`, `scripts/launch_mp_gui.bat`, `tests/test_mp_gui.py`):
  - `install.sh` writes `dreamworlds` to `~/.config/mix-manager/theme` when that file does not exist yet, and prints the theme it selected. The file is shared by the CLI manager, MP Gui, the Liquid Player and the tracklist viewer, so every window opens on the default theme.
  - An existing `theme` file is never touched, so updates and reinstalls keep the theme the user chose with **C**.
  - MP Gui and the Windows GUI launcher fall back to `dreamworlds` instead of Retro Linux Memories when no theme has been saved, and they now preserve a saved theme instead of overwriting it on every launch.

- **Loader-video test passes on a symlinked home** (`tests/test_loader_video.py`):
  - `test_stale_borderless_binary_does_not_wait_for_swiftc` compared the played video path against `str(BASE_DIR / "MP_loader.mp4")`. `BASE_DIR` resolves `/home` to its physical `/var/home`, while the child bash inherits the logical `PWD` and prints `/home/...`, so two spellings of the same file failed a literal `assertIn` on Fedora/Bazzite hosts where `/home` is a symlink.
  - The test now extracts the played path and compares `Path(...).resolve()` on both sides, so it still rejects a genuinely wrong file while accepting either spelling of the right one.

- **`tests/run_tests.py` no longer reports a broken module as a green run** (`tests/run_tests.py`):
  - A child process that exits non-zero without printing a `FAIL:`/`ERROR:` line (a signal, a segfault, or a crash during interpreter teardown) used to print `FAIL <module>` and then report `0 failed` and exit `0`. It is now counted as `<module> (module exited rc=N without a result)`, so the run always fails when a module does.

---

### Originally documented for the November 13 build

> The notes below were published with the first 0.5.3 cut on November 13. They are kept verbatim as the
> record of that build; everything above shipped later and is included in the November 14 release.

### Added
- **MP Visualizer button at the bottom of the Web Companion** (`scripts/mp_visualizer_control.py`, `scripts/mp_suite_control.py`, `scripts/mix_web_companion.py`, `scripts/mp_liquid_visualizer_vulkan.py`, `tests/test_web_companion_visualizer.py`):
  - The dashboard's launch pad had four suite buttons that focus an already-open app. The new full-width **MP Visualizer** button under the grid is a three-state control over **exactly one** window: the first press opens it (windowed), the second takes that same window fullscreen, and the third closes it again.
  - The window is identified by a random instance token that is passed in its environment **and** stamped on its command line (`--web-instance`), and the pid is published to `~/.config/mix-manager/mp_web_visualizer.json` once its window is on screen. Every command is a signal sent to that one pid — never to a process group and never by process name — so an MP Visualizer the user opened from the tray, a desktop shortcut or the Liquid Player is never toggled and never closed.
  - The published state carries `fullscreen`, so a tap always follows what the window is really doing. Pressing **Z** inside the window and then tapping the button does the right thing: the button does not count taps, it reads the mode.
  - A "pending" record is written the moment the window is launched, so a double tap during the startup delay can never open a second window (a threaded server can deliver both taps at once, so the decision is also serialised by a lock). It expires after 45 s, and a record whose pid is gone is cleared automatically.
  - The window the dashboard owns opens detached (`MP_VISUALIZER_DETACHED=1`): it does not bond behind the Liquid Player, which would pin it to the player's rectangle and refuse the fullscreen toggle.
  - `SIGUSR1` toggles fullscreen and `SIGUSR2`/`SIGTERM` close the window. The handlers are installed before the window is created (their defaults would kill the process) and only set flags; the render loop applies them on its own thread, so GLFW is never touched from a signal handler. The close path escalates to `SIGKILL` if the window is wedged, still against the recorded pid only.

- **TTY Audio Mode disables every desktop launcher in the Web Companion** (`scripts/mp_suite_control.py`, `scripts/mix_web_companion.py`, `tests/test_web_companion_visualizer.py`):
  - While `~/.config/mix-manager/tty_audio_mode` (or `MP_MIX_MANAGER_TTY_MODE=1`) is set, the manager is running in the text console and every MP desktop window is deliberately closed. The launch pad now renders all five buttons disabled, greys them out, and shows a **MP Mix Manager TTY Audio Mode** banner explaining that they are disabled and that returning to the desktop re-enables them.
  - The lock is enforced on the host as well as in the page: `launch_app`, `close_all_apps` and `web_visualizer_action` all return `tty_mode` without spawning anything, so a stale page or a scripted POST cannot open a window from the console either. The status payload reports `tty_mode` and `launch_disabled` so the dashboard and any other client agree.

- **MP Auto Tester: one command runs every test system and photographs all four shortcuts** (`MP_Auto_Tester_Flash.sh`, `scripts/mp_auto_tester_report.py`, `scripts/mp_auto_tester_report.sh`, `scripts/mp_win_agent_server.py`):
  - `MP_Auto_Tester_Flash.sh` runs the full unit-test suite on the local Bazzite workstation, the Fedora 45 workstation, the macOS mini, the Windows 11 VM and the FreeBSD 15.1 VM, launches the four MP desktop shortcuts on each system, waits at least **20 seconds** per application, and captures `TEST_MP_VISUALISER_<HOSTNAME>.jpeg`, `TEST_MP_GUI_<HOSTNAME>.jpeg`, `TEST_MP_LIQUID_PLAYER_<HOSTNAME>.jpeg` and `TEST_MP_MIX_MANAGER_<HOSTNAME>.jpeg`.
  - Screenshots are filed under `~/MP_TESTER_MIX_MANAGER_SCREENSHOTS/<stamp>/<system>/`, and a **MP_TESTER_MIX_MANAGER_SCREENSHOTS** desktop shortcut (`.desktop` on Linux, `.lnk` on Windows, Finder alias on macOS) is created on every system and opens the archive.
  - Remote systems run the same script as a `--agent`; the controller deploys only the application, tests, scripts, themes and shaders (32 MB rather than the whole 130 MB tree), pulls the results and screenshots back, and reports unreachable systems instead of failing the run.
  - The Windows 11 VM is driven through the guest's existing `C:\MP_AGENT\agent.ps1` HTTP task queue (`scripts/mp_win_agent_server.py`), which is reliable across focus and keyboard-layout changes, and its framebuffer is captured over QMP.
  - `scripts/mp_auto_tester_report.py` renders a PDF with every screenshot embedded plus a text summary; `scripts/mp_auto_tester_report.sh` is the MP Reporter bridge that prints the `Report generated:` line MP Reporter parses, so the **Stephen Hawking voice briefing** covers the run.
  - Registered as **`DAILY_REPORT_TASK_005_AUTO_TESTER`** (`10:10` daily) so the briefing is part of the daily report, and `scripts/mp_daily_email_reporter.py` now keeps the Auto Tester PDF as its own dedup family and attaches the latest run's screenshots to the daily email.
  - Capture is verified, not assumed: each screenshot waits for a painted window (`frame_is_populated`) and then the full settle time, so a fresh GTK glass frame is never photographed blank.

- **Left/Right arrow keys switch MP Visualizer presets, wrapping at both ends** (`MP_Audio_Player.py`, `scripts/mp_liquid_visualizer_vulkan.py`, `scripts/liquid_player_visualizer.py`, `scripts/visualizer_player_bond.py`, `tests/test_visualizer_arrow_key_presets.py`):
  - The MP Liquid Player already owns `+`/`-` for the playing track's pitch, so the visualizer presets could not use them. The arrow keys are the equivalent, and they step through the **whole** library rather than just the nine numbered slots: `Right` on the last preset returns to the first, and `Left` on the first preset jumps to the last. Preset `11`, "Dreamworlds (Chromatic Storm)", is reachable this way.
  - Both standalone backends bind them: `GLFW_KEY_LEFT` (263) / `GLFW_KEY_RIGHT` (262) next to the existing `+`/`-` handlers in `mp_liquid_visualizer_vulkan.py` and `liquid_player_visualizer.py`, and the OpenGL path handles them in the same `_on_key` branch so neither backend lags behind the other.
  - In the MP Liquid Player the arrows **always keep their original ±10 s seek**, in every state — the preset keys live only inside the MP Visualizer window, so an arrow can never change the theme while a video plays. Both `\x1b[C`/`\x1b[D` and the application-cursor forms `\x1bOC`/`\x1bOD` are handled, and the player header and its `[0-9] theme` hint no longer advertise the arrows.
  - **A preset chosen in the visualizer window is no longer reverted by the player.** The bonded companion and the player share one preset through `liquid_player_visualizer.json`, but only the player wrote it, so the next bond poll (every 100 ms) switched the window straight back to the player's older `preset_id`. `PlayerBondMixin._publish_bonded_preset()` now writes the choice back from the window. Only a window that is already bonded publishes, so a free-standing `Z` window never marks the visualizer active; spawned F10/F11 windows and the screensaver still write nothing. The player reads that state back twice a second in `_follow_visualizer_preset()` so its header follows the preset that is actually on screen.

- **Path resolver library** (`scripts/lib/mp_install_dir.sh`, `scripts/lib/mp_install_dir.py`) with the same lookup order in shell and Python.
- **`mp-desktop-exec` helper** (`bin/mp-desktop-exec`) that launches a named script from the resolved tree, for desktop entries and shell aliases.
- **`tests/test_install_dir_resolution.py`** covering resolver lookup, moved and renamed trees, and the desktop entries.
- **Hidden Mode is wired into every host** (`scripts/mp_hidden_mode.py`, `MP_Audio_Player.py`, `MP_Audio_Player_Liquid_Glass.py`, `MP_Audio_Player_Liquid_Glass_OpenGL.py`, `scripts/mp_liquid_visualizer_vulkan.py`, `scripts/liquid_player_visualizer.py`, `Mix_Archive_Manager.sh`, `tests/test_hidden_mode.py`):
  - **`H`** hides and restores every MP window from the manager menu (`CHAR:h`/`CHAR:H`), both Liquid Glass hosts and both visualizer backends; all five call the same `toggle()`, so one state file and one restore path is shared.
  - In the terminal player `H` remains Favorites (the binding documented since 0.4.2) and Hidden Mode is bound to **`Ctrl+H`**, because plain `H` has to stay a text key in that window. Favorites gained **`Shift+F`** as an alias, and lowercase **`f`** still reveals the selected file.
- **The Whitest White is selectable** (`Mix_Archive_Manager.sh`, `MP_Audio_Player.py`, `scripts/liquid_glass_opengl.py`, `scripts/mp_theme_palette.py`, `scripts/view_tracklist_console.sh`, `tests/test_whitest_white_theme.py`):
  - The theme is registered in the manager palette table, the menu-number table, the background/foreground pair, the picker (**option 23**) and the **`C`** cycle right after Retro Linux Memories, and in the player's `THEMES`/`_THEME_PALETTES`. Its body ink is black, `MENU_WHITE` flips for it, the welcome banner follows the active theme, and the OpenGL glass renderer carries its text colour.
  - `scripts/mp_theme_palette.py` is now the single colour and alias table for the OpenGL, Vulkan and Metal renderers and the player; the alias maps had drifted, so `cosmic dreamworlds`, `sensorium interface`, `earth flipper` and `orbital void reflux` resolved on Vulkan but silently fell back on the default OpenGL backend.
- **The 21-format conversion menu is wired to the registry** (`Make_SOF_FLAC_CONVERSION.sh`, `Mix_Archive_Manager.sh`, `scripts/audio_conversion_suite.py`, `tests/test_conversion_menu_and_output_rule.py`):
  - The format list is generated from `scripts/audio_conversion_suite.py` instead of a hand-written five-entry picker, so all 21 advertised formats are reachable, plus a **Convert to EVERY Format** batch row, a **Show All Output Formats** table, a **Converted Output Folders** browser and a **Conversion Statistics & History** page.
  - Runs stage into a temp tree and publish into `*_CONVERTED_OUTPUTS` only after every requested format finishes, so a cancelled or failed batch leaves no half-made folder. The encoder is driven from the registry too (`command --argv` emits NUL-separated ffmpeg argv).
  - Metadata gains saved presets, custom fields, back cover, disc image and disc-number tags, plus a "skip the rest of the batch" escape on the per-file prompt; formats that cannot carry artwork warn instead of failing the encode.
- **Traktor playlists open in the player (`Shift+T`)** (`MP_Audio_Player.py`, `scripts/traktor_playlist_importer.py`, `tests/test_traktor_player_shortcut.py`):
  - `Shift+T` opens a generated-playlist picker over the playlist area. It lists only the `.json` + `.m3u8` pairs in `PLAYLISTS_GENERATED`, refuses with an explanation when no Music Folders are configured, and validates every path before it reaches the engine, reporting how many tracks were unavailable.
  - `_TraktorPlaylistScanner` presents the imported tracks through the same `get_mixes()`/`playlist_path` interface the player's own scanner uses, so no other code needed a special case. `AudioEngine.load_track` refuses a missing file before it moves the cursor, and `_ensure_playable_path` rejects zero-byte files as well as missing ones.
- **Two Dynamic Waves presets and a screen saver that tours them** (`shaders/mp_liquid_visualizer.frag`, `shaders/presets/p11.frag.spv`, `shaders/presets/p12.frag.spv`, `shaders/presets/manifest.json`, `scripts/mp_liquid_visualizer_vulkan.py`, `scripts/liquid_player_visualizer.py`, `scripts/pid_stream.py`, `scripts/mp_visualizer_idle_saver.py`, `tests/test_sof_dynamic_waves.py`):
  - **Presets 12 and 13** are the horizontal and vertical Stream of Frequency rivers, drawn as full-frame accent sweeps on a paper-white page rather than raymarched vessels. Shipped SPIR-V was rebuilt for all 13 presets and the manifest digest updated, because the old manifest was stale and every preset was falling back to a module built from a different shader revision.
  - `pid_stream` gained a demo mode and `stream_vector()`, which owns one event driver per window so two visualizer windows cannot share an event clock; the saver drives it from new Linux PIDs and the player drives it from beats.
  - The screen saver now tours the Dynamic Waves pair by default instead of the whole library and renders **80% of the capped area** (`SAVER_PIXEL_SCALE`); the console twin skips one column in five. Saver session ownership is guarded: a live newer wall takes over, a dead one is reclaimed, and teardown only clears the session it still owns.
- **MP Dev Utility builds arm64 and amd64 packages from one source tree** (`MP_Dev_Utility.sh` in `BASH_SCRIPTS`, `tests/test_package_dev_utility.py`):
  - New `--arch host|amd64|arm64|all` option and an interactive architecture prompt. Previously every format silently took its architecture from `uname -m`, so a packaging run on a PC could only ever produce PC packages.
  - `.deb`, `.rpm`, pacman `.pkg.tar.zst` and portable tarballs are now produced once per requested architecture, with the matching `Architecture:`/`arch =` metadata and file names (`mp-mix-manager_<version>_arm64.deb`, `..._aarch64.pkg.tar.zst`, `..._arm64_portable.tar.gz`).
  - No cross-compilation is involved: the payload is interpreted Bash and Python, and the only difference between the two packages is the recorded architecture. A native ELF scanner refuses to put an amd64 binary into an arm64 package unless `--allow-native-binaries` is passed.
  - Cross-architecture RPMs build inside an emulated container of the target architecture (needs binfmt/qemu); the Alpine `.apk` is `noarch` and covers every architecture from one file.
  - Formats that embed a native builder or base runtime are reported rather than faked: the AppImage and Snap are host-only (`--arch` lists them as skipped), and the Flatpak stays on the build host because `flatpak-builder` only builds for its own architecture.
  - Every run writes `BUILD-MANIFEST.txt` with the host, the requested architectures, the formats, and the exact artifacts produced; each package also carries `architecture.txt`/`ARCHITECTURE.txt`.

### Changed
- **Version bump to `0.5.3 Stable (Sensorium)`.** `VERSION` now reads `0.5.3 Stable (Sensorium)`. Every current-release label in the tree follows it: the installer and migration fallbacks, the manager header, the help and loader banners, the report, calendar and promo generators, the Liquid Player, the Web Companion and the MP Assistant, the macOS `CFBundleShortVersionString`/`CFBundleVersion` values (`0.5.3`), and the Windows launcher resource (`0,5,3,0`). Release notes in `README.md` and the badges on this page name 0.5.3.
- **Launchers and desktop shortcuts resolve the install tree instead of hard-coding a path** (`scripts/lib/mp_install_dir.sh`, `scripts/lib/mp_install_dir.py`, `bin/mp-desktop-exec`, `Mix_Archive_Manager.sh`, `scripts/launch_liquid_glass.sh`, `scripts/launch-liquid-glass`, `bin/mix-archive-manager`, `bin/launch-manager`, `bin/launch-manager-fullscreen`, `install.sh`, `manage_installation_config.sh`, `scripts/install_desktop_shortcuts.py`, `scripts/liquid_glass_reward.py`):
  - Nothing points at `/var/home/<user>/MP_Mix_Manager_v0.x` or any other version-numbered directory any more.
  - The install tree is found from `$MP_MIX_MANAGER_DIR`, then `~/.config/mix-manager/install_dir` (written by `install.sh` and by the migration menu), then the `~/.local/bin` launcher symlinks, then the newest matching tree under `$HOME` and `/opt`. Every candidate is verified with a marker file, so a stale symlink or a backup copy cannot win.
  - `Mix_Archive_Manager.sh` now follows its own symlink, so `manager` and `~/.local/bin/mix-archive-manager` report the tree they really point into.
  - Linux `.desktop` entries call `mp-desktop-exec` and use the icon name, so a moved or renamed tree keeps its shortcut and icon.
  - The Liquid Player launcher, MP Gui, the audio and WAN2GP output monitors, the alarm clock, and the KDE Connect helpers use the same resolver.

### Fixed
- **Every dashboard button was silently refused with `missing_csrf_header`** (`scripts/mix_web_companion.py`, `tests/test_web_companion_visualizer.py`):
  - The server requires the `X-MP-Companion` header on every state-changing request (the CSRF half of the security model), but the page never sent it. Transport, theme, library-mode, launch-pad and assistant-stop POSTs were all answered with **403 `Request refused: missing_csrf_header`**, and the page swallowed the error, so taps looked like they did nothing.
  - `fetch` is now wrapped once at the top of the dashboard script: every non-GET request gets the header, GET and HEAD are left exactly as they were. The header name comes from `mp_companion_security.REQUIRED_HEADER`, so the two cannot drift apart. Verified against the live handler: a bare POST is still refused, a wrapped one is accepted.
  - This is what made the new MP Visualizer button (and the existing suite buttons) work from a phone at all.

- **The engage gate no longer scrolls, so the Engage Audio Stream button cannot leave the page** (`scripts/mix_web_companion.py`, `tests/test_web_companion_visualizer.py`):
  - The gate was `justify-content: center` with `overflow-y: auto` on itself and on the card, so on a short screen the card grew past the viewport and the only way forward — the engage button — could be scrolled off the bottom. The gate and the card now never scroll (`overflow: hidden`, `overscroll-behavior: none`).
  - The card is a flex column centred in the gate, and the engage button is a fixed-height row, so it is always the last thing on screen and always centred from the top down. Short viewports shrink the heading and copy through two height media queries (560 px and 400 px) instead of hiding the button.

- **The last MP Visualizer preset holds 60 fps instead of about 5** (`shaders/mp_liquid_visualizer.frag`, `shaders/mp_liquid_visualizer.frag.spv`, `shaders/presets/p0`–`p10.frag.spv`, `shaders/presets/manifest.json`, `scripts/liquid_player_visualizer.py`, `tests/test_chromatic_storm_performance.py`):
  - **Preset 11, "Dreamworlds (Chromatic Storm)", evaluated all twelve of its SDF scenes at every march step.** The preset crossfades between twelve vessel sequences, but the crossfade only ever holds one sequence at weight `1.0` and fades the next in, so ten of the twelve weights are exactly `0.0` at all times. `csMapSequences` still called every scene unconditionally. Those ten calls cost the same as the two that can affect the picture, and each one is a full SDF evaluated up to 84 times per pixel. Measured on the reported machine: **27.7 fps before, 60.4 fps after** — the preset now meets the same vsync cap as the other ten. Uncapped, the same preset went from **28.7 fps to 140 fps**.
  - Each `seq[i]` is now assigned only when `w[i] > 0.0`. The blend array, the accumulation loop and the material scan are unchanged, so the distance field and blend order are identical; a skipped slot is multiplied by its exactly-zero weight and cannot contribute. Verified frame by frame across **all twelve held states and all eleven crossfades** (24 frames): every frame matches the old output to within **1/255** on all but a handful of antialiasing edge pixels.
  - The OpenGL port already guarded these calls with a regex applied to the GLSL at load time. The guard now lives in the shader itself, so both backends get it from one place and the regex is a no-op. OpenGL output is **pixel-identical** before and after.
  - **A stale `manifest.json` was silently serving the old shader, which is why editing the GLSL alone changed nothing.** `vulkan_preset_spirv` compares the shader's SHA-256 against the manifest's `source` digest and, on a mismatch with no `glslangValidator` installed, falls back to the shipped module *with no message*. The fix therefore had to regenerate the shipped SPIR-V too, not just the source. The fallback now prints a warning naming the module and both digests, so a manifest that no longer describes the shader can never again look like a working change.
  - All eleven preset modules and the unfolded module were rebuilt from the updated source with shaderc (Fedora ships shaderc, not glslang), and the manifest digest and step count were updated to match. Presets 0–9 render **pixel-identical** to their previous binaries.
  - `tests/test_chromatic_storm_performance.py` pins the guard, the zero-weight skip, the GL/Vulkan parity and the manifest digest, and asserts the stale-digest warning. Reverting a single guard line makes four of its tests fail.

- **The visualizer sits directly behind the player and stays visible over other windows** (`scripts/visualizer_player_bond.py`, `scripts/liquid_glass_slider.py`, `scripts/launch_liquid_glass.sh`, `tests/test_visualizer_player_bond.py`):
  - **`_NET_WM_STATE_BELOW` was the wrong mechanism, and it caused the reported bug.** The bond asked for it with `wmctrl -b add,below`. KWin reads that state as "below **every** normal window", not "below this one window", so the visualizer sank beneath the rest of the desktop. That is exactly what the two screenshots show: over an empty area the player blended with the visualizer, but over a maximised console the player blended with the *console*, because the visualizer was underneath the console.
  - The fix is to order the two windows **against each other**: `XConfigureWindow` with `CWSibling|CWStackMode` and `StackModeBelow`, using the player's own window as the sibling. The visualizer is now the player's immediate lower neighbour, so whatever is below the pair no longer matters. The stale hint is removed on bond, and the order is re-asserted every 0.5 s because the player is raised whenever it gains focus. Verified live: visualizer index 2, player index 3 — immediately adjacent — with `_NET_WM_STATE` free of `BELOW`.
  - `XLowerWindow` (my first attempt) had the same defect as the hint: it sends the window to the very bottom of the stack. It survives only as a fallback for when the player window cannot be identified.
  - **The transparency bar looked black.** The GTK overlay had no RGBA visual and no paintable window, so it painted an opaque theme background. It now asks for `set_app_paintable` plus the screen's RGBA visual, and clears the `window`/`drawingarea` CSS background, so only the bar is drawn. The bar itself is a frosted glass trough — the theme hue lightened toward white at low alpha, with a translucent accent fill and a bright glass knob. It now reads within **1–3 RGB units** of the surrounding pane instead of a black strip.
  - **The companion could be skipped at launch.** The launcher's `pgrep -f "MP_Liquid_Visualizer|mp_liquid_visualizer_vulkan"` matched any shell whose command line merely *mentioned* those names, including the launcher's own wrapper. It then believed a visualizer was already running and started none. The check now matches only an interpreter actually running the script.

- **The MP Liquid Player keeps its themed glass, an animated visualizer behind it, and the transparency bar on the Vulkan backend** (`scripts/mp_liquid_visualizer_vulkan.py`, `scripts/liquid_glass_vulkan.py`, `scripts/liquid_glass_slider.py`, `MP_Audio_Player_Liquid_Glass.py`, `CHANGELOG.md`):
  - **Three separate faults, all on the Vulkan path** (the backend this desktop is configured for), which is why the OpenGL path looked fine:
    - **The window was fully transparent.** `LiquidGlassRenderer.draw()` painted the visualizer *inside* the player window and returned, skipping the themed glass shader entirely. The bonded companion window already supplies that picture, so the player became a see-through frame over the raw scene with no theme colour on it. On Linux the host now always draws its own glass pane, exactly as the 0.4 release did — `v0.4`'s `draw()` has no in-window visualizer branch at all. Platforms without a companion window keep the in-window path.
    - **The visualizer never moved.** The standalone Vulkan renderer never called `march_advance()`, so `accum_time` stayed at `0.000` whenever no audio was playing and the scene was a still image. The OpenGL in-player layer already had this call; the standalone Vulkan renderer now advances the clock too, using a real frame delta. Measured: `accum_time 0.000 → 0.000` before, `0.009 → 0.931` after, with 70% of pixels changing over two seconds.
    - **The transparency bar was missing.** Only the OpenGL host ever drew one, inside its GL frame. The Vulkan host had no slider code at all — `grep slider MP_Audio_Player_Liquid_Glass.py` returned nothing, and the same is true of the 0.4 release, so the bar disappeared the moment Vulkan became the default. Added `scripts/liquid_glass_slider.py`, a borderless GTK overlay pinned to the 44 px strip the terminal well reserves at the bottom.
